Caller marshalling at every call site: `sub rsp, 8*nfields ; <store each field at [rsp + 8*i]> ; mov rdi, rsp ; call ; add rsp, 8*nfields`. Callee prologue copies struct slots into its own rbp slots so the body's `Field(Ident(input), name)` access reads `[rbp - 8*(i+1)]` unchanged — same shape as the single-field 5.1a path, just preceded by an N-slot copy. Self-call site supports both pass-through `Ident(input)` (copy current rbp slots to struct slots) and `Record(input_concept, { name: expr, ... })` (evaluate each expression in source order, store at the struct offset implied by the FIELD NAME — source-order independent of declaration-order). Single-field rules (slice 5.1a/5.1b) keep the rdi-as-value fast path; the multi-field path activates only when `concept.fields.len() > 1`. **No pointers in the language**: the rdi pointer is an ABI artifact between caller and callee, lifetime bounded by the call/ret pair (`sub rsp` allocates, `add rsp` frees). No syntax for `&x`, `*p`, casts, or arithmetic on it. Numbers-only payload in this slice; multi-field text payload is a later slice. | ~950 B | `gcd.verbose::gcd` (954 B; Euclidean GCD on Pair{a, b}) |
| A (slice 5.4) | **Mutual recursion via shared SelfCallCtx labels map.** First Verbose binary where two distinct rules emit as separate callables and `call` each other. `SelfCallCtx` evolved from `{rule_name, target_offset}` (single rule) to `{labels: HashMap<rule_name, target_offset>}` (N rules); the emit_eval_expr Call arm looks up the callee in `ctx.labels` to decide real-call vs inline. `compile_native_code` detects the cycle, computes the SCC containing the entry rule via forward/backward-reachable intersection (`collect_scc_containing`), and passes the ordered SCC to `emit_self_recursive_program`. **Two-pass label resolution**: pass 1 measures each callable's size with placeholder labels (all zero); pass 2 emits with real offsets computed from those sizes. Required because cross-rule `call`s in earlier callables forward-reference later callables. Pass-1/pass-2 instruction sizes match exactly (`call rel32` is always 5 bytes regardless of value); only the rel32 values differ. New helper `emit_callable_into` encapsulates per-rule prologue+body+epilogue so the orchestrator loops over SCC members. Slice constraints: all SCC rules share the same input concept and output type (cross-concept mutual is Phase B-shaped work). Text input fields lifted in PR #52; let bindings lifted in PR #54; transitive callees with different types lifted in PR #56. Worked example: `even_odd.verbose` (`even_ish` ↔ `odd_ish`, single-Number-field, 879 B). | ~880 B | `even_odd.verbose::even_ish` (879 B; mutually-recursive parity) |
| B (slice 1) | **`concept_group [max_depth: N, max_nodes: M]` declaration surface.** New `Item::ConceptGroup` AST node containing N mutually-recursive sum-type concepts in a single lexical scope. Parser recognizes the block syntax; nested `concept` declarations reuse the existing path. Verifier validates the group bounds (0 < N, 0 < M ≤ 65535 — 16-bit index ceiling preserved for B.4+'s emitter), variant payload types (Number/Text/Bool/Named concept either top-level or same-group), and rejects: `collection(group_concept)` (deferred to B.1b), cross-group references, record-style fields inside a group (sum-type only). Group concepts share the program-wide namespace with collision detection. **No interpreter, no native, no codegen** — pure declaration surface. Subagent-delegated PR #40. | +0 B (declaration-only) | n/a (no runtime path until B.3) |
| B (slice 3) | **Interpreter handles recursive Variant values.** Pre-B.3 the `Expr::Call` arm refused with "call argument must be a record" when its arg evaluated to a `Value::Variant`; B.3 lifts this. New `eval_rule_with_value(rule, all_rules, concepts, input_value)` entry accepts any `Value` (Record OR Variant OR other). Existing `eval_rule(... HashMap)` wraps as `Value::Record` and delegates — purely additive, every existing call site stays unchanged. The verifier-level refusal from B.1 ("rule using concept_group type") MOVES to `compile_native_code`: `--run` now works on rules whose input/output is a group concept; `--native` keeps refusing with a B.4+ breadcrumb. Main.rs and the verifier integration test extended to use `iter_all_concepts` (covers group-nested concepts) so the interpreter resolves positional binders correctly. Worked example: `sum_chain.verbose` (Expr = Int \| Add, `build_chain` produces a chain, `eval` walks it recursively, `sum_seed` composes both). First Verbose program with mutually-recursive types evaluated at runtime via `--run`. | +0 B (interpreter only) | `sum_chain.verbose::sum_seed` (n ∈ {0, 3, 5, 10} → 0, 6, 15, 55) |
| B (slice 4a.1) | **Concept group prologue infrastructure — no new emit semantics.** First slice that lifts `compile_native_code`'s blanket refusal on programs declaring a `concept_group`. The blanket refusal at `compile_native_code:127-142` is replaced by two slice-constraint checks (one group per program max — multi-group is B.4a-followup; one concept per group max — multi-concept is B.4b) plus an early walk over every rule's logic that refuses VariantConstruct and MatchVariant on group concepts with breadcrumbs naming B.4a.2 and B.4a.3 respectively. The walks use group-aware concept resolution via `iter_all_concepts` (B.1's helper) so refusal messages name the right concept even when it lives inside a group. `emit_record_loop_prologue` grows two purely additive frame reservations when (and ONLY when) a group is present: 8 bytes for `node_count_slot` (init'd to 0) and `max_nodes * entry_size` bytes for the arena (entry_size = `(1 + 8 * max_payload_fields)` rounded up to 8 — Int(value) ⇒ 9 → 16; Add(lhs, rhs) ⇒ 17 → 24; whole-concept max is the per-entry size). Two new instructions emit at the prologue: `mov qword [rbp + nc_slot], 0` and `lea r11, [rbp + arena_rbp_offset]`. The per-group abort tail at the end of the binary (~17 B `mov rax,60 ; mov rdi,1 ; syscall`) is emitted unconditionally when the group is present so 4a.2's bounds-check sites have a stable patch target. New ctx fields (`arena_size`, `arena_rbp_offset`, `node_count_slot`, `entry_size`, `arena_abort_patches`) are unconsumed in 4a.1 — they sit empty/zero until 4a.2. **Byte-for-byte invariant**: programs WITHOUT a concept_group compile to bit-identical binaries (every existing Phase A/B example pinned: factorial 802 B, gcd 954 B, even_odd 879 B unchanged). The 6 prologue call sites of `emit_record_loop_prologue` were threaded with a new `concept_group: Option<&ConceptGroup>` parameter; 5 of them pass `None` because they're driven by emitter paths a group rule never reaches in 4a.1 (recursive emit refuses group concepts anyway via slice 5.x's "Number-only input fields" constraint). | +0 B (no group declared); +~30-50 B (group declared, body bytes only) | n/a (sum_chain.verbose still refuses natively until 4a.2 + 4a.3 land) |

| B (slice 4c) | **Text payloads in variant arena entries.** `Type::Text` fields in concept_group variants now compile natively. Arena entry layout changes from uniform 8-byte slots to type-aware widths: Number/Named(self-ref) fields stay 8 B, Text fields get 16 B (ptr, len). `compute_arena_entry_size` uses `arena_field_byte_width` per field type; `arena_field_offset` computes cumulative offsets per variant. VariantConstruct pre-eval (step 1) dispatches text fields through inline (ptr, len) production — text literals via jmp-over-data, BoundText via slot load, input text fields via ptr + strlen — then stores both to 16 B tmp slots. Step 7 writes both i64s to the arena entry at type-aware offsets. MatchVariant binder extraction reads (ptr, len) from the arena entry and registers text binders in `extended_text_bindings` (not `extended_offsets`), making them available to `emit_length`, `emit_text_write_to_fd`, and any BoundText consumer in the arm body. Frame sizing updated: `count_max_match_arm_binder_slots` returns slots (text binder = 2) instead of count; tmp pool sized at max variant payload bytes. **Arena-safety constraint**: text field expressions restricted to allocation-free shapes (literal, input field, BoundText from read/let, Ident in text_bindings). Concat and Call refused — their stack buffers are freed before the arena entry is consumed. **Bugfix (secondary)**: Call inline path in `emit_eval_expr` was dropping `arena_ctx` (passing `None`); now threads it through so VariantConstruct inside an inlined callee sees the arena. Programs without text payloads compile byte-for-byte identically (arena_field_byte_width returns 8 for all existing types; slot math unchanged). | same as 4a.1-4a.3 (no worked .verbose example yet) | n/a (tested via 3 integration tests: compile, runtime number extraction, runtime length of text binder) |

| C (slice 1) | **Structural recursion proof for concept_group types.** New `structural : <param>` in the termination block. Verifier walks every recursive Call site and checks that the argument is a binder from a MatchVariant arm corresponding to a self-referential variant field. When the proof verifies, the mandatory stderr breadcrumb is suppressed. Worked example: `sum_chain.verbose::eval` with `structural : e`. | +0 B | `sum_chain.verbose::eval`, `label_tree.verbose::total_label_length` |
| C (slice 2) | **Decreasing numeric field proof.** New `decreasing : <field>` in the termination block. Verifier checks every recursive call passes `input.field - k` (k > 0) for a bounded Number field. Combined with runtime bounds-check, proves termination mechanically. Breadcrumb suppressed. | +0 B | `factorial.verbose::fact`, `sum_chain.verbose::build_chain`, `label_tree.verbose::build_tree` |
| C (slice 3) | **Increasing numeric field proof.** New `increasing : <field>` — symmetric of `decreasing`. Checks `field + k` (k > 0). Covers the scanner pattern where position advances. | +0 B | `scan_word.verbose::word_length` |
| text input | **Text input fields in recursive callables** (PR #52). Lifts the Number-only constraint. Text fields flow through the multi-field pointer-in-rdi ABI as `(ptr, len)` pairs: 16 B per text field in the struct, 2 rbp slots in the callee's frame, registered in `callable_text_bindings`. `SelfCallCtx` grows `struct_layouts` for type-aware marshalling. | ~920 B | `scan_word.verbose::word_length` (921 B) |
| let in callable | **Let bindings in recursive callables** (PR #54). Number lets fully supported; text lets allowed for allocation-free shapes. Frame layout extended with let slots between input fields and binder slots. Caveat: lets are evaluated eagerly (before the body), so they must not depend on the recursion guard. | ~1.3–1.7 KB | `first_word.verbose::first_word_len` (1293 B), `count_tokens.verbose::count_tokens` (1691 B) |
| callable routing | **Transitive callable extension unconditional** (PR #56). Non-recursive callees with let bindings are now emitted as callables instead of inlined (the inline path ignores callee let bindings). Entry rule's let bindings stripped from _start prologue (the callable handles its own). Constraint walk's same-input/output check scoped to the entry's SCC, not transitive callees. | n/a | `token_scan.verbose::classify` (2260 B, 4 callables) |
| streaming emit | **Streaming lowering for recursive text-returning rules** (2026-06-10) — the FIRST EMITTER slice of the self-hosting arc. When the entry rule's output is text, the rule is recursive, and a body uses text shapes beyond slice 5.2's literal-leaf grammar (concat of fresh text, MatchVariant), native lowers the whole SCC to WRITER mode: each callable writes its bytes to fd 1 in order during the walk (literal → guarded write; text field/BoundText/substring → slot load + write; number expr → itoa, no newline; concat → per-arg in order, no buffer, no sizing pass; if/match → branch + stream arms; tail call to a text SCC member → `call`, callee streams). `_start` writes only the trailing newline. EVERY streamed write is wrapped `push r11 … pop r11` (20 B, size-stable) because syscalls clobber r11 = the arena base that in-callable code TRUSTS (the node arena's unspoken no-syscall precondition — first consumer to break it). Mode is a whole-SCC ABI property decided once in compile_native_code; bodies passing the old literal-leaf grammar keep the materializing path, so all existing examples compile BYTE-IDENTICALLY (10-binary SHA-256 gate at b214fb1). v1 restrictions: text-SCC calls in tail text positions only (refused in conds/scrutinees/value positions with a streaming breadcrumb); lets keep callable rules + no text-SCC references. Verifier untouched. Documented divergence: on mid-tree abort the binary has already written a stdout prefix (interpreter: nothing); exit codes agree. Design arc: docs/emitter-streaming-design.md. | ~1.1 KB | `print_chain.verbose::show` (1088 B; seed 3 → `3+2+1+0`, == --run) |
| agg-1 | **A rule RETURNS a record to another rule** (2026-08-19) — the first aggregate that crosses a `call`/`ret` boundary. Design: [docs/bytes-value-return-design.md](docs/bytes-value-return-design.md), implemented as its §6. **ABI**: `rdi` = the input value or fields-struct pointer (UNCHANGED); **`rsi` = a pointer to a destination the CALLER allocates in its own frame**; `rax` on return = that same pointer. Width and field order are the concept's declaration (`concept.fields`, a `Vec`) — never inferred, never a `HashMap` walk. The caller's `let p = mk(x)` reserves N consecutive rbp slots (one per Number field), and `p.<field>` afterwards is an ordinary `mov rax, [rbp - k]`: the same instruction as reading an input field, because the destination slot group has the same layout as the input slot group. **`rsi` deliberately diverges from System V** (which puts sret in `rdi` and shifts the arguments) so the shipped input ABI does not move and every existing recursive binary stays byte-identical. **Three details are load-bearing and each would ship a WRONG BINARY rather than a refusal.** (1) `lea rsi, [rbp - dest]` is emitted **LAST, immediately before the `call`** — the input-struct marshalling clobbers `rsi` (`mov rsi, rax` + `emit_strlen` per text field; `lea rsi, [rsp+22]` inside `emit_itoa_inline`), and it is safe there only because `rbp` is stable across marshalling. (2) Field values land in **`rax`**, not `rdx` — `emit_eval_expr` leaves its result in rax universally — so the callee reloads the destination into `rcx` per field (`mov rcx, [rbp-sret] ; mov [rcx+off], rax`) instead of parking it: `rbx`/`r15` belong to the A2/A4 fast path and `r11` is the arena base. (3) **`qualifies_rbx` gains `&& !is_record_output`**: `scalar_output = !is_text && !is_bytes` is TRUE for `Type::Named(C)` and `body_is_pure_scalar_arith` has an `Expr::Record` arm, so a single-Number-field callable with a `Record { … }` body of arithmetic otherwise satisfies every conjunct, takes the A2 prologue (which emits **no `sub rsp`**), and the sret slot at `[rbp-8]` lands on the pushed `rbx`. The flagship fixture does NOT hit this (its 2-field input fails `single_scalar_field`), so the control uses the `N { v }` shape. **The sret slot goes strictly BELOW the tmp pool**: `tmp_slot_base_local` is computed WITHOUT `tmp_slots` while `total_slots` includes it, so a slot inserted above that anchor silently re-points every VariantConstruct spill (PR #175 was exactly that bug in this frame). **Field lookup is keyed `__agg_<let>_<field>`**, not by bare name: native field lookup is bare-name-keyed (`__state_<field>` the one precedent), so a returned concept `Q` sharing a field name with the caller's input concept `P` would make `p.a` silently resolve to the INPUT's slot — a plausible number at rc 0, the silent-wrong-answer class this repo spent eight gen0 slices closing. **Additive by measurement, not by argument**: sweeping all 152 examples × every rule (1372 rule-binaries) with a compiler built from `f134efb` and one from this branch, **1370 identical by size + sha256, 0 size-changed, 0 bytes-only-changed**, exactly ONE status change (`aggregate_pair::total` refused → 793 B), and the baseline-vs-baseline control came back empty first. Ten refusals (§6.2) each name the offender AND the slice that lifts it; each carries a corrected twin in the tests. Recursion is refused (a per-frame destination is agg-2), which is why **slice agg-1 delivers no X25519 speedup at all** — both X25519 rules are self-recursive. Its prize is ASSURANCE: it moves a stage of `tools/tls_gen/vcrypto.py`'s composition driver inside the verifier's reach. WASM refuses with a named breadcrumb; the interpreter was already correct and is untouched. | ~790 B (2-field) / ~630 B (1-field) | `aggregate_pair.verbose::total` (793 B; argv `7 9` → `9007`, == `--run`) |
| agg-2a | **A RECURSIVE rule returns an aggregate** (2026-08-21) — lifts agg-1's refusal #1, BOTH halves (a record-returning rule in a cycle, and one on the callable path of a recursive ENTRY). Design: [docs/bytes-value-return-design.md](docs/bytes-value-return-design.md) §7 row 1, **whose stated mechanism is wrong and is corrected in that note's header**. §7 priced it as *"needs a per-frame destination for non-tail calls (`sub rsp, N*8 ; lea rsi, [rsp]`)"*; **nothing per-frame is needed.** When the recursive aggregate call sits in a record **TAIL position** — the whole value of the body, or of an `if` arm, recursively — the callee's result IS this rule's result, so the callee is handed the pointer THIS frame was handed rather than a fresh one. The whole chain therefore has exactly **ONE destination**, the one the outermost non-recursive caller allocated, and **only the base case ever writes it**: a tail arm is structurally either a `Record` (terminal) or a `Call` (forward), so an intermediate frame cannot write. Cost **4 bytes per recursive call site**, zero frame growth, nothing to free. **`SretDest` is a TYPE and not a boolean, and that is the whole safety story**: `Frame(off)` emits `lea rsi, [rbp+off]` (the ADDRESS of a slot group this frame owns — agg-1's caller) while `Forward(off)` emits `mov rsi, [rbp+off]` (the VALUE of the pointer this frame was handed — agg-2a's tail). Both are emitted LAST, immediately before the `call`, for agg-1's reason. **Where the destination lives, and what frees it**: the caller's own rbp frame, as a `let` slot group, allocated by the frame's single `sub rsp, frame_bytes` and freed by `mov rsp, rbp ; pop rbp`. It **cannot** share the input fields-struct's `sub rsp, N*8`, because that region is freed by the matching `add rsp` the instant the `call` returns while the destination must outlive it — the caller reads `p.<field>` afterwards. **Mutual recursion falls out** (the forward is a per-CALLABLE property, not a per-SCC one) and is pinned with per-leg base cases so the answer names WHICH callable wrote the shared destination. **The two-pass label resolution is untouched**: the forward's size depends only on the sret slot's rbp offset, which is AST-derived and identical in both passes, and `call rel32` is 5 bytes regardless — proven end-to-end by the mutual-recursion binary, whose two callables forward-reference each other. **Three narrower refusals replace #1**, each naming the offender and the lifting slice, each with a corrected twin: a tail callee returning a DIFFERENT concept (**agg-2b**); a record-returning rule that BINDS another aggregate to a `let` (**agg-2b** — #8, narrowed from "calls" to "binds"); and a record-output ENTRY on the callable path, where `emit_self_recursive_program`'s result dispatch would itoa the destination pointer (**agg-2c**). **The non-tail shape is not merely refused, it is INEXPRESSIBLE in a terminating way**: `mk(n).x` does not parse, so the only syntax is a `let`, and a callable's lets are EAGER — measured with the gate removed, the binary SIGSEGVs on *every* input including the base case, and `--run` on the same source overflows the interpreter's stack. agg-2b's real blocker is lazy `let` evaluation, not a destination convention. **Additive by measurement**: sweeping all 153 examples × every rule/reaction/service (1374 rule-binaries) with a compiler built from `036d12b` and one from this branch, **1373 identical by size + sha256, 0 size-changed, 0 bytes-only-changed**, exactly ONE status change (`aggregate_recurse::fib` refused → 1029 B), with the baseline-vs-baseline control empty first. Negative controls: dropping the forward is INVISIBLE on the flagship (nothing there clobbers `rsi`, so all five depths still pass — the NC-2 trap again) and gives **`0` at rc 0** from depth 1 on a fixture that does clobber it; `lea` instead of `mov` at the forward site gives the right answer at depth 0 and `0` at rc 0 thereafter. **X25519 IS now computable in one process — and it is SLOWER, see the "still rejects" bullet.** | +4 B per recursive call site | `aggregate_recurse.verbose::fib` (1029 B; argv `10 0 1` → `55089`, == `--run`) |
| text-state-1 | **A service `state:` field can hold BYTES across requests** (2026-08-30). Design: [docs/text-state-fields-design.md](docs/text-state-fields-design.md), implemented as its §6. A `state:` line may be `<name> : text [..N] = "<literal>"`. **Layout**: the state block keeps its position (between the handler let slots and the resource block) and grows per field — a Number field contributes the same 8 bytes in the same order, a Text field `16 + ((N + 7) & !7)`: the byte-for-byte shape a `resource` and a `connection` already have in this frame. Offsets come from a **descending cursor over `service.state_fields`, a `Vec`** — a HashMap walk here would reach emitted bytes. **Persistence needed no new mechanism**: the comment that already justified an 8-byte counter surviving `lea rsp, [rbp - frame_size]` makes no reference to width, so an N-byte buffer inherits it verbatim; that is the single largest reason this is a slice. **Three emit sites**: init (once, above the socket — `lea` the buffer address into the ptr slot, store the literal length, `rep movsb` the literal in via jmp-over-data, so the whole state block stays visible to `strings <binary>`); the `set` COPY in the after loop (`emit_text_produce_ptrlen` → 13-byte backstop → `rep movsb` into the buffer, `len_slot` updated, **`ptr_slot` deliberately never rewritten** so every BoundText reader works unmodified); and read registration into `http_text_bindings` under `__state_<f>`. **The composite key is not stylistic**: `req.body` is registered under the BARE name `"body"`, and three BoundText key extractions matched `Expr::Field(_, n)` discarding the base — measured on a bare-name build, a service with a state field named `body` answers `s=seed r=seed`, i.e. `req.body` silently resolves to the state field's slots. Those three sites are now base-aware. **The overflow gate is a compile-time PROOF, not a runtime check** (see the Language Features entry); the 13-byte backstop is reached only by a patched compiler, and NC-6 shows it is what separates a parse-guard regression from a remote SIGSEGV. **Additive by measurement**: 1375/1375 pre-existing rule-binaries byte-identical by size + sha256, control empty first. One byte-neutral fix landed with it: an `after:` block now forces the dynamic service path, because the constant fast path emits no state init and no after block and would silently drop the mutation — the same hazard `analyze_http10_handler_shape` already records for handler `let`s. | ~1.0 KB (one text field) / +0 B for every pre-existing binary | `last_path_service.verbose::memo` (1027 B; `/alpha` → `prev:none`, `/beta` → `prev:/alpha`, `/gamma` → `prev:/beta`) |

Design: [docs/bytes-value-return-design.md](docs/bytes-value-return-design.md) §7 "A record-output CALLER" row, **whose between-slices re-framing was wrong in the direction that made the slice look big**: the brief re-read the refusal as "no path can simultaneously RECEIVE a destination and PROVIDE one", and checked against the code that is false — both halves have shipped inside `emit_callable_into` since agg-1 (incoming `rsi` spilled to the sret slot; each record `let` its own slot group via `SretDest::Frame`), coexisting behind the refusals as never-exercised code that worked the first time it was reached. What was actually missing is what the design note always said: **`_start` had no record result arm.** Built as ~60 lines in `emit_self_recursive_program`: the destination is allocated below rsp per record-loop iteration (`sub rsp, N*8`), the entry callable is handed it in `rsi` emitted **LAST, after the input marshalling** — agg-1's hazard #1 verbatim, reproduced in `_start`: measured with the `lea` early, a text-input-field driver prints `{"x":0,"y":140720364978897}` at rc 0, the y a live 0x7ffc… stack address (a silent wrong answer AND an ASLR disclosure), while the all-number flagship PASSES on the broken build — the vacuous-on-the-flagship trap for the third slice running, so the pinned control uses the text-field fixture — and the returned slots are serialised from `[rsp + off]` between rsp-balanced helpers as byte-for-byte `emit_record_as_json`'s `{"field":value,...}` format (one shape, one rendering, whichever emitter serves it). **Three refusals narrow**: #7 to Text/Bytes callers (a record caller is now served; and agg-2a's record-output-ENTRY refusal is deleted, which **closes the one cell where gen0 was MORE capable than verbosec** — `fib_pair` as entry now compiles to 1157 B printing the same bytes gen0's 3750 B binary already printed, so the gaps-table disagreement is agreement. **Additive by measurement**: sweeping all 153 pre-existing examples × every rule/reaction/service (1374 rule-binaries) with a compiler built from `6f94e5c` and one from this branch, **1373 identical by size + sha256, 0 size-changed, 0 bytes-only-changed**, exactly ONE status change (`aggregate_recurse::fib_pair` refused → 1157 B), baseline-vs-baseline control empty first. gen0 at the new example: rule #0 (`step`) ACCEPTS at 3859 B printing the same bytes verbosec prints (the first agg rule-#0 credit matched by the reference); | entropy-1 | **The declared randomness effect** (2026-09-02) — `entropy <name> { bytes: N }` + `random(<name>)`, the NEW ROW in the closed effect catalogue (design: [docs/randomness-effect-design.md](docs/randomness-effect-design.md), implemented as its §6; catalogue row in [docs/effect-model.md](docs/effect-model.md)). **The one draw site** (`emit_entropy_draw`, mirrored on `emit_resource_read_sequence` with the `open` / `close` removed): `push r11 ; mov rax, 318 ; lea rdi, [rbp+buf] ; mov rsi, N ; xor edx, edx ; syscall ; pop r11 ; cmp rax, N ; jne abort ; mov qword [rbp+len], N ; lea rax, [rbp+buf] ; mov [rbp+ptr], rax` — objdump on the flagship at `0x1c6`: exactly that, 50 bytes with disp8. **Frame**: the resource / connection triple verbatim, `16 + ((N + 7) & !7)` bytes per referenced name, allocated on the same descending cursor BELOW connections and ABOVE the `now_unix` slot; reservation happens at prologue, the syscall does NOT — it is emitted BELOW `loop_top`, after the field loads and their bound checks and before the `let` loop, so the unit is per RECORD (and per LINE under `--stream`), not per invocation like `now_unix`. In `emit_http10_dynamic_bytes` the draw goes immediately after the connection block — after the fork dispatch and the HTTP parse — so every instruction of it runs in the child under `forked`, and a request the parser drops never spends a draw. The `(ptr, len)` registers under `__entropy_<name>` (a composite key, and the design's NC-6 proves it is not stylistic: keyed bare, an item named `body` makes `req.body` echo raw draw bytes — measured `"\n���nF�J:10"` for a POST body `seed`). Consumers resolve through the existing BoundText lookups: `emit_starts_with_load_text` (so `byte_at`) and `emit_length` gain a `Random` arm, `emit_streaming_bytes_body` streams a whole-body draw to fd 1, and `classify_concat_arg` admits `byte_at` as a Number arg. **Dispatch-level refusals** in `compile_native_code` keep a draw out of every emitter with no draw site (rows 9–11 of the design, each naming the emitter and the lifting slice), and `compile_service` refuses a `raw_tcp` handler by name. **Negative controls, all run** (§6.5): NC-1 hoist above `accept_top` → sequential 64 requests "share a draw", forked 1 distinct of 8, T6 fails on position; NC-2 hoist above `loop_top` → "records in one invocation must not share a draw" while two invocations still differ; NC-3 syscall deleted → **caught by the BACKSTOP first** (rax = 318 ≠ N → rc 1), and only with the backstop ALSO deleted does the screen see it ("chunk 0 is all zero") — a correction to the design, which expected zeros from the syscall alone; NC-4 `mov edx, 1` → only T6 fails, T1/T2 pass on a seeded host, the reason the byte-scan test exists; NC-5 syscall 335 without the backstop → a token printed at rc 0 (T2 catches), with it → rc 1 and zero bytes; NC-6 as above; **NC-7 does NOT fail** — every entry-level arena access reloads r11 since the 2026-06-22 mmap correction, and entry level is the only level a draw can be emitted at in slice 1, so the `push r11 / pop r11` is insurance for entropy-4, documented as such at the draw site. **A PRE-EXISTING `--stream` defect surfaced and fixed**: the wrapper searched BACKWARD for the last `mov rax, 60` and truncated there — for any binary with an abort tail (a bounded field, a resource, now a draw) that is the tail's `exit(1)`, so the record loop's own `exit(0)` survived, the process answered ONE line and exited 0, and every abort patch pointed into the loop-back. Measured on the baseline compiler: `examples/alert.verbose --stream` fed three lines printed one — CLAUDE.md's own `true\nfalse` example had been false since the 2026-08-05 text bound check gave `alert` its abort tail. Now the epilogue's `mov rax, 60 ; syscall` (the only such adjacency; abort tails are `mov rax, 60 ; mov rdi, 1 ; syscall`) is overwritten IN PLACE with the 9-byte `mov rsp, rbp ; pop rbp ; jmp stream_top`, so abort tails and their patches survive; abort-tail-free stream binaries are byte-identical (the truncated 9 bytes were exactly what the overwrite writes). Pinned by `stream_mode_processes_every_line_when_an_abort_tail_is_present`. **Additive by measurement**: sweeping all 158 examples × every rule / reaction / service (1386 targets; 1383 pre-existing: 824 emitted / 559 refused), compiler from `5cf3d79` vs branch, baseline-vs-baseline control EMPTY first — **0 status changes, 0 size changes, 0 same-size-different-bytes on every pre-existing target**; the only rows that move are the two new files' (baseline cannot parse `entropy`). The sweep compiles without `--stream`, so the stream fix's cost is stated separately: every `--stream` binary that has an abort tail changes (its tails are now kept), every one without is byte-identical. **gen0** refuses both new examples at rule #0 and at the declared entry (rc 1, zero bytes) — no `random` in `span_is_primitive`, no `entropy` keyword — the safe direction; EXPECTED_ACCEPTED stays 97 of a corpus now 158, R0/R1/R2 hold, and gen0's own binary is byte-identical (sha256 `ea4a16c1…`, 657831 B). Clean negative: no existing test consumed a `random` / `entropy` surface, and none of the 561 grew into the hole. | 469 B (32-byte CLI draw) / 3659 B (16-byte HTTP token service, 16 `byte_at` sites) / +0 B for every pre-existing binary | `nonce_cli.verbose::nonce` (argv `1 2 3` → 96 bytes, 3 distinct) / `nonce_service.verbose::token_server` (64 + 64 forked requests, pairwise-distinct tokens) |
| rawtcp-inspect-0 | **A `raw_tcp` handler can INSPECT its input** (2026-09-03) — design: [docs/multistep-connection-design.md](docs/multistep-connection-design.md) §4, implemented as its six items. Until this slice a raw_tcp handler was pinned to the identity shape by an emitter gate (`compile_service`) and its input was invisible to the byte-addressed primitives by a verifier gate (`check_byte_addressable_operand` admitted only a `b"…"` literal and a `random()` draw) — so every computational primitive in the language was inert inside it (design §1.2's fourth argument). **Three changes, one per gate plus the emitter.** (1) The verifier's byte-addressed gate grows a THIRD admitted shape: a `Field` whose base is the rule's input and whose DECLARED type is `Type::Bytes` — and ONLY that shape, never `Type::Bytes` at large, because a bytes `concat` is streamed with no sizing pass and its length is nowhere (design W2). The gate's criterion is restated rather than weakened: *a length the emitter knows, either as a constant or in a slot it owns* — for `req.<field>` it is the `read` syscall's return value, the same shape as `read(<resource>)`'s `len_slot`. Outside a service the rule-binary path still refuses a bytes field (`only number/text today` — measured twice: a bytes-input rule with `output: number` is refused at the input prologue, and `tag_probe::probe` as a RULE target at its record-output field, by both compilers in the sweep), so the admission cannot reach a rule binary. (2) `emit_raw_tcp_dynamic_bytes`: the HTTP frame discipline MINUS the parse and serialize — `push rbp ; mov rbp, rsp ; sub rsp, frame_size`; socket / `SO_REUSEADDR` / bind / listen; `accept_top`; client_fd at **`[rbp-48]`** (the slot, not `r13`: design §3 W3's register audit found `r13`/`r14` handler-reachable scratch once the handler computes); ONE `read`; `test rax, rax ; jle close` (EOF or error runs no handler — design §4.3 row 4: the `jle`, not the bounds check, is what keeps `byte_at` off an empty buffer); the read count stored at `[rbp-16]` and the buffer address at `[rbp-8]`; handler lets (Phase 2I-H's prologue verbatim, on the REAL input concept); the response streamed by `emit_streaming_bytes_body` **with an fd parameter threaded through** — `WriteFd::Imm(1)` emits the exact 7-byte `mov rdi, 1` every pre-existing caller emitted, `WriteFd::RbpSlot(-48)` a 4-byte `mov rdi, [rbp-48]` — then `close` and `lea rsp, [rbp - frame_size] ; jmp accept_top`. One read, one write, then close: exactly today's contract, only the handler changed. The identity handler keeps `emit_raw_tcp_echo_bytes` UNTOUCHED — `raw_tcp_echo.verbose` is 358 B at the same sha256 (`0fd08b45…`), pinned. (3) `req.<field>` registers as a BoundText under the **COMPOSITE key `__req_<field>`**, and the three readers that can see a bytes field — `emit_starts_with_load_text` (so `byte_at`), `emit_length`, and a new `Field` arm in `emit_streaming_bytes_body` (the whole chunk as a response or concat argument) — probe it before the bare key `req.body` uses. Composite is mandatory here for a sharper reason than in HTTP: the field name is AUTHOR-CHOSEN, so a handler `let` of the same name would overwrite a bare registration (NC-0b below). **Worked example** `examples/tag_probe.verbose` (627 B): `01 41 42` → `01 03000000`, `02 41` → `ff 00000000`, `01` → `01 01000000`, an empty send → connection closed, no bytes, server alive — all over real TCP. **Refusals, each naming the offender and the lifting slice** (design §4.4): `substring` on a bytes field → `rawtcp-inspect-0b` (a bytes VALUE, whose only sinks are streamed — §4.2); `starts_with` / `ends_with` / `contains` / `json_escape` / `parse_int` / text `==` on a bytes field → *"'req.data' is bytes; … Convert explicitly with byte_at"*, `rawtcp-inspect-0b`; `byte_at` / `length` on a bytes concat or `le32`/`le64` → *"operand has no length the emitter can load"*, naming the admitted set; a bytes-typed handler `let` → `rawtcp-inspect-0b` (no bytes sibling of `emit_text_produce_ptrlen`); a record-valued `let` → the `agg-svc-1` breadcrumb of PR #202, inherited verbatim; `random()` in a raw_tcp handler → `entropy-2`, unchanged; `state:` / `after:` / `concurrency: forked` on raw_tcp → `multistep-1`, unchanged; and three the design did not list because they are slice 0's OWN scope line — `read(<resource>)` / `fetch(…)` / `now_unix()` inside a raw_tcp handler (the per-accept effect blocks are the HTTP emitter's; named `rawtcp-effects-1`) and a response that is not `<Out> { <f>: <bytes expr> }` directly or under `if/else` (e.g. a call as the whole body; `rawtcp-inspect-0b`). Each refusal has a corrected twin in the tests. **`byte_at` out of range WAS the design's `sys_exit(1)` of the whole server — implemented as specified (§4.6-3 / NC-0c) and stated plainly at the time as a kill switch; CORRECTED 2026-09-03 by slice `svc-client-abort-1`** (the security bullet below): the abort now closes the connection without a response and the listener survives, via a scope the service emitters open around handler lowering rather than an abort target threaded through `emit_eval_expr`. **A streaming caveat the design does not state**: with `byte_at` as a concat ARGUMENT after a literal, the literal is already on the wire when the abort fires — the client sees `01` then a reset (measured, pinned) — the streaming lowering's documented mid-walk divergence, so "no response" is exact only when the index is evaluated before the first streamed write (the `if` condition), which the worked example's shape does. **Negative controls, all run, all bite**: NC-0a (store `max_request` in the length slot) → both length tests fail with `01 00 01 00 00` = le32(256); NC-0b (bare-name registration) → the colliding fixture answers `[3,0,0,0, 3,0,0,0, 'a','b','c']` — `length(req.data)` AND the echoed chunk resolve to the let — while the non-colliding fixture passes in the same run; NC-0c (`jae` deleted) → the 1-byte frame is ANSWERED from stale buffer bytes instead of exiting; NC-0d (fd hardcoded to 1) → the client receives `[]`. **Additive by measurement**: sweeping all 159 examples × every rule / reaction / service (1388 targets; the 1386 pre-existing: 826 emitted / 562 refused), compiler from `85e95a8` vs branch, baseline-vs-baseline control EMPTY first — **0 size changes, 0 same-size-different-bytes, and exactly ONE status change: `tag_probe::prober` refused → 627 B**. Suite 582 passed / 26 ignored, serial. **gen0** refuses `tag_probe` at index 0 (its only rule, so rule #0 and the declared entry coincide) with rc 1 and zero bytes — exactly its answer for `raw_tcp_echo`, because gen0's own verifier refuses `protocol: raw_tcp` outright; the protocol, not the bytes-field gate. EXPECTED_ACCEPTED stays 97 of a corpus now 159, gen0's binary is byte-identical (`ea4a16c1…`, 657831 B). Interpreter and WASM untouched (services are native-only, structurally — design §2.6). | 627 B (tag_probe) / 358 B (raw_tcp_echo, unchanged) / +0 B for every pre-existing binary | `tag_probe.verbose::prober` (`01 41 42` → `01 03000000`, `01` → `01 01000000`, empty → closed with no bytes) |

*Locked designs for each phase (3, 4, 5b, 2F, 2G, 2H-b) are in [docs/native-designs.md](docs/native-designs.md). They're frozen after implementation — consult them for rationale, not for the current state.*

*The full catalogue of declared external effects (append_file, read, fetch, service listen, fork, clock_gettime) — with declaration shape, required proof, syscalls emitted, error policy, memory bound, audit visibility, and allowed contexts — lives in [docs/effect-model.md](docs/effect-model.md). Read it before adding a new effect, or before judging a refusal. The "what is NOT in the model" section is part of the contract.*

### What native still rejects, and in which priority

- **Result(T, E) with non-scalar T other than Record** (e.g. `Result(collection, _)`) — each remaining shape needs its own calling convention. Decide shape by shape, never fabricate a "universal Result" that carries unnecessary machinery. `Result(Record, text)` lifted (2026-05-07): the Ok arm routes the constructor's fields through `emit_record_as_json` (one JSON line per record, same format as `output: Named(C)` rules); the Err arm stays text → stderr → exit 1. Slice scope (initial): Ok-arm inner is a direct `Concept { ... }` constructor — the rule body's outer if/else still wraps it normally. **Slice extension same day**: `if/else` is now legal INSIDE the Ok arm too, via `emit_ok_record_dispatch` (recursively handles `Record` and `If`; both branches converge on the common `jmp loop_top` after the dispatch). So `Ok(if cond then RecordA{...} else RecordB{...})` works, with two distinct concept-named record shapes selectable at runtime. Pinned by `native_result_record_if_inside_ok_runtime`.
- **Reductions with non-number, non-text output** — Phase 4 covers `output: number` with top-level fold, Phase 5a covers `output: text` per-record, Phase 5b covers `output: text` via fold (append-only body, two-pass sizing). Still refused: `output: Record` with fold-computed fields (needs multi-slot record accumulator), nested folds (acc-slot stack discipline), and non-append-only text fold bodies like `concat(X, acc)` (would force O(N²) memory regardless of strategy — workaround: reorganize into append-only form).
- **Collection-returning rule calls or collection-valued reduction targets** — `map`/`filter` and Phase 4's `fold` target must be a direct `Field(Ident(input), coll_field)`. Composing through an intermediate rule that returns a collection is not supported; the caller has to inline the collection source.
- **Nested `concat(...)` inside a Call arg with its own Call args** — Phase 2H-b unlocked `Call` as a concat argument (one level of pre-eval). The callee's body can itself be a concat, but that inner concat cannot have its own Call args (`is_nested=true` rejects them with a clear message). Two levels of pre-eval would need ad-hoc rbp slots for the outer's r11 across nested pre-evals. Workaround: flatten the composition into a single concat, or an intermediate helper rule.
- **`match_result` with cross-concept callees where the callee's fields are NOT a subset of the outer's** — `match_result` cross-concept (2026-05-06) lifts the same-concept restriction PROVIDED the callee's concept's fields are a subset of the outer's by name AND type. The native emitter validates the subset up-front in `emit_match_result_inlined` and aborts with a breadcrumb naming the missing field or the type mismatch (pinned by `native_cross_concept_match_rejects_missing_field` / `_type_mismatch`). The inlined callee body uses `callee.input_name` for its Field accesses; the existing `offsets` map (built from the outer's concept) resolves them since by contract every name the callee uses also lives in the outer. Worked end-to-end via `native_cross_concept_match_result_runtime`: outer takes Purchase{amount, age, name}, callee `validate_amount` takes AmountOnly{amount}, both arms exercised. **Still refused**: callee fields that aren't a subset (would need argument-passing through additional slots or a real callee frame).
- **Nested `match_result` deeper than the prologue's static walk** — Nested match_result (2026-05-06) lifts the single-quartet limit. The prologue runs `count_match_result_max_depth` over the rule body; reserves N quartets where N = max nesting depth (≥1). Each quartet is 32 bytes (4 × i64) deeper into the frame than the previous one, indexed by depth via `match_slots_at_depth`. The emitter chain (`emit_eval_result_expr` → `emit_match_result_inlined` → `emit_redirect_callee_leaves`) carries both the current `slots` (used by the current match) and a `next_slots` (slots for any nested match_result inside the outer arms; just the current shifted by 32 bytes). For non-nested rules N = 1 — byte-for-byte the same layout as before. exit_flag_slot moves past the last quartet, so its rbp offset is now `-(base + 4*N + 1)*8` instead of fixed `-(base+5)*8`. Worked end-to-end via `native_nested_match_result_runtime`: 2-deep chained validation `match_result(validate_amount(p), amt => match_result(validate_age(p), age => Ok(amt), e => Err(e)), e => Err(e))` exercises all three reachable arms (outer Err, outer-Ok-then-inner-Err, outer-Ok-then-inner-Ok). **Still refused**: nesting deeper than what the static walk discovers (a runtime-built match_result tree would mis-reserve; the AST is fully static so this stays purely a slice-budget concern).
- **VariantConstruct / MatchVariant text fields with concat or Call** — Slice 4c lifts the blanket text refusal but restricts text field expressions to allocation-free shapes (literal, input text field, BoundText, Read). Concat and Call in VariantConstruct text fields are refused — their stack-allocated buffers are freed before the arena entry is consumed. Workaround: use a text literal, input field, or `read(<resource>)`. No current workaround for concat-built text in variant fields (would need arena-local text storage, a later slice).
- **Concat-producing text let bindings in recursive callables** — Number lets and allocation-free text lets (field pass-through, BoundText) work. Concat in a text let RHS is refused because the stack buffer's lifetime doesn't survive across recursive frames. Workaround: use a text input field or `read(<resource>)` instead of building text at the let site.
- **Text return from recursive callables with text input** — The text-return ABI `(rax=ptr, rdx=len)` and the text-input ABI `(ptr, len in struct)` haven't been composed yet. A rule that takes text input AND returns text (e.g., `scan_lexeme(source, pos) → text`) would need both paths active. Workaround: return a number (position or length) and let the caller extract via `substring`.
- **Record constructor text fields in recursive calls: arbitrary expressions** — Only pass-through (`s.source`) and BoundText are supported. Arbitrary text expressions (concat, substring) in a Record constructor field for a recursive call are refused. Workaround: pass the text through unchanged and reconstruct at the callee.
- **Multi-concept `concept_group`** — Slice 4a.1 caps groups at one concept (`g.concepts.len() == 1`). Mutual type recursion across two concepts (e.g. `Expr` ↔ `Stmt`) needs a tag namespace + arena partitioning design and lands in B.4b. Refused with a breadcrumb naming the slice; verifier still accepts the declaration so the interpreter (`--run`) keeps working on multi-concept groups.
- **Multiple `concept_group`s in one program** — Slice 4a.1 caps at one group per program. Multi-group native lowering needs the recursion-across-arenas design and lands in B.4a-followup. Refused with a breadcrumb; same interpreter compatibility note as the multi-concept case.
- **Aggregate return outside slice agg-1 / agg-2a / agg-2c's scope** — a rule may return a plain record concept to another rule, but only in the shape §6.1 of [docs/bytes-value-return-design.md](docs/bytes-value-return-design.md) permits, as widened by agg-2a and agg-2c. Refusals, each naming the offender and the slice that lifts it: **(#1) is LIFTED by slice agg-2a** — a record-returning rule may be in a cycle, and may sit on the callable path of a recursive ENTRY, provided every recursive aggregate call is in a record TAIL position (the whole value of the body, or of an `if` arm) and the callee returns the SAME concept. Three narrower refusals took its place: a tail call whose callee returns a DIFFERENT concept (the forward hands over this rule's destination, so the layouts must match — **agg-2b**); a record-returning rule that BINDS another aggregate to a `let`, which is #8 restated and is the shape that would need a per-frame destination (**agg-2b**, and see the design note's header for why a destination alone would not be enough — callable lets are eager, so that shape SIGSEGVs natively and stack-overflows the interpreter); and a record-output ENTRY on the callable path, where `emit_self_recursive_program`'s result dispatch would itoa the destination pointer (**agg-2c — LIFTED 2026-08-28**: `_start` grew a record result arm, so the entry allocates the outermost destination below rsp, hands it over in `rsi` LAST after the input marshalling, and serialises the returned slots as `emit_record_as_json`'s exact JSON). (#2) any returned field that is not `Type::Number` — Text needs the `(ptr, len)` pair convention and Bool does not emit as a record field in ANY native path today (`emit_record_as_json` refuses it), both **agg-3**; (#3) a `concept_group` (sum-type) return, which already has a convention — one i64 arena index in rax — and must not get a second; (#4) the call is not the ENTIRE right-hand side of a `let`, e.g. `let z = 1 + mk(n)` or `out = 1 + mk(n)`; (#5) the record binding used as anything but `p.<field>`, e.g. `out = p * 1000`. **Both #4 and #5 shipped as emitter-only because they VERIFIED CLEAN at rc 0, and both are now ALSO refused at verify time (2026-08-20)** — #4 by the new `Expr::Binary` operand arm (a `Call` returning `Named(C)` in a Number operand position), #5 by `infer_expr_type`'s `Expr::Ident` arm resolving a record-typed `let` through the binding map, the widening PR #178 declined on the grounds that it "would add strictness far beyond a `.field` access" — which was exactly right, and exactly what #5 needs. The emitter checks STAY: they are the backstop, they carry the slice-naming breadcrumbs, and they are what the `--native` path is judged on. **Refusal #3's verify-clean twin is NOT closed and is the surviving member of the family**: `let g = <group-returning call>` then `g.value` still verifies, because `record_concept_of` filters sum-type concepts out of the binding map by design (a sum-type value is consumed by `match`, never by field access); (#7) the CALLER's own output is not Number/Bool **— narrowed by agg-2c to Text/Bytes callers**: a plain-record caller is now served by `_start`'s record arm, while a text caller's `(rax, rdx)` return and a bytes caller's streaming walk each need their own result-arm interaction analysed, and neither has a consumer yet; (#8) the record-returning callee BINDS a record-returning rule to a `let` **— narrowed by agg-2c to record rules IN A CYCLE**, because the hazard is the eager-let/recursion interaction (the guard never fires), not the composition: a NON-recursive record rule binding an aggregate is agg-2c's own shape, so two aggregate hops compose and a driver may bind TWO aggregates in one frame; the cyclic case stays refused (**agg-2b** — lazy let evaluation with a per-frame destination); (#9) is not a refusal but an implementation — `__agg_<let>_<field>` keying, because bare-name lookup would silently resolve `p.a` to the input's `a`. Also out of scope and listed in the design note's §7: passing a whole record as a call argument, an aggregate in a nested position, `Result(Named(C), E)`, and `concat(le64(p.w0), …)` in a bytes-output caller (that one routes the whole SCC through `emit_streaming_bytes_body`'s streaming ABI, whose interaction with a destination slot group is unanalysed).

  **THE X25519 PRIZE, MEASURED — and the headline is that agg-2a makes it EXPRESSIBLE in one process and MAKES IT SLOWER (2026-08-21).** `docs/bytes-value-return-design.md` §7 row 1 says recursive aggregate return *"unblocks `ladder → x25519_finish`, i.e. **X25519 in one process** and 52 → 1 spawns"*. Half of that is now true and the other half is not, and the difference matters because it relocates the remaining work.

  **What was verified against the real files, not argued.** Both X25519 rules already have exactly agg-2a's tail shape (`out = if <base> then <which-dispatch over lets> else <self>(…)`), so both convert mechanically: replace the `which` chain with a record constructor over the same let-bound values and change `output:`. Done in the scratchpad (deliberately NOT in the repo — converting them is a follow-up, and `examples/ladder_recursive.verbose` has no generator): `ladder` → a 20-field `LadderLimbs` verifies and compiles to 78408 B whose twenty limbs are **byte-identical** to the shipped 20-spawn binary on RFC 7748 §6.1; `x25519_finish` → a 32-field `Digest` verifies; and a driver doing **TWO aggregate hops in one rule** (`let lim = ladder(s)` then `let dig = x25519_finish(X25519RecState { … lim.l0 … })`) compiles to 106582 B and reproduces RFC 7748 §6.1 **byte-exact** (`8520f0…9b4e6a`). Two record `let`s in one caller need no new machinery — each gets its own slot group, and `sret_dest` is set per-let.

  **It is 52 → 32, not 52 → 1, and 32 is SLOWER than 52.** The driver still hands its answer back as a *number*, so it is invoked once per output byte — and each of those 32 invocations now recomputes the ladder, where the shipped path ran the ladder only 20 times. Measured sequentially on this host (WSL2, Ryzen 7 5800X), median of 7: **shipped 20+32 spawns = 91.7 ms; agg-2a 32 spawns = 102.0 ms**. Per-spawn (hyperfine -N, 300 runs): process floor 0.29 ms, `ladder` 2.8 ms, `x25519_finish` 1.2 ms, one-process 3.0 ms — so `20×2.8 + 32×1.2 = 94 ms` and `32×3.0 = 96 ms`, which is the whole explanation. **Folding a prefix into a `which` fan-out multiplies the prefix by the fan-out.**

  **The real prize is one invocation, and it is gated on something that is not a destination.** A SINGLE run of the one-process binary computes the entire X25519 in **3.0 ms** and returns one byte; if the caller could return all 32, that is **~31× against 91.7 ms**. Getting there needs a caller that can EMIT an aggregate — either a record-output CALLER (refused here as **agg-2c**) or `concat(le64(…))` in a bytes-output caller (§7's last agg-2 row). Neither is about crossing a call boundary; both are about how `_start` renders a result. So the sentence to carry forward is: **agg-2a closed the composition gap and left the EMISSION gap open, and only the second one holds the CPU win.** For the TLS consumer the 32 may not even matter — if the next stage is also a Verbose rule, `dig.b0 … dig.b31` feed straight into its input record in the same process (measured on a two-stage probe); what is missing is one way to emit 32 bytes at the very end.

  **THE EMISSION GAP CLOSED, AND THE WIN IS DELIVERED AND MEASURED (2026-08-28, slice agg-2c).** The same scratchpad conversion (deliberately still NOT in the repo — the two crypto files have no generator, and converting them is its own follow-up), driven by a `Digest`-output driver: `let lim = ladder(s)` then `let dig = x25519_finish(X25519RecState { … lim.l0 … })` then `out = Digest { b0: dig.b0, … b31: dig.b31 }` — 110655 B, **one invocation, all 32 bytes as one JSON object, byte-exact against RFC 7748 §6.1** (`c3da5537…a28552`), and both converted rules byte-identical to the shipped per-spawn binaries on all 20+32 outputs first. Measured on this host (WSL2, Ryzen 7 5800X, hyperfine -N): **one process = 3.16 ms ± 0.62 (median 3.19, 50 runs)** against the shipped 52-spawn sequential chain re-measured at **93.1 ms ± 3.3 (10 runs)** — **~29×**, with per-spawn components floor 0.22 ms / ladder 2.94 ms / finish 0.98 ms (arithmetic 90.1 ms, agreeing with the measured chain). So agg-2a's projected "3.0 ms / ~31×" is CONFIRMED within noise — and the honest sequence over the arc is: shipped 52 spawns 93.1 ms → agg-2a's 32 spawns **102 ms (a regression)** → agg-2c's 1 invocation **3.2 ms**. The remaining `concat(le64(…))`-in-a-bytes-caller row of §7 is now about BYTE-STREAM output (for piping into a non-Verbose consumer), not about the CPU win — a TLS-stage consumer reads `dig.b0…b31` straight from the record already.

  **THE CONVERSION IS IN THE REPO, AND vcrypto IS 2 SPAWNS (2026-08-28, the follow-up the
  paragraph above names).** `examples/ladder_recursive.verbose` (hand-edited — it has no
  generator) and `examples/x25519_rec.verbose` (REGENERATED — the paragraph above says "the
  two crypto files have no generator", and that is half wrong: `tools/tls_gen/x25519rec_gen.py`
  writes x25519_rec and was verified to reproduce the shipped file byte-for-byte BEFORE being
  edited) now return records: `ladder` → a 20-field `LadderLimbs` (x2_0..x2_9, z2_0..z2_9),
  `x25519_finish` → a 32-field `Digest` (b0..b31). The `which` field is GONE from both input
  concepts, so one invocation yields the whole aggregate as one JSON object. Byte-exactness
  measured, not argued: the shipped which-form binaries' outputs were captured FIRST (all 20
  limbs + all 32 bytes, THREE RFC 7748 vectors — §5.2 v1, §6.1 public-key, §6.1 shared
  secret), and the record binaries reproduce every value exactly; the interpreter agrees on
  all values for both rules. `tools/tls_gen/vcrypto.py::x25519()` collapsed from 52 pooled
  spawns to 2 sequential ones parsing the record JSON — measured on this host (WSL2, Ryzen 7
  5800X, time.perf_counter over 20 calls): **38.7 ms median (52 parallel spawns, 64-thread
  pool) → 2.44 ms median (2 spawns)**, ~16× against the POOLED path (the pool was already
  hiding the 93 ms sequential cost; the honest ladder of figures is 93.1 sequential → 38.7
  pooled → 2.44). The self-test prints `x25519=0.003s`, was `0.1s`. The thread pool STAYS —
  sha256 / key-schedule / gctr / ghash `which`-loops still need it; X25519 no longer touches
  it. gen0's verdict IMPROVED, not regressed: it accepts both converted files at rule #0
  (their only rule) and its emitted binaries agree with verbosec's **byte-for-byte on
  stdout** (the `fib_pair`-at-rule-#0 agreement cell, not the record-let SIGTRAP class —
  neither file joins the `aggregate_pair::total` gaps-table row). The native pin
  `x25519_ladder_recursive_matches_rfc7748` got STRONGER: it asserts the exact 20-limb JSON
  record from ONE run, where the which form sampled 4 limbs at one full ladder each.

  and `derive_s_hs_traffic` → a 32-field `Digest` (b0..b31) each, `expand_key` → a 16-field
  `KeyBytes`, `expand_iv` → a 12-field `IvBytes` — one concept PER RULE in the two-rule file,
  because the rules' output WIDTHS differ (16 vs 12) and a record's width is its concept
  declaration; the field names stay `b0..bN-1` in all four so the host unpacks every record
  with one `rec[f"b{i}"]` loop. Each generator was verified byte-in-sync with its committed
  `.verbose` BEFORE being edited (regenerate → `git status` clean), then edited and re-run —
  the `which` field is GONE from `Ecdhe` / `DeriveInput` / `Secret` and from every `reads:`

  vectors + the vcrypto self-test vector), each capture validated against the independent
  Python hmac/hashlib reference before any edit; the record binaries reproduce all 16

  are one record spawn each, so a server key-schedule leg (hs → s_hs → key + iv) is **4

  **SHA-256 FOLLOWED (2026-08-29, tranche 4) — and it is the FIRST tranche
  whose converted rule is RECURSIVE, so it exercises agg-2a (tail recursion)
  AND agg-2c (record-output entry) rather than agg-1 alone.**
  `examples/sha256_fold.verbose` (`tools/tls_gen/sha256n_gen.py`,
  generator-driven and verified byte-in-sync with the committed file BEFORE
  editing — regenerate → `git status` clean) declares ONE rule `sha256_fold`
  that RECURSES over N pre-padded 64-byte blocks (`decreasing : i`) and at the
  base case now returns all 32 digest bytes as one `Digest` record (b0..b31)
  instead of one byte per `which` spawn. Two edits: the base case's 32-branch
  `if s.which == k` dispatch became a `Digest { b0: band(shr(s.h0,24),255),
  … b31: band(shr(s.h7,0),255) }` constructor — the recursion sits in a record
  TAIL position (the whole value of the `else` arm), so agg-2a's forward hands
  the ONE caller-allocated destination down every frame and only the base case
  writes it — and the recursive tail call dropped `which: s.which`; the `which`
  field is gone from `ShaState` and from the `reads:` proof. 106230 → 110256 B
  native.
  Byte-exactness measured FIRST, oracle-anchored: the shipped which-form
  outputs were captured on FOUR vectors — empty → `e3b0c442…`, `"abc"` →
  `ba7816bf…` (FIPS-180), the 448-bit 2-block vector → `248d6a61…`, and a
  200-byte 4-block message (exercises the recursion depth) — each validated
  against the published SHA-256 vector AND Python `hashlib` before any edit;
  the record binary reproduces all four exactly, and the interpreter agrees on
  all four. Reproducible emit: compiled twice, byte-identical.
  `tools/tls_gen/vcrypto.py::sha256()` collapsed from 32 parallel `which`
  spawns (64-thread pool) to ONE record spawn via `_record_bytes` — measured
  on this host (WSL2, Ryzen 7 5800X, perf_counter, median of 20, on the
  200-byte 4-block message): **25.6 ms → 0.35 ms, ~73×**; the self-test grew a
  timed three-vector sha256 leg (empty / abc / 4-block, each vs hashlib) and
  prints `sha256=0.001s` beside `x25519=` / `keysched=` / `sched=`, AEAD
  roundtrip green. The thread pool STAYS — AES/GCM/GHASH still spawn one byte
  per `which`; those are the final tranche.
  CONSUMERS: five files reference `sha256_fold`. `vcrypto.py` is the ONLY
  which-spawn site and is converted; `sha256n_gen.py` is the generator
  (updated). The three P-256 files (`make_cert_p256.py`, `ecdsa_p256.py`,
  `tls_browser_p256_server.py`) only `ensure`-compile the binary and hash via
  `V.sha256()`, so they need NO interface change (nor do `tls_server.py`,
  `tls_browser_server.py`, `verify_binder.py`, which likewise route through the
  wrapper). P-256 exercised: `ecdsa_p256.py`'s RFC 6979 Appendix A.2.5 vectors
  (messages `"sample"` and `"test"`) produce the EXACT published (k, r, s)
  through the record-form `sha256()` (its `__main__` PEM tail needs the absent
  `cryptography` module — unrelated to this change). Clean negative: NO
  `src/native.rs` test consumed this rule's which interface (the only
  `sha256_fold` grep in `src/` is a comment in the corpus-sweep doc block), so
  nothing grew into the hole.
  gen0's verdict is the AGREEMENT cell, and it is the FIRST converted crypto
  file gen0 leads INTO agreement rather than refusing (tranche 3's tls_schedule
  refused at every index): `sha256_fold` is a single rule, so rule #0 == the
  declared entry, and gen0 ACCEPTS the record form (199940 → 202260 B), its

  across all three vectors (all == hashlib). Same shape as `fib_pair`
  post-agg-2c: gen0's recursive record emit serialises the arena node its own

  Corpus sweep over all 154 examples × every rule/reaction/service (1375

  **AES/GCM/GHASH FOLLOWED (2026-08-30, tranche 5) — the LAST tranche of the
  aggregate cash-in: NOTHING vcrypto compiles spawns per-`which` any more.
  THE POOL DOES NOT DIE, and the reason is the finding.** The finish line
  this arc aimed at was deleting vcrypto's 64-thread pool once AES/GCM/GHASH
  converted — and its premise ("the thread pool below exists solely for
  them", the module docstring) was ALREADY false when tranche 5 started:
  `run_bytes` has EIGHT external call sites in FIVE files driving still-
  which-form rules — `hkdf_extract` (tls_server.py, tls_cert_server.py,
  tls_browser_server.py, tls_browser_p256_server.py) and `psk_early_secret`
  / `psk_ext_binder_key` (tls_server.py, verify_binder.py). So the pool
  STAYS, its docstring now names its real users, and the oracle line's
  parenthetical says "the pool serves only external run_bytes callers —
  hkdf_extract + psk_*". Convert `hkdf_extract.verbose` +
  `psk_schedule.verbose` (outside this tranche's three-file scope) and the
  pool dies for real.
  NO GENERATOR EXISTS for any of the three files — the first tranche that is
  entirely hand-edited (tranches 2-4 were generator-driven; the x25519
  tranche was half-and-half). Verified by grep over `tools/`: no `*_gen.py`
  writes aes_encrypt / aes_gctr / ghash_nblocks.
  THE SHAPES, per file. `examples/aes_encrypt.verbose::encrypt` → a 16-field
  `CipherBlock` (b0..b15): the 16-way `which` dispatch became a constructor
  over the same `st10_*` lets, `which` gone from `EncryptInput` and the
  `reads:` proof (39915 → 41756 B). `examples/ghash_nblocks.verbose::
  ghash_fold` → a 16-field `GhashOut`: RECURSIVE, sha256_fold's exact shape
  (agg-2a tail forward + agg-2c record entry) — base case's dispatch became
  the constructor, the recursive tail constructor dropped `which:` (271435 →
  273269 B). `examples/aes_gctr.verbose::gctr` → a PER-BLOCK `CipherBlock`:
  a fixed-width record cannot cover variable-length data, so `which` becomes
  a BLOCK index (`[0, 63]`, was data-byte `[0, 1023]`), the counter is
  `s.which + 2` directly, and the rule decodes all 16 of its block's hex
  byte-pairs and XORs them with the keystream lets (40297 → 48637 B). The
  HOST loop over blocks STAYS — `_gctr` spawns once per block (nb spawns,
  16× fewer than per-byte), PADS the tail block's hex to 32 chars and
  truncates after unpacking. The padding is LOAD-BEARING, not cosmetic:
  `byte_at`'s fail-closed bounds mean an unpadded short tail ABORTS the
  binary — the record form reads all 16 pairs where the which form only ever
  read the bytes the host asked for.
  **ONE COMPILER CHANGE, the first in any cash-in tranche, and it is a
  ROUTING widening, not an emitter.** encrypt/gctr are record-output entries
  with CROSS-CONCEPT callees (`aes_sbox(Byte { b: ... })` / `xtime`), a
  combination no prior tranche hit (sha256_fold/ghash_fold call only
  themselves; the schedule rules call nothing). `emit_record_program`'s Call
  inline shares the caller's offsets map — measured, the converted encrypt
  failed there with `unknown field 'b'` — while the WHICH forms had always
  routed through the CALLABLE path via `compile_native_code`'s cross-concept
  detector, whose entry gate was `Number | Bool | Text`. The gate now also
  admits a plain-record-output entry (`entry_routable`), landing it on
  agg-2c's `_start` record arm with agg-1's callee-side field writes — both
  halves already shipped; the tranche connected them. Such an entry is also
  walked by `check_aggregate_shape` (same gate as the other record-entry
  routes). Additive BY MEASUREMENT: corpus sweep over all 154 examples ×
  every rule/reaction/service (1375 unique targets), baseline `f99eedb` vs
  branch, baseline-vs-baseline control EMPTY first — **exactly the THREE
  converted rule-binaries change, 1372 byte-identical by size + sha256, 0
  status changes, 0 same-size-different-bytes, refused set 558 → 558.**
  Byte-exactness measured FIRST, oracle-anchored twice over: the shipped
  which-form binaries' outputs captured on TEN vectors — FIPS-197 C.1 and
  NIST SP 800-38A F.1.1 for encrypt, NIST GCM Test Case 2 for gctr (C =
  0388dace…) and for ghash (S = f38cbb1a…), the src/native.rs 48-byte
  3-block gctr vector and 4-block ghash vector, plus the vcrypto self-test's
  H / EJ0 / gctr / ghash values — every capture validated against an
  INDEPENDENT Python FIPS-197/SP-800-38D reference before any edit, the
  published vectors matching where one exists. The record binaries reproduce
  all ten exactly AND the interpreter agrees on all ten (20/20 cells).
  Reproducible emit: each converted rule compiled twice, byte-identical.
  `tools/tls_gen/vcrypto.py`: `_aes_block` 16 pooled spawns → ONE record
  spawn; `_ghash` 16 pooled spawns → ONE (the recursive fold walks every
  block in-process); `_gctr` len(data) pooled spawns → nb sequential record
  spawns. The AEAD roundtrip (encrypt+decrypt, 11-byte payload) measured on
  this host (WSL2, Ryzen 7 5800X, perf_counter, N=10): **140.9 ms median
  (which, pooled) → 3.6 ms median (record), ~39×**. The self-test grew a
  NIST-anchored AES/GCM leg (FIPS-197 C.1 through `_aes_block`, GCM TC2
  through `_gctr` AND `_ghash`) and a timed AEAD leg: VCRYPTO_OK prints
  `aead=0.004s` beside the other legs, roundtrip green.
  CONSUMERS, enumerated: `vcrypto.py` (converted); `tls_record_check.py`,
  the only OTHER which-interface driver (`/tmp/tr_*` binaries), converted to
  the record interface with the same pad-and-truncate contract —
  TLS_RECORD_OK re-verified over its 3 random records (5/17/40-byte
  payloads, so the tail truncation is exercised) against its own Python GCM
  reference. The TLS servers (`tls_server.py`, both browser servers,
  `tls_cert_server.py`) drive AEAD exclusively through the vcrypto wrappers
  (`aead_encrypt`/`aead_decrypt`) — confirmed by grep, no direct spawn
  sites. `src/native.rs`: the three runtime pins moved to EXACT-JSON record
  asserts (values + field count in one comparison, stronger than the
  per-`which` integer parses), `aes_gctr_matches_reference` grew the
  pad-then-truncate tail case (20-byte message), and
  `aes_family_declared_sbox_tables_match_fips197_oracle`'s two floor rows
  for aes_encrypt/aes_gctr became record-constructor asserts (the 16-way
  dispatches legitimately became constructors; the branch UPPER bound still
  catches a re-unrolled S-box chain). Clean negative otherwise: no other
  test consumed the which interface.
  gen0's verdicts, at BOTH indices per the metric discipline (rule #0 in the
  two AES files IS `aes_sbox` — the gaps-table trap): aes_encrypt and
  aes_gctr are REFUSED at rule #0 AND at the declared entry (index 2), in
  BOTH forms — the pre-existing `bytelit_operands` refusal (declared S-box
  table), so the conversion moves nothing and EXPECTED_ACCEPTED stays
  96/154. `ghash_nblocks` (single rule, so the two indices coincide) is
  ACCEPTED in both forms (549567 → 550791 B), and the record-form gen0
  binary AGREES with verbosec **byte-for-byte on stdout + exit code** on
  both GHASH vectors — the sha256_fold/fib_pair agreement cell: gen0's
  recursive record emit serialises the arena node its own walk produces, and
