  **HKDF-EXTRACT + THE PSK SCHEDULE CLOSED THE ARC (2026-08-30, tranche 6) —
  THE POOL IS DEAD.** The three LAST which-form rules vcrypto's consumers
  drove — `hkdf_extract` (examples/hkdf_extract.verbose), `psk_early_secret`
  + `psk_ext_binder_key` (examples/psk_schedule.verbose) — now return one
  32-field `Digest` record each, so `run_bytes` and the 64-thread `_POOL`
  reached ZERO callers and are DELETED from `tools/tls_gen/vcrypto.py`,
  along with the `concurrent.futures` import. The module docstring's premise
  ("to beat the one-byte-per-process-run cost, every which-loop is spawned
  in parallel") is dead with it: **every TLS primitive vcrypto spawns is ONE
  record spawn of a verified binary** — that sentence is what the whole
  aggregate-return cash-in (tranches 1-6) existed to make true. Honest
  scope: vcrypto's pool. `ed25519.py` keeps its OWN per-call 64-thread pool
  for the five still-which-form Ed25519 rules (sha512_fold / ed_scalarmult /
  ed_affine / sc_reduce / sc_muladd) — a separate module that never imported
  vcrypto; the asymmetric half is a future arc.
  THE SHAPES. All three rules are non-recursive HMAC let-chain unrolls with
  `calls : []`, so they land on the plain record-entry path (agg-1 caller
  shape, agg-2c record entry) — NO compiler change, unlike tranche 5's
  routing widening: with no callees there is nothing to route. Per rule: the
  32-way `which` dispatch became a `Digest { b0: …, …, b31: … }` constructor
  over the same let-bound values, `which` left the input concepts (Extract /
  Psk / Early) and every `reads:` proof; the psk file's two rules share ONE
  Digest concept (tranche 3's width argument). 326384 → 329708 B
  (hkdf_extract), 323008 → 326332 (psk_early_secret), 322960 → 326284
  (psk_ext_binder_key). Generator-driven: `extract_gen.py` + `psk_gen.py`
  verified byte-in-sync BEFORE editing (regenerate → git status clean).
  Byte-exactness measured FIRST: the shipped which-form outputs captured on
  EIGHT vectors (four hkdf_extract — the self-test's HMAC(derived, ecdhe)
  shape, the binder chain's HMAC(fin_key, thash) shape, a zero salt, a fixed
  random pair; two per psk rule — the repo PSK chain and a second PSK),
  every capture validated against Python hmac/hashlib BEFORE any edit; the
  record binaries reproduce all eight exactly, the interpreter agrees on all
  eight, and each rule compiles twice byte-identical. **RFC 5869 TC-1 is NOT
  expressible through this rule and the tranche brief was wrong to name it
  as the oracle**: the rule's IKM — the HMAC *message* — is fixed at 32
  bytes, TC-1's IKM is 22. The TC-1 anchor (published PRK 077709362c…) lives
  in examples/hkdf.verbose, whose 64-byte-salt/22-byte-IKM `hkdf_extract`
  CAN express it; `hkdf_matches_rfc5869` drives THAT file, is untouched, and
  stays green — scope the two same-named rules apart before citing either.
  CONSUMERS: all EIGHT external `run_bytes` sites converted to the three new
  vcrypto record wrappers (`hkdf_extract` / `psk_early_secret` /
  `psk_ext_binder_key`, now in ALL_RULES): tls_server.py (3 sites),
  verify_binder.py (2), tls_cert_server.py, tls_browser_server.py,
  tls_browser_p256_server.py (1 each — their local hkdf_extract helpers now
  delegate). PROVEN LIVE, not just grepped: `verify_binder.py` printed
  BINDER_VERIFIED against a FRESH ClientHello captured from openssl 3.6.2
  (not a stored fixture), and `tls_server.py` completed a FULL PSK-DHE
  handshake against `openssl s_client` — binder OK, client Finished
  decrypted, "hello world" delivered over TLS_AES_128_GCM_SHA256 — the first
  end-to-end TLS handshake in this repo where every crypto step is a single
  record spawn and the pool no longer exists to fall back on.
  KEYSCHED_ASSEMBLY_OK and TLS_SCHEDULE_OK re-verified. Timings: VCRYPTO_OK
  from a cold /tmp (x25519=0.003s keysched=0.001s sched=0.002s sha256=0.001s
  extract_psk=0.001s aead=0.003s, roundtrip green; extract_psk is the new
  self-test leg, oracle-asserted); the converted leg alone (WSL2, Ryzen 7
  5800X, perf_counter, median of 20): **74.1 ms (96 pooled which spawns) →
  1.0 ms (3 record spawns), ~74×**.
  Additive BY MEASUREMENT: corpus sweep over all 154 examples × every
  rule/reaction/service (1375 targets), baseline `08dcb3b` vs branch,
  baseline-vs-baseline control EMPTY first — exactly the THREE converted
  rule-binaries change, 1372 byte-identical by size + sha256, 0 status
  changes, refused set 558 → 558. Clean negative: no src/ test consumed
  these rules' which interface. gen0, at rule #0 AND the declared entries:
  **both files ACCEPTED in BOTH forms at every index** (hkdf single-rule;
  psk idx 0 = psk_early_secret, idx 1 = psk_ext_binder_key, the two indices
  emitting DIFFERENT ELFs — idx honored), and every record-form gen0 binary
  is **byte-for-byte == verbosec on stdout + exit code** across the capture
  vectors — the first tranche where EVERY converted rule lands in the
  agreement cell. EXPECTED_ACCEPTED stays 96/154; R0/R1/R2 hold. One
  measurement note for future probes: gen0 must be built `--stdin-raw`
  (`compile_native_stdin_raw`, as the acceptance sweep does) — a plain
  `--native` gen0 tokenizes stdin and refuses every file at rc 1, which
  reads exactly like six refusals and is an artifact of the wrong build.

  **THE AEAD FRAMING FOLLOWED (2026-09-02) — the §4 "no cryptography in the
  host" claim is now true of every TRANSFORMATION, and the tranche needed
  NO compiler change.** `docs/tls-io-statemachine-design.md` §7 MAJOR-1
  found the claim false: the host computed nonce = IV XOR seq, the AAD and
  GHASH length block, J0 = nonce‖00000001, tag = S XOR E(J0), the partial-
  block zero padding and the tag compare — all in Python, so a reviewer
  grepping the harness could NOT confirm every tag byte came from a
  verified binary. Every one of those but the padding is FIXED-WIDTH, which
  is exactly what a record-returning rule can now hand back, so
  `examples/gcm_frame.verbose` moves each into a rule: `gcm_nonce` /
  `gcm_j0` / `gcm_aad` / `gcm_lenblock` / `gcm_tag` / `gcm_tag_eq` (see the
  examples list). Hand-written, no generator — the nearest siblings
  (aes_encrypt / aes_gctr / ghash_nblocks) have none.
  THE ONE AGGREGATE HOP IS `gcm_j0`: it `let n = gcm_nonce(i)` and then
  emits `J0Block { b0: n.b0, … b11: n.b11, b12: 0, b13: 0, b14: 0, b15: 1 }`
  — agg-1's record let inside agg-2c's record-output entry, on a same-
  concept callee, which is `aggregate_emit::driver`'s shape without the
  recursion. It compiled the first time it was reached (5371 B). That is
  the whole point of the arc restated in one rule: J0 is derived from the
  nonce INSIDE the verifier's reach instead of by slicing in the host.
  THE CONSTANT-TIME COMPARE IS BRANCH-FREE BY SHAPE, AND THE SHAPE IS
  CHECKED IN THE EMITTED x86. `gcm_tag_eq` folds the 16 byte pairs into one
  accumulator with `bxor` / `bor` only (15 chained lets), then answers
  `1 - min(d15, 1)` — `min` lowers to `cmp + cmovl`, not a `jcc`. objdump on
  the 4459-byte binary: from the first field load after the last input
  bounds check (`0xe29`) to the result (`0x10cd`) there are 676 bytes of
  `mov / push / pop / xor / or` and one `cmp rcx,rax ; cmovl rax,rcx ; sub`
  — **zero conditional jumps**. The first `jcc` after it (`jns` at `0x10e2`)
  is the itoa's sign test on the 0/1 RESULT, uniformly not-taken. What is
  NOT constant-time is stated in the rule's own comment: the argv
  transport — the per-field decimal `atoi` loops once per digit, so `"5"`
  and `"255"` cost different iterations — is a property of how the host
  feeds the binary, not of the compare, and the binary's own `[0,255]`
  bounds checks branch uniformly-not-taken for every in-range input.
  BYTE-EXACTNESS MEASURED FIRST, AGAINST AN INDEPENDENT REFERENCE: a pure-
  Python GCM (AES + GF(2^128) core copied from `tls_record_check.py`'s
  reference, the six framing steps written out one function each) was
  checked against the PUBLISHED SP 800-38D Test Cases 1–4 intermediates
  (H, C, GHASH S, E(K,Y0), T — all reproduced) BEFORE any edit, and the
  host's pre-change Python path captured against it (TC1–4 C and T exact,
  11 RFC 8446 nonce cases exact). The six rules then reproduce every cell
  natively AND in the interpreter: J0 == Y0 and T == S XOR E(Y0) on all four
  test cases, the TC4 length block `…00a0 …01e0` (20-byte AAD, 60-byte C),
  8 AAD lengths, 8 length-block pairs, 11 nonce cases including seq =
  2^63−1 (the i64 ceiling the `seq` field declares). `gcm_tag_eq`: equal →
  1, and 0 on EVERY single-bit flip in every one of the 16 positions (128
  flips × 3 tags), plus the NIST tags cross-matrix. Each rule compiled
  twice, byte-identical (reproducible emit).
  `tools/tls_gen/vcrypto.py`: `_nonce` and `_gcm` are GONE; `aead_encrypt`
  / `aead_decrypt` call the six wrappers. The BEFORE/AFTER host-crypto
  ledger, so the §4 claim can be quoted rather than believed — BEFORE:
  nonce XOR, AAD construction, length-block construction, J0 construction,
  tag XOR, tag compare (`calc != tag`, and NOT constant-time), zero-
  padding, block loop. AFTER: zero-padding of a variable-length tail
  (`_pad16`), concatenating `pad(A) || pad(C) || lenblock` into GHASH's hex
  input, the per-block `gctr` loop, byte shuttling between binaries, TLS
  framing that is not crypto (append/strip the inner content type, slice
  header/ct/tag), and a branch on `gcm_tag_eq`'s public VERDICT. The only
  `^` left in the file is the self-test's single-bit-flip negative control.
  Randomness (`os.urandom` for the server scalar / random) stays the one
  host-side SECRET source, as §7 already named. Cost of the honesty,
  measured on this host (WSL2, Ryzen 7 5800X, perf_counter, median of 20,
  quiet system): protecting the 11-byte self-test record goes **1.64 →
  3.93 ms** and unprotecting it **3.26 → 3.80 ms** (roundtrip ≈ 4.9 →
  7.7 ms); a 40-byte record 3.41 → 4.70 / 4.81 → 4.82; an empty one 1.71 →
  3.84 / 3.29 → 3.55. Protect is 8 + nb spawns instead of 3 + nb (each
  ~0.3 ms process floor); unprotect barely moves because it no longer
  runs `gctr` over the ciphertext BEFORE checking the tag. The record
  bytes are byte-identical to the pre-change capture on all three vectors.
  ORACLES: `rm -f /tmp/v_*` then `python3 tools/tls_gen/vcrypto.py` →
  VCRYPTO_OK with a new NIST-anchored `framing=` leg (nonce / J0 / length
  block / tag / tag_eq asserted against the published TC2 + TC4 values)
  and the AEAD roundtrip + tamper-rejection green; `tls_record_check.py`
  (TLS_RECORD_OK — its `v_tls_record` driver now spawns the five framing
  binaries, so the check is Verbose-framed records against a pure-Python
  reference, and it compiles its `/tmp/tr_*` inputs on demand instead of
  assuming them); and the live `tls_server.py` PSK-DHE handshake against
  `openssl s_client` 3.6.2 — binder OK, client Finished decrypted, "hello
  world" delivered — which is the real oracle for the framing, since a
  wrong nonce or tag fails the handshake.
  Additive BY MEASUREMENT: corpus sweep over all 156 examples × every
  rule/reaction/service (1383 targets, 824 emitted / 559 refused),
  compiler from `490cd50` vs branch, baseline-vs-baseline control EMPTY
  first — every pre-existing target byte-identical by size + sha256, and
  the only rows the new file adds are its own six. The compiler (`src/`)
  is UNCHANGED apart from the sweep test's constants, so "everything else
  byte-identical" is structural as well as measured. Clean negative: no
  `src/` test consumed `_nonce` / `_gcm` / the AEAD host path (zero greps
  outside `tools/`), so nothing grew into the hole.
  gen0, at rule #0 AND every other index (five of the six rules are call-
  graph roots): `gcm_nonce` / `gcm_aad` / `gcm_lenblock` / `gcm_tag` /
  `gcm_tag_eq` ACCEPTED, each gen0 ELF **byte-for-byte == verbosec on
  stdout + exit code** on the reference vector; `gcm_j0` (index 1) is the
  fourth file with the `aggregate_pair::total` gaps-table signature — gen0
  rc 0, a 22510-byte ELF that SIGTRAPs with no output where verbosec emits
  5371 B printing the J0 record; the existing row covers it. EXPECTED_ACCEPTED
  moves 96 → 97 (rule #0 is `gcm_nonce`, which gen0 gets right), total 155
  → 156; R0/R1/R2 hold.

- **A record-valued `let` inside an HTTP service handler is refused, and the refusal now tells the truth (2026-09-03, diagnostic only — slice `agg-svc-1` is the lowering, and it is NOT built).** `let q = swap2(In { a: 1, b: 2 })` in a service handler, where `swap2` returns a plain record concept, verified clean (`all proofs check out`) and then died with **`native codegen error: unknown rule 'swap2' for native inlining`** — a rule that exists, that the verifier had just resolved, named as unknown. The mechanism is a deliberate emptiness the message never mentioned: `compile_http10_dynamic_service` hands `emit_http10_dynamic_bytes` an EMPTY rules map (`no_rules`, `src/native.rs:20904` → `:20934` at `c5e46b0`) because the service path inlines no rule call (a callee's fields would resolve against the handler's `HttpRequest` offsets — the `unknown field` failure tranche 5 hit on the rule path). Two consequences, one visible: `let_rhs_is_text`'s Call arm (`:1224`) consults that empty map and classifies EVERY call as a number let, the number arm of the Phase 2I-H let prologue (`:21827`) hands it to `emit_eval_expr`, whose Call arm looks the callee up in the same empty map (`:16118`) and reports it unknown. Fix: the let prologue is handed the program's REAL rules and concepts for diagnostics only (two new parameters, nothing is emitted from them), and a `let` whose RHS is a Call to a callee whose `output:` satisfies `plain_record_concept` — the same predicate the rule path keys agg-1's sret slot on — is refused with a breadcrumb naming the service, the handler, the let, the callee, the record concept and its field count, the fact that the prologue lowers TEXT `(ptr, len)` and NUMBER slots only, and `agg-svc-1` as the lifting slice (agg-1's caller-allocated destination applied to the service frame). **The producer matrix, measured on a 13-cell probe set (one handler, one RHS per cell) with the `c5e46b0` compiler and the branch — only ONE cell moves**: a number expression (942 B), a text literal (403 B), `concat` (987 B) and `req.path` (934 B) compile byte-identically on both; `read(cfg)` and `fetch(up, …)` as a WHOLE let RHS are refused on both with `Expr::Read in number context — read() returns text, use it in a text-typed position` (and the Fetch twin) — misleading in its own way, since `let_rhs_is_text` has no Read/Fetch arm so the let IS classified number, but a DIFFERENT producer (the Read/Fetch arms of `emit_eval_expr`) and deliberately not touched here; a Call to a NUMBER-returning, TEXT-returning, `Result`-returning, top-level-`variants:`-returning or `concept_group`-returning rule still reports `unknown rule '<callee>' for native inlining` on both — the SAME producer line and the same falsity, left unchanged because this slice moves exactly the record cell, and each of those needs its own lifting-slice name (a follow-up can widen the same branch: the callee is already looked up); the RECORD-returning Call goes from `unknown rule 'swap2' for native inlining` to the breadcrumb above. **Zero emitted bytes, measured**: all 158 examples × every rule / reaction / service (1386 targets, 826 emitted / 560 refused), compiler from `c5e46b0` vs branch, baseline-vs-baseline control EMPTY first — 0 status changes, 0 size changes, 0 same-size-different-bytes. Pinned by `native::tests::record_valued_handler_let_is_refused_with_a_truthful_breadcrumb` (verify-clean fixture → refusal naming all seven needles, anti-assertion on `unknown rule`, zero bytes written, and the number-let twin still compiling); verified to FAIL against `c5e46b0` with `got "unknown rule 'swap2' for native inlining"`.

### Register conventions across emitters

Emitters that span multiple syscalls or phases share a register layout. Adding a new cross-phase register use requires either claiming a currently-unused register or saving/restoring on the stack — do not casually reassign any of these without auditing every caller.

| Register | Used by | Introduced |
|---|---|---|
| `r12` | argc (read at `_start`) | Phase 0 |
| `r13` | argv base pointer | Phase 0 |
| `r14` | current record index inside the main loop | Phase 0 |
| `rbp` | field-slot frame base (fields + let bindings at `rbp - 8*(i+1)`) | Phase 0 |
| `r15` | (per-emitter role — one or the other, never both in the same binary): file descriptor from `open()` in reaction emitters (Phase 1A) / inner loop counter in collection emitters (Phase 3) | Phase 1A / 3 |
| `r10` | concat buffer base for later length calculation | Phase 1B |
| `rbx` | concat write pointer (advances as args are written) | Phase 1B |
| `r9`  | saved pre-allocation `rsp`, used by the dynamic-sized concat path to free the buffer via `mov rsp, r9` (Linux `write` takes only 3 args, so `r9` survives the syscall). Set only when at least one concat arg is a text field. | Phase 1B (text-field) |
| `r11` | arena_base — `lea r11, [rbp + arena_rbp_offset]` set once at the entry rule's prologue when the program declares a `concept_group`. Recomputable at any point from `rbp + arena_rbp_offset` (the offset is a compile-time constant), which is the failsafe when an intervening syscall has clobbered r11. Linux syscalls clobber rcx and r11 by ABI, so the per-record body's text-write / append_file / read / fetch syscalls all destroy r11 — slice 4a.2's VariantConstruct emit will either reload r11 from its lea expression before each arena access OR keep arena access strictly outside syscall-touching code paths. Slice 4a.1 sets up r11 unconditionally on group programs (the lea is harmless even when no VariantConstruct site reads from it); the load-bearing artifact in 4a.1 is the rbp-relative arena_rbp_offset, not the register value. | Phase B slice 4a.1 |

Dedicated rbp-relative slots:

| Slot | Used by | Introduced |
|---|---|---|
| field slots (`rbp - 8*(i+1)`) | input concept fields — Number via atoi, Text stores argv pointer | Phase 0 / Phase 2E |
| let-binding slots (`rbp - 8*(nfields + k + 1)`) | `let` bindings evaluated in source order | Phase 0 |
| `match_slot` at the bottom of the frame | `match_result`'s inlined-callee Ok-value binding (reserved unconditionally in `emit_result_program`) | Phase 2D |
| MatchVariant arm-binder pool (`n_binder_slots` × 8 B, immediately below `exit_flag_slot` and ABOVE the resource / connection / now_unix / arena blocks) | Payload binders bound by an arena `match` arm. Reserved by `emit_record_loop_prologue` **only** for `emit_full_program` (the one emitter that builds an entry-level `ArenaCtx`, so the one whose body can reach `emit_eval_expr`'s arena dispatch) and only when a `concept_group` is declared; every other caller passes `reserve_match_binder_pool: false` and is byte-for-byte unchanged. `emit_callable_into` reserves its own pool below its own input+let slots. **Sized by DEPTH, not width** — `max_stacked_match_binder_slots`, not `count_max_match_arm_binder_slots`: the dispatch bumps each binder below every live slot, so a `match` nested in an arm body (including one reached through an INLINED rule call, since `emit_full_program` passes `self_call: None`) stacks below the enclosing arm's binders. See the frame-collision note below the table. | 2026-08-18 |
| `sret_slot` (8 B, the LAST regular slot of a callable's frame — strictly BELOW the VariantConstruct tmp pool) | Slice agg-1's destination pointer, spilled from `rsi` at the callable's prologue. Allocated ONLY when the rule's `output:` is a plain record concept, so every other frame is byte-identical. **Below the tmp pool is not a preference**: `tmp_slot_base_local` is computed WITHOUT `tmp_slots` while `total_slots` includes it, so a slot inserted above that anchor silently re-points every VariantConstruct spill. Reloaded into `rcx` per field rather than parked in a register — `rbx`/`r15` are the A2/A4 fast path's and `r11` is the arena base. **Slice agg-2a gives the slot a SECOND reader**: a recursive aggregate call in a record TAIL position loads it back into `rsi` (`mov`, 4 B) immediately before its own `call`, so the callee writes into the destination THIS frame was handed. `SretDest::Frame` (`lea`, a slot group this frame owns) vs `SretDest::Forward` (`mov`, a pointer this frame was handed) is a TYPE and not a boolean for that reason: measured, swapping `mov` for `lea` at the forward site prints the right answer at depth 0 and **`0` at rc 0** from depth 1 on. | agg-1 / agg-2a |
| `node_count_slot` (8 B at the bottom of the frame, below now_unix when present) | `concept_group` runtime arena entry count. Initialized to 0 in the prologue; slice 4a.2+ will bump on each successful VariantConstruct. Only allocated when the program declares a group. | Phase B slice 4a.1 |
| arena (`max_nodes * entry_size` B below `node_count_slot`) | `concept_group` runtime arena storage. Entry = 1 tag byte + 8 bytes per payload field, padded to 8-byte alignment. Reserved unconditionally when a group is declared, written by slice 4a.2's VariantConstruct emit. | Phase B slice 4a.1 |

Registers *not* in this table (`r8`, `rcx`, `rdx`, `rsi`, `rdi`, `rax`) are ephemeral — emitters may clobber them freely within a single expression. Note that Linux syscalls clobber `rax`/`rcx`/`r11`; any state that must survive a syscall belongs in `r10` or `r12`–`r15`. Slice 4a.1's `r11` arena base is set BEFORE any syscall in the prologue and is RE-set at every prologue (rule-entry, not per-record), so the only emitter constraint introduced is "don't make a syscall between `lea r11, ...` and the first VariantConstruct site". The recursive call/ret path doesn't touch r11 because Verbose-emitted callables don't use it.

**A frame region that is WRITTEN but never RESERVED is invisible to every gate in this repo (fixed 2026-08-18).** The arena `MatchVariant` dispatch derived its binder slots as `lowest_live_slot - 8`, scanning `binding_offsets` + `text_bindings` for that lowest slot. The five bottom-of-frame reserved slots — the match_result quartet plus `exit_flag_slot` — are in **neither map**, and `frame_slots` carried no binder term, so the pool did not sit below the reserved region, it sat **on top of it**: binder #1 aliased `match_slot`, #2 `err_ptr_slot`, #3 `err_len_slot`, #4 `err_frame_save_slot`, **#5 `exit_flag_slot`**, and #6+ ran on into the resource / connection / arena blocks.

The symptom is the reason this is worth a paragraph: **the binary printed the RIGHT answer and exited with the 5th binder's value mod 256.** A number-output binary must exit 0 — a nonzero exit is the fail-closed signal every abort path in this compiler uses — so `if ./binary; then …` was wrong for 255 of every 256 values, with correct stdout and no diagnostic. Measured on a synthetic probe: N=4 binders → rc 0, N=5 → rc `a+5`, N=8 and N=12 → still rc `a+5` (the 5th binder, independent of N), and `a=251` → rc 0, the one input in 256 where the defect hides.

**Sizing it needed DEPTH, not width, and that is the half a "reserve the max arm" fix gets wrong.** The pool is consumed by a bump-below-the-lowest-live-slot discipline, and the binders an enclosing arm placed ARE live inside its body — so a nested `match` stacks below them, and nesting reaches through an **inlined rule call** (`emit_full_program` passes `self_call: None`, so every call on that path is inlined with the arm-extended offsets map). Measured pre-fix on a 2-level chain: 2×2 stacked binders → correct, 2×3 → rc 9, 2×4 → rc 8. Hence `max_stacked_match_binder_slots`, a callee-following walk with a visited set, rather than the per-arm max `count_max_match_arm_binder_slots` the callable path uses.

**Why nothing caught it, which is the transferable part.** `examples/vexprparse.verbose` has **413 arms with ≥5 binders** (133 at 5, 56 at 6, 201 at 7, 23 at 12) and gen0 self-compiles correctly — so the corpus *looks* like a positive control and is not one. Two independent reasons, both measured rather than assumed: the SCC's matches run inside **callables**, where `emit_callable_into` already reserved a pool at exactly the offset the naive derivation lands on; and of the 162 rule-binaries whose bytes this fix changes, **all 162 take a `concept_group`-typed input**, which argv cannot marshal (the same shared verbosec/gen0 hole that makes `callable_let_variant::depth` SIGSEGV on both compilers). They could never be *run*. gen0's real entry `elf_program_src` is byte-identical across the fix, which is why R0/R1/R2 held before and hold after. **A construct being everywhere in the corpus is not coverage if nothing executes it** — and an exit code, unlike stdout, is invisible to a differential that only compares output.

Pinned by `match_variant_binder_pool_does_not_alias_reserved_frame_slots` (N ∈ {4, 5, 8}, because the THRESHOLD is the defect and a single-N test passes against an off-by-one fix; includes the `a=251` mod-256 wrap) and `nested_match_variant_binder_pool_stacks_without_overflow`; both verified to FAIL pre-fix.

**Self-hosted emitter invariant (`examples/vexprparse.verbose`, service slice 5a).** The service accept loop it emits is **closed**: every branch tail is `e9 <negative>` and the trampoline contains **zero `e8` call bytes** — it never transfers control to any emitted proc. That is what makes the arena registers reusable inside it: `r14` (the node counter) has been clobbered by the S2 field-select since slice 2, and slice 5a claims `r15` (the mmap arena base) as the `open()` fd for the per-request `log:` block. Both are safe *only* because no proc ever runs from inside the loop. This breaks the day a service trampoline calls an emitted proc — at that point the log block's fd needs a real save/restore or its own slot. `rbx` is NOT available: it has been the parsed request-field pointer since slice 2 (`write(r13, rbx, r14)` is S4's body write), which is why the log block diverges from the reaction trampoline's rbx-as-fd convention and matches verbosec's `emit_append_file_call` (fd in r15) instead. The swap is byte-length-neutral (`mov r15,rax` = `mov rbx,rax` = 3 B; `mov rdi,r15` = `mov rdi,rbx` = 3 B), so every reaction sizing mirror transfers unchanged.

**Slice 5b's span capture — a LIVENESS window, not register freedom.** When a `log:` block's content names `req.method` / `req.path`, the emitter splices a 9-byte capture per field at tramp+258, immediately after the parse and **before** the handler's field-select (`method → (r10, r8)`, `path → (rbp, r9)`). The select collapses one endpoint pair **asymmetrically** — the method variant destroys `rbx` (path_end) — so "read the other field after the select" is not a general answer. Of the four registers used, **`r10` and `rbp` are genuinely free** in the accept loop; **`r8` and `r9` are not** — `r8` is the parse loop's byte scratch (dead only *after* the loop exits) and `r9` is the itoa's sign save (write-before-read, and every itoa sits *after* the capture). They are safe purely because the captured values only need to survive `[capture, log block)`. **The load-bearing consequence: the log block can never move later than immediately-after-the-select** — a post-response-write placement would read an itoa-clobbered `r9` and emit a garbage path. This binds S5c's `on_error` tail and any future post-response hook. Note that `rbp` is the *safest* of the four, not the riskiest: emitted procs `push rbp` / `pop rbp`, so a future tramp→proc call would preserve it — `r14`/`r15` are the casualties of the closed-loop invariant precisely because procs clobber them without saving. Do not generalize the invariant to `rbp`.

**Slice 5d (2026-08-10) made that window the ONLY log placement.** Slice 5b left a second slot — a STATIC-content log stayed pre-read at tramp+158, where 5a first put it, because moving it would have changed bytes. Measured against verbosec, that slot writes an audit line for every *accepted connection*, including one the accept loop then drops unanswered, so it is now gone: `x86_service_tramp` emits the block only at the post-select site, for every content shape, and `jlog` is unconditionally `logsz`. Two consequences. First, the liveness argument above is no longer conditional on the content naming a field — the placement is fixed, so a future slice cannot quietly reintroduce a pre-parse variant with different audit semantics. Second, the paragraph's warning binds harder: the block sits between the field-select and the response write, and **both** ends are now load-bearing for **every** service that declares a log. The S1 constant-response branch is the single exception, and only because it emits no parse and therefore has no such window at all (see the `log:` row of the gen0 gaps table).

**verbosec's native emitter is DETERMINISTIC — the same source produces the same bytes (fixed 2026-08-10, was live from slice 5.4 until then).** The compiler is now reproducible: recompiling a `.verbose` file yields a byte-identical binary, which is what the project's audit story requires — someone verifying a deployed binary against a rebuild must not see a spurious mismatch.

The defect was that `collect_scc_containing` ended with `forward.intersection(&backward).cloned().collect()` — a `HashSet` iteration, and Rust's default hasher is randomly seeded. That `Vec<String>` is the order `emit_self_recursive_program` lays its callables out in, so a rule whose SCC had ≥2 members got a *different byte order* on every compile: **same size, different bytes**. Measured on an UNMODIFIED compiler, a synthetic 3-member cycle produced 2 distinct sha256 over 30 compiles, and `examples/vexprparse.verbose`'s `mb_args` produced 5 distinct hashes over 8 runs (`mb_arms` / `mb_ast` / `mb_vfields` / `shape_ast` / `shape_vfields` 4 each, `shape_args` / `shape_arms` 5 each) while single-member neighbours like `mb_binds` produced exactly 1. Fix: `out.sort()` before returning. The caller (`compile_native_code`) pushes the entry rule first, so the layout is **entry-then-alphabetical**; no position is load-bearing, because `emit_self_recursive_program` resolves the entry by NAME (`labels[entry_rule.name]`).

Cost, measured with a baseline-twice / fixed-twice sweep over the 749 native rule-binaries the corpus produces: **exactly 8 binaries changed, 741 byte-identical, 0 changed size** — and the 8 are precisely those `vexprparse` multi-member-SCC rules named above. Fixed-vs-fixed showed **0 differences across all 749**, which is the corpus-wide determinism proof. `elf_program_src`, `eval_ast` and `x86_program_src` were confirmed byte-identical across the change (they are single-member), so R0/R1/R2 hold and corpus acceptance stays 100/151. Pinned by `scc_callable_order_is_deterministic_across_compiles` (`src/native.rs`), which asserts both halves — that `collect_scc_containing` returns sorted, and that 40 end-to-end compiles are byte-identical — and each half was verified to FAIL independently against the unsorted build.

Two sibling sources were found in the same sweep and fixed with it. **`emit_eval_expr`'s multi-field pass-through fallback** iterated `offsets.iter()` (a `HashMap`) to emit one `mov` per field; the copies are commutative and the total size is order-invariant, so it was pure byte churn. It is a *dead defensive path* today (it needs `labels` to hit while `struct_layouts` misses, and all three `SelfCallCtx` constructions populate them from the same loop) — fixed anyway, sorted by rbp offset i.e. field declaration order, because a latent landmine costs nothing to defuse and the branch would otherwise reintroduce the exact symptom. **`src/wasm.rs`'s data-section sort** was `sort_by_key(|(_, &off)| off)`, and offsets tie: `literal_cursor += s.len()` means an EMPTY literal does not advance the cursor, so the next distinct literal gets the same offset, and `sort_by_key` is *stable* — ties kept HashMap order. Not theoretical: `out = concat("", w.v, "alpha")` produced 2 distinct module hashes over 20 builds at an identical 304 bytes. Fixed by tie-breaking on the literal itself; 30/30 identical after.

**The measurement lesson outlives the bug, because it changes how you read a sweep.** Until this fix, a *same-size-different-bytes* delta was **as likely to be the hasher as a real regression** — and same-size-different-bytes is exactly the signature of the dangerous silent byte-swap you run these sweeps to catch. The discipline that was required was a **baseline-vs-baseline** run: compile the corpus twice with the *unmodified* compiler, and treat anything differing there as noise rather than as your patch's doing. That control is what separated the 5 noise entries from the 8 genuinely-changed ones in this very change. Post-fix the control should come back **empty**, so it is now a cheap assertion rather than a subtraction step — and if a future baseline-vs-baseline sweep is ever non-empty again, a new nondeterminism source has been introduced and the sweep's conclusions are void until it is found. Note also what the fixed point could NOT tell you: gen0 self-compiles deterministically and always did, because its own entry chain is single-member — a green R0/R1/R2 is silent about the multi-member rules sitting in the same file.

Remaining known nondeterminism, deliberately NOT changed because none of it reaches emitted bytes: several `NativeError` messages `{:?}`-print a `HashSet` (`ac.group_concept_names`), and the verifier emits duplicate-fetch / missing-payload-field / purity errors by iterating `HashMap`s and `HashSet` differences. These vary the ORDER and wording of diagnostics between runs, never the accept/reject verdict and never the AST the emitter consumes. They would matter to a golden-file test of compiler stderr; there is none today.

**gen0's node arena is a first-class budget, and running out of it does not look like running out of it.** `examples/vexprparse.verbose` declares `concept_group VExpr [max_depth: 4096, max_nodes: N]`, and N is what the Rust native emitter mmaps for gen0. Before the bitwise arc, N was 6 000 000 and gen0's self-compile was touching essentially ALL of it — measured peak RSS 633 440 KiB against a 624 MB arena, i.e. no headroom whatsoever, while the comment in the file still claimed a "~1.5M node" peak. Any slice that added rules tipped it over, and the symptom is thoroughly misleading: gen0 does not report exhaustion, it **exits 1 having already streamed a partial ELF to stdout** (~750 KB of the 2.6 MB it owed), because the emit is streaming and the arena bounds-check fires mid-walk. That reads like a verify refusal or a codegen bug, and — because what actually varies is how many AST nodes an addition costs — it appears to track the SHAPE of whatever you just wrote (one branch fine, three not; a record-marshalled call fatal; a 3200-byte dead literal fine). A shape bisect will produce a coherent, confident, wrong diagnosis. Raised to 8 000 000, leaving ~2M nodes (~25%) spare; the reservation is `MAP_NORESERVE`, so the extra 208 MB is virtual and only touched pages cost RAM. **When a slice that grows this file starts failing strangely, check `max_nodes` first.**

### Self-hosted compiler (gen0): known verification gaps

gen0 (`examples/vexprparse.verbose`) parses, verifies and emits — but its verifier is a **strict subset** of verbosec's. Some declarations it parses PAST without checking. That is not the same as accepting them: the declaration is still consumed structurally (so the program is not truncated), it is simply not mechanically verified. Each gap below is a case where **gen0 accepts a program verbosec would reject**, and each closes in its own later slice.

| Declaration | verbosec checks | gen0 does |
|---|---|---|
| `hints:` name, and `justification` presence / non-emptiness, and INVERTED `overflow : [min, max]` | the name is a closed set (`vectorizable`, `parallel`, `cache_result`, `overflow`); the three trust-based hints require a non-empty string justification, the bare keyword form being a parse error; `min > max` is a verify error | **FIXED** (2026-08-14, `hint_errors`, corpus unchanged at 100). Three of the four hint gaps, closed by one linear TOKEN walk. **The token-vs-source call inverts vs. the attribute rows above, and that is the whole reason this was cheap**: `attr_errors` / `attr_pres_errors` walk the RAW SOURCE because the tokenizer *discards* attribute lines, so there is nothing downstream to check — but every token of a `hints:` block reaches the parser, which is precisely how `span_is_hints` finds the header in order to `skip_indented_block` past it. The information was already tokenized, already length-delimited, already free of comment and indentation noise; re-deriving it from bytes would have been more work and less precise. `hint_errors` anchors on the four-token run `Ident("hints") ':' Newline Indent` — a field named `hints` is `hints : <type>` with no Newline after the colon, so it cannot false-positive, and a stray top-level block (already refused by `toplevel_junk`) is harmlessly also checked. **The legal name set was DERIVED, not remembered**: `Parser::parse_hints_block` matches `overflow` as the bracket form and dispatches the string form over exactly `vectorizable` / `parallel` / `cache_result`, everything else falling to `unknown hint '{}' (allowed: …)`. **Why an unknown NAME is the row that matters most**: `hints:` is the one construct the language guarantees is semantically inert ("a hint must never change observable behaviour"), so a misspelled hint — `vectorisable`, `paralell` — reads as a declared, verified optimization and is in fact nothing at all, with no diagnostic anywhere. That is the closed-attributes rule applied to hints. Every check is conservative on shape: an unrecognised entry charges nothing rather than guessing, and the empty-justification test is `Str.len > 2` (a Str token's `len` spans both quotes) where verbosec additionally rejects an all-whitespace string — weaker in the safe direction, as with the attribute NAME row. Cost: **zero emitted bytes** — verify-side, mechanically confirmed by sweeping all 151 examples with a gen0 built from HEAD and one built from the patched source: **150 of 151 byte-identical (size + sha256)**, the 151st being `vexprparse` itself, with the baseline-vs-baseline control coming back empty first. gen0's own source grew 620802 → 628435 B (+7633, nine new rules) and still self-compiles: R0/R1/R2 hold. Not one of the 512 existing tests had grown into this hole (contrast PR #162's fifty-five) — the same negative result PR #163 reported. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_verifies_hint_names_justifications_and_overflow_shape`, whose 15 cases each carry a corrected twin, put the bad entry SECOND in three `multi_*` cases (the single-hint shape every `examples/negative/` fixture uses is the degenerate one), and cover `cache_result` — a legal name appearing in **no file in `examples/`**, so a typo in its recognizer would pass every existing gate |
| `hints: overflow` — declared interval vs. COMPUTED range | `computed range [0, 2000000] exceeds declared [0, 2]` — the declared bounds are checked against the rule's logic by interval arithmetic | **OPEN, measured** (`hint_overflow_bad`) — the one hint gap left, and the row above is the reason it is now stated separately. **The two `overflow` fixtures were previously described as ONE gap** ("needs interval arithmetic"), and that mis-scoping is what kept the cheap half open: `min > max` needs two integer literals and a `>`; `[0, 2]` against a computed `[0, 2000000]` needs an abstract interpreter over the parsed logic — the same absent machinery the `termination.bound` row is waiting on. A real arc, not a slice. Asserted as-is in the pin above, both as documentation and as the discriminator proving the three closed checks are not merely "gen0 refuses anything with a `hints:` block" |
| `@layer` NAME (`domain \| application \| interface`) | closed set — `unknown layer 'X' (allowed: domain, application, interface)` at parse time | **FIXED** (2026-08-10, `attr_errors`) — see the attribute-line note below. Was: gen0 emitted a 2023-byte ELF at exit 0 for `@layer: architectural` |
| unknown `@attribute` name | closed set per declaration kind — `unknown attribute '@X' in rule (allowed: @intention, @source, @layer)` | **FIXED for the name, deliberately WEAKER than verbosec** (2026-08-10, `attr_errors`) — gen0 checks the name against the UNION `{verbose, intention, source, layer}`, so it catches `@author` but accepts `@layer` on a *concept*, which verbosec refuses. Weaker in the safe direction: it can miss a violation, never invent one |
| `@source` / `@intention` PRESENCE | mandatory on every declaration; a missing one is a parse error | **FIXED** (2026-08-12, `attr_pres_errors`, corpus unchanged at 100). Was: gen0 emitted a 567-byte binary at **rc 0** for a rule with no `@intention` at all — a binary whose blocks trace back to nothing, which is exactly what design priority #4 (Traceability) exists to prevent. **The matrix was DERIVED, not assumed, and that was half the slice**: only `rule` and `concept` had a fixture, so the other five declaration kinds had never been measured. `src/parser.rs` shows all SEVEN ending their parse with the same `.ok_or_else(...missing @intention / @source)` pair (concept_group 225/228, concept 310/312, rule 579/581, reaction 1745/1746, service 1999/2000, resource 2139/2140, connection 2294/2295) — **no kind-specific exemption, no conditional** — and each was then probed to confirm the read. Twelve fixtures were added to complete the matrix (7 kinds × 2 attributes, plus the NESTED-concept shape where the enclosing group carries both and only the concept inside does not). **This row's previous version called the check structurally out of reach** — "a presence check must … decide where a declaration ENDS (`concept_group`'s nesting makes that genuinely ambiguous)" — and that framing was the expensive artifact, the same way the stale INDENT/DEDENT comment was: the question never has to be answered, because a declaration owns every line up to the NEXT header, so the inner scan simply STOPS at the next header and nesting falls out for free rather than being a special case. The sixth consecutive slice whose fix was WIRING: all seven keyword recognizers (`span_is_rule` / `_concept` / `_concept_group` / `_reaction` / `_service` / `_resource` / `_connection`) already existed for gen0's own parser, so `span_is_decl_kw` is a seven-way `if` over them and the walk reuses `attr_errors`' own `line_width` / `next_line_start` line stepping. **STRICTNESS DIRECTION INVERTS vs. the NAME row above, and that is the load-bearing difference**: the name check may use the weaker UNION because it can only ever MISS a violation, whereas an over-strict PRESENCE check would REJECT VALID PROGRAMS — so this one is exact, and the guard is that a header is `<keyword> <space(s)> <identifier>`, all three required (an output field named `rule` puts `rule = …` at the head of a logic line, and `=` is not an identifier start). Cost: **zero emitted bytes** — verify-side, so 99 of the 100 accepted binaries are byte-identical by sha256 and the 100th is `vexprparse` itself; the baseline-vs-baseline control came back empty. gen0's own source grew 617907 → 620802 B (+2895, five new rules) and still self-compiles: R0/R1/R2 hold. **The one real casualty was the repo's own TEST CORPUS, and it is a finding rather than a fixture to paper over: 55 of the 509 unit tests began failing, every one of them for the same reason.** The inline gen0 probes throughout `src/native.rs` are written in gen0's *header-less* dialect — no `@verbose` line, no attributes — because gen0 checked neither, so they were invalid Verbose in two independent ways and 55 tests were resting on the gap. (Two of the shapes are worth naming, because they show how deliberate the drift was: `self_hosted_emitter_embeds_large_checker_source_without_truncation` builds its fragment with a `strip` closure that explicitly DROPS `@`-lines, and `verbatim_scan_word_...` prepends an attribute-less `rule main` driver onto an otherwise-valid `examples/` file.) The check was NOT relaxed for them. A single idempotent test helper, `with_probe_attrs`, adds the two lines at the gen0 invocation sites — idempotent being what makes blanket application safe, since a declaration that already has an attribute is left alone, so a verbatim `examples/` file passed through it is unchanged byte-for-byte. It also cannot mask a defect: the lines it adds are pure traceability, so a probe invalid for any *other* reason stays invalid, which is what keeps the verify-gate and refusal tests meaningful. Two consequences worth knowing. First, two probes stopped fitting in a single argv slot (the count_rules fragment measured 121754 → 136678 B against Linux's 131072 `MAX_ARG_STRLEN`) and now pipe into a stdin-raw-built emitter. Second, **14 service byte-pins were re-baselined, and the SHAPE of the move is the proof the emitter did not change**: every row moved by EXACTLY +96 B (= 48 B per declaration × the handler rule + the service) or by EXACTLY 0, nothing in between, and no row changed bytes while keeping its size — the zeros being the shapes whose ELF embeds no source blob, which a longer source cannot reach. A real emitter change could not produce that pattern. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_enforces_mandatory_intention_and_source`, which verifies each of the 15 fixtures AND its corrected twin, and carries an explicit false-positive guard |
| `@source file:line` RESOLUTION | the referenced file must exist and have that line | **OPEN** — split out of the PRESENCE row when that one closed. Needs to open the referenced file, i.e. a second input channel gen0 does not have (it reads exactly one program on stdin). No fixture: a fixture would be refused by verbosec for a reason gen0 cannot even in principle observe today |
| `proofs:` BLOCK PRESENCE, and `purity:` / `termination:` INSIDE it | all three mandatory on every rule, refused at PARSE time — `rule 'X' missing 'proofs' block`, `proofs missing 'purity'`, `proofs missing 'termination'` | **FIXED** (2026-08-14, `proofs_errors`, corpus unchanged at 100). A rule's proofs are what make it verifiable at all — a rule with no `proofs:` block has nothing to verify — and gen0 required none of the three. **TWO OF THE THREE ONLY LOOKED CAUGHT, and that is the finding this row exists for.** Measured on 517451e with a body that READS a field, the no-`proofs:` and no-`purity:` shapes came back rc 1, which reads exactly like a presence check and is not one: with no `purity:` block the declared read set is empty, the body performs a read, and PR #161/#166's under-declaration walk fires. Change the body to a constant and all three sail through — `no proofs` rc 0 / 548 B, `no purity` rc 0 / 548 B, `no termination` rc 0 / 548 B (and rc 0 / 562 B even with a reading body, since the purity walk has nothing to say about termination). The row's previous version reported the gap as one shape ("gen0 accepts a rule with no `proofs:` block") because that is the one the failing-test corpus surfaced; the sub-blocks were never probed. **The fifth check in this arc found to be correct on half the input space**, after text `==`, `purity_reads_missing`'s empty-declaration-only shape, and the degenerate-only `hints:` fixtures — so all three negative fixtures use a no-read body, and the reading-body variants are pinned separately where a corrected twin makes them attributable. **THE MATRIX IS RULE-ONLY, DERIVED NOT ASSUMED, and it is the first one in this arc that is NOT seven-celled**: PR #162 found all seven declaration kinds requiring both attributes, so another seven-cell matrix was the expectation — but `parse_proofs_block` has exactly ONE call site in `src/parser.rs` (`parse_rule`, line 567) and exactly one `.ok_or_else` consuming it (line 588), and the other six kinds do not merely not-require a `proofs:` block, they do not ACCEPT one, each rejecting it with its own closed-set body error (probed on all six: `expected attribute, 'fields:', or 'variants:' in concept body`, `… 'trigger:', or 'effects:' in reaction`, and so on). **A TOKEN walk, not a source walk** — the same call `hint_errors` makes and the opposite of `attr_errors` / `attr_pres_errors`, for the reason those two exist: attribute lines are lexed away, whereas every token of a `proofs:` block is in the stream (that is how `span_is_proofs` finds the header to skip past it). Tokens are also strictly MORE precise here, because verbosec accepts `proofs :` and `purity  :` — an Ident and a Colon with whitespace between — so a byte match on `"proofs:"` would REJECT A VALID PROGRAM. Order is scanned rather than assumed for the same reason: verbosec's `parse_proofs_block` is a dispatch loop, so `termination:` before `purity:` is valid and a positional check would refuse it. The ninth slice in this arc whose fix was WIRING: `span_is_proofs`, `skip_indented_block`'s depth/seen accounting, `skip_seps_dedent` and `toplevel_junk`'s declaration walk all existed; the two new `span_is_*` recognizers are copies of `span_is_proofs` with different bytes. `proofs_errors` deliberately does NOT call `parse_rule_decl_pos` — a rule declaration is ONE indented block, so `skip_indented_block` from the header already steps over logic, proofs and hints, and re-parsing ~925 rules a third time would spend arena nodes for nothing. Cost: **zero emitted bytes** — verify-side, mechanically confirmed by sweeping all 151 examples with a gen0 built from the parent and one from the patched source: **150 of 151 byte-identical (size + sha256)**, the 151st being `vexprparse` itself, with the baseline-vs-baseline control coming back empty first. gen0's own source grew 1848579 → 1857157 B and its binary 635053 → 639902 B (+4849); rules 925 → 932, concepts 364 → 365; R0/R1/R2 hold. **15 of the 512 tests had grown into the hole** (contrast #162's 55, #159's 3, #161's 1), all of them inline gen0 probes in gen0's header-less dialect carrying no proofs — corrected, not accommodated, by widening the same idempotent `with_probe_attrs` helper #162 introduced. The inserted `reads: [] / calls: []` is truthful by construction: a probe that performed a read or a call while declaring none was ALREADY refused by the purity walk, so any probe passing without a block performs neither. **NOT ONE CORPUS FILE AND NOT ONE `vexprparse` RULE TRIPPED THE CHECK** — and because a clean sweep of a newly-strict check is exactly the shape a VACUOUS check has, that was verified rather than assumed: `examples/invoices.verbose` with its `termination:` block deleted is accepted by the parent gen0 at **513 B** and refused at zero bytes after. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_requires_proofs_purity_and_termination`, which carries a corrected twin per shape, pins both body halves so a regression to incidental-catching is a distinct failure, and guards the over-strict direction with reversed sub-block order, spaced colons, a trailing `hints:` block, and a second rule as the one missing its proofs |
| `@layer` STRATIFICATION (sealed subgraph) | verified across the call graph: a layered rule may only call layered rules, and only layers its own may call | **FIXED** (2026-08-15, `layer_list`, corpus unchanged at 100). Was: `examples/negative/layer_violation.verbose` and `layer_calls_unlayered.verbose` both accepted at **rc 0, 596 bytes**, where verbosec reports `rule at layer 'domain' calls 'is_app' at layer 'application'` and `rule declares layer 'application' but calls unlayered rule 'helper'`. `@layer` is a documented language feature — the sealed-subgraph discipline — and gen0 verified none of it. **THE ROW MIS-PRICED ITSELF, for the FOURTH time in this arc** (after the INDENT/DEDENT comment, the attribute-PRESENCE "genuinely ambiguous" note, and the `overflow`/`termination.bound` pairing): it said this "needs the call graph, not the source text, and needs the layer value ATTACHED to each rule … the one attribute check a source-line walk structurally cannot do", which is two independent reasons to file it as an arc and **both are false**. `check_layer_discipline` (`src/verifier.rs`) is **DIRECT-CALL ONLY** — for each call in the rule's facts, resolve the callee BY NAME, error if it is unlayered, error if `caller_layer.can_call(callee_layer)` is false — with no transitive closure, no traversal and no fixpoint; so the "call graph" is one lookup per callee. And the source walk needs no new anchor, because a parsed `RuleDecl` already carries `rd_name_start`, its own name's byte offset, making `decl_layer_at` byte-for-byte the `decl_has_intention` walk (scan from the line after the header, stop at the next header) with a different payload — O(declaration) per rule, not O(file). The tenth-plus consecutive slice whose fix was WIRING: `attr_value_start` and the `span_is_domain` / `_application` / `_interface` trio already existed for #160's closed-set VALUE check, `decl_header_at` for #162's PRESENCE segmentation, `find_rule` / `rule_named` / `prf_calls` / `rd_proofs` for the parser and the purity walks. **THE CALLEE SET IS THE DECLARED `calls:` LIST, not a fresh AST walk, and that is a real dependency stated so it can be found**: it is sound only because purity is now checked in BOTH directions (missing PR #161, extra PR #165, the twelve opaque families walked PR #166), so for any program verbosec ACCEPTS `check_purity`'s set comparison guarantees declared == performed and the declared list IS `facts.calls`; on a program where they differ, purity refuses it anyway, so no accepted program is judged against the wrong set. Weaken either purity direction and this check silently weakens with it. **STRICTNESS IS EXACT, like PRESENCE and unlike the attribute NAME row**: an over-strict layer check REJECTS VALID PROGRAMS, so `layer_call_ok` enumerates all nine caller×callee cells plus the unlayered-callee case rather than approximating, and an UNLAYERED CALLER returns 0 before anything else runs (verbosec only enters the check under `if let Some(caller_layer) = rule.layer`) — which is what keeps `examples/vexprparse.verbose`'s own ~940 rules untouched, since **it declares no `@layer` at all** (verified: zero line-leading `@layer` in the file). **THE ONE CELL A NAIVE PORT GETS WRONG is `interface -> unlayered`**: `Layer::can_call`'s Interface arm returns `true` unconditionally, so a bare port accepts it, while verbosec's `None` arm runs FIRST and refuses — hence the leading `callee == 0` guard. That cell is invisible to every other gate in the repo, because **`@layer: interface` appears in no file anywhere**, fixtures included. Cost: **zero emitted bytes** — verify-side, mechanically confirmed by sweeping all 151 examples with a gen0 built from the parent and one from the patched source: **150 of 151 byte-identical (size + sha256)**, the 151st being `vexprparse` itself, with the baseline-vs-baseline control coming back empty first. Corpus acceptance stays **100/151** with an unchanged refused set; gen0's own binary grew 642469 → 645062 B (+2593, six rules and four concepts), rules 932 → 942 and concepts 365 → 369; R0/R1/R2 hold. **NOT ONE of the 512 tests had grown into the hole** (contrast PR #162's 55, #167's 15, #166's 9) — and because a clean sweep of a newly-strict check has exactly the shape a VACUOUS one does, non-vacuity was verified rather than assumed, on a REAL corpus file: `examples/layers.verbose` with its two `@layer` values SWAPPED is accepted by the parent gen0 at 2023 B and refused at zero bytes after, while the file as shipped still compiles to the same 2023 B. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_verifies_layer_stratification`, which enumerates the full 4×4 matrix (10 accept, 6 refuse) asserting verbosec's verdict on the same bytes FIRST so a moved reference is a distinct failure, carries a minimally corrected twin per fixture, and pins the swap control; verified to FAIL pre-change |
| `purity.reads` / `purity.calls` | verified against the AST, BOTH directions — a declared entry the logic never performs is an error (`extra: [...]`) exactly as a performed one that is undeclared (`missing: [...]`) | **BOTH DIRECTIONS now checked — MISSING 2026-08-11, EXTRA 2026-08-14, so the declared set must EQUAL the performed set rather than merely contain it.** The row's previous version reported MISSING as caught, and that was **false — it held only for the DEGENERATE shape**. `purity_reads_missing` declares `reads: []`, and the empty declaration was the only shape gen0 caught: measured on 36eccbe, `reads: [t.a]` against a body reading `t.a + t.b` was **accepted at rc 0, 720 bytes**, where verbosec reports `missing: [t.b]`. Root cause: `parse_name_list` stored each entry's **ROOT IDENT** only (`skip_path` walked past the `.field` suffix without recording it), and `count_undecl_read_ast`'s AstField arm **descended to that root before querying** — so the question asked was *"is the input mentioned anywhere in reads:"*, not *"is THIS path declared"*, and one declared entry blessed every field of the input. **A partial under-declaration — declare most reads, omit one — is the realistic shape of a concealed read**, and the declared set IS the audit surface, so the check was close to cosmetic. Fix (verify-side, **zero emitted bytes**): a new `path_end` rule gives `parse_name_list` the entry's WHOLE span (`s.source`, 8 bytes, not `s`), and new `ast_path_head_start` / `ast_path_head_len` let the AstField arm query the whole path `root..last-segment` and NOT recurse when the root is the input. `name_in_namelist` is untouched — the comparison was always right, it was being fed a root ident on both sides, which is the fifth slice in this arc whose fix was WIRING rather than machinery. Deeper paths (`t.a.b`) are checked once at the outermost node, mirroring verbosec's `expr_to_path`. **`purity.calls` was measured both ways at the same time and was already correct** — a `calls:` entry is a bare rule name, so whole-entry and root-ident spans coincide and the storage bug could not reach it; `calls: [h1]` against a body calling `h1` and `h2` was already refused. Cost: 151/151 corpus binaries byte-identical (size + sha256), acceptance unchanged at **100/151**, gen0's own 906 rules still accepted (they were already strict-clean, because verbosec has always verified them this way). Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_detects_partial_purity_underdeclaration`, and by two new fixtures `purity_reads_missing_partial` / `purity_calls_missing_partial`.

**EXTRA CLOSED 2026-08-14** (`extra_reads` / `extra_calls` + `ast_has_opaque`, wired into `purity_errors`; `purity_reads_extra` and `purity_calls_extra` flip GAP → PASS, taking the negative corpus to 30/5/0). Was: over-declaring conceals nothing, so it was the benign direction — but not harmless, because half of a set comparison is not a set comparison. With only MISSING, the declared set is a SUPERSET of the performed set, so a rule may claim to read a field, a resource or the clock it never touches and no tool disagrees, and that is *false explicitation*, which this project forbids by name. Measured on 8c7b4f8: `purity_reads_extra` **rc 0, 567 B**, `purity_calls_extra` **rc 0, 625 B**, against verbosec's `extra: [i.tier]` / `extra: [helper]`. **NO NEW WALK WAS WRITTEN, and that is the whole design**: `count_undecl_read_*` / `count_undecl_call_*` already answer *how many performed sites are ABSENT from this NameList*, so `sites(NmNil)` is every performed site (TOTAL) and `sites([q])` is every site except those matching `q` — hence `q` is EXTRA exactly when `sites([q]) == TOTAL`. One walk with the empty list per rule plus one singleton walk per declared entry; the comparison, the path semantics and the scoping all come from the existing rules unchanged, which is what stops the two directions ever disagreeing about what "the same read" means. The seventh slice in this arc whose fix was WIRING. **TWO GUARDS SUPPRESS IT, and they are the load-bearing part.** Both walks return a flat 0 for the same twelve node families PR #150 found `count_badcall_ast` stubbing (`AstOk`, `AstResErr`, `AstMatchResult`, `AstSum`, `AstCount`, `AstFold`, `AstAll`, `AstAny`, `AstMap`, `AstFilter`, `AstMinFold`, `AstMaxFold`), and `count_undecl_read_ast` additionally recognizes a read ONLY when the path root byte-equals the rule's INPUT NAME, so a rule with no `input:` block (gen0's parens dialect, `rule f(x)`) has no name to match and sees zero reads. For the MISSING direction each blindness is fail-OPEN — a read inside `sum(...)` is simply not flagged. **For the EXTRA direction the SAME blindness inverts to fail-CLOSED**: the performed site is invisible, so a CORRECTLY declared entry looks unperformed and gen0 would refuse a program verbosec accepts, the one direction this arc must never move in. So `ast_has_opaque` walks the body first and `purity_errors` skips BOTH extra checks for any rule containing one of the twelve, and `extra_reads` stands down on `in_len == 0`. Measured with the opacity guard removed, the idiomatic `out = sum(w.items, e => e.v)` with the CORRECT `reads: [w.items]` is refused at rc 1 with zero bytes. The guards are per-RULE, so the COST is stated rather than hidden: **an over-declaration inside a rule containing any of the twelve, or in a parens-dialect rule, is still undetected** — coarse, and deliberately in the direction that can only MISS a violation, never invent one. **The `in_len == 0` arm was found by measurement, not design**, and by an instrument the negative corpus structurally cannot be: nothing in `examples/` uses the parens dialect (verbosec's parser makes `input:` mandatory), so it surfaced only in the R2 fixed-point corpus, whose call-chain probe declares `reads : [x]` for `rule f(x)` — a proof that is correct under verbosec's model, where a parens param is not a let binding, while gen0's `param_to_binding` treats it as a LOCAL. The two models genuinely disagree about that dialect and the disagreement was invisible until the EXTRA direction made it fatal. Cost of the EXTRA slice: **zero emitted bytes** — verify-side, mechanically confirmed by sweeping all 151 examples with a gen0 built from the parent and a gen0 built from the patched source: **99 of the 100 accepted binaries byte-identical (size + sha256)**, the 100th being `vexprparse` itself, with the baseline-vs-baseline control coming back empty first. Acceptance stays **100/151**; gen0's own 929 rules still verify (a 929-rule proof corpus now checked in both directions — and none of them contains any of the twelve families, so **all** of them are genuinely exercised); R0/R1/R2 hold. gen0's own BINARY grew 628435 → 633194 B (+4759) and its source 1821846 → 1841259 B (+19413, four rules and two concepts, plus banners). *(Label correction, measured here: the "gen0's own source grew X → Y B" figures in the two rows above are gen0's BINARY size, not its source — 620802 B is the `--native` output at 87b0b65, whose source is 1805410 B. The series is right, the noun is wrong.)* Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_rejects_purity_over_declaration`, which carries a corrected twin per shape, asserts the spurious entry FIRST / MIDDLE / LAST (`count_extra_reads` recurses over the declared list), and pins BOTH guards — including the deliberate ACCEPT of an over-declaration inside a guarded rule, so a later "simplification" that drops a guard fails a test instead of silently rejecting valid programs. *(That deliberate ACCEPT was PAID OFF the same day — see the next paragraph. It is the clearest argument in this arc for asserting a known cost instead of commenting it: when the cost became payable, a named test failed and said so.)*

**THE TWELVE OPAQUE FAMILIES ARE NOW WALKED, WHICH CLOSES BOTH DIRECTIONS WHERE COLLECTION WORK LIVES (2026-08-14, corpus unchanged at 100).** The two paragraphs above both name the same twelve node families as a blind spot — `AstOk`, `AstResErr`, `AstMatchResult`, and the nine collection nodes `AstSum` / `AstCount` / `AstFold` / `AstAll` / `AstAny` / `AstMap` / `AstFilter` / `AstMinFold` / `AstMaxFold` — and both treat that blindness as a bounded cost. It was not bounded in the way the wording suggested. **Under-declaration was only ever closed for the paths the walk VISITED**, and those twelve were never in scope, so a read or a call performed inside a `sum` / `fold` / `map` / `Ok(...)` body was invisible to the proof check. Measured on a014fd0: `out = sum(w.items, e => e.v + w.secret)` declaring `reads: [w.items]` was **accepted at rc 0, 854 bytes**, where verbosec reports `missing: [w.secret]`; the `calls:` sibling, `out = sum(w.items, e => dbl(e))` declaring `calls: []`, was **accepted at rc 0, 853 bytes** against `missing: [dbl]`. The declared `reads:` set IS the audit surface, and the hole sat exactly where non-trivial work lives — so the set was reliable precisely for the rules that do nothing interesting. **PR #150 had already fixed this identical stub in `count_badcall_ast`** (the undefined-callee lint, same twelve arms, same *"Result tier slice 1 stub"* label) and the purity pair was simply left behind; this is the eighth slice in the arc whose fix was WIRING an existing shape into a walk that had not been taught it. Both walks now descend, node-for-node identically to `count_badcall_ast` — a split there would have the two lints disagreeing about what a body contains. **BINDER SCOPE IS THE HALF THAT CAN GO WRONG, and it fails in the opposite direction:** descending into `sum(w.items, e => e.v)` without scoping `e` reports `e.v` as an undeclared read of `w` and REFUSES A VALID PROGRAM. verbosec's `collect_expr_facts` walks each lambda body into a FRESH fact set and drops every path rooted at the binder; gen0 gets the same answer from the other side, consing the binder onto `binds` (new one-cons rule `binds_with_one`, the singleton sibling of `binds_with_binders`) BEFORE walking the body, so the existing `name_bound` guard rejects the path first. Mirrored per family exactly as verbosec scopes it: the collection expression and a fold's `init` are walked in the OUTER scope, the body under the binder; `fold` binds acc AND item; `match_result` binds ok_var over ok_body and err_var over err_body with the scrutinee outer; `Ok` / `Err` are pass-throughs binding nothing. Nesting and shadowing need no extra machinery — an inner lambda conses onto the scope the outer already extended, and a binder that shadows the input name is rejected by the same `name_bound` guard, which is verbosec's behaviour too. **`ast_has_opaque` AND ITS FOUR LIST WALKS ARE DELETED, not emptied**: with all twelve walked the opaque set is empty, so the predicate would structurally return 0 for every input and the `if opaque == 0` around both extra checks would suppress nothing — a constant-0 mechanism is exactly the *false explicitation* this project forbids by name. Removal was validated empirically before it was argued: acceptance stays 100/151 with the guard gone, which is the statement that no valid program lost its extra check to a walk that still cannot see something. What the removal BUYS is the guard's stated cost: an over-declaration inside a rule containing any of the twelve, previously undetected, is now caught (`reads: [w.items, w.tag]` over `sum(w.items, e => e.v)` moves from rc 0 / 1003 B to rc 1 / zero bytes). **The `in_len == 0` guard on `extra_reads` SURVIVES untouched** — it is a hole in the input-name matcher, not in the node walk, and nothing here closes it. Cost: **zero emitted bytes** — verify-side, mechanically confirmed by sweeping all 151 examples with a gen0 built from the parent and one built from the patched source: **150 of 151 identical by size + sha256**, the 151st being `vexprparse` itself, and no binary changed bytes while keeping its size. **NOT ONE CORPUS FILE AND NOT ONE `vexprparse` RULE TRIPPED THE CHECK**, in either direction — and because a clean sweep of a newly-strict check is exactly the shape a VACUOUS check has, that was verified rather than assumed, by a positive control on two REAL corpus files: `examples/sum_by_tag.verbose` with `target` deleted from its `reads:` (its `sum` body performs `read(target)`) and `examples/access_check.verbose` with `role` deleted, both accepted by the parent gen0 at 2881 B / 2353 B and both refused at zero bytes after. `vexprparse` itself uses none of the twelve as expressions (its `AstOk` / `AstFold` / `match_result` occurrences are variant NAMES in match arms), so its own rules are unaffected — worth knowing, because a green fixed point says nothing about a family the compiler's own source never uses. gen0's own source grew 1841259 → 1848579 B (+7320) and its binary 633194 → 635053 B (+1859); rules go 929 → 925 (one added, five deleted) and concepts 363 → 364. Peak RSS on the self-compile moved 712044 → 713668 KiB (+0.2%) against an 8M-node arena, so arena headroom is unaffected. R0/R1/R2 hold. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_purity_walk_descends_into_collection_and_result_nodes`, which enumerates **all twelve families with a corrected twin each** (they are twelve independent match arms — eleven correct and one stubbed reads exactly like twelve correct ones to any test that samples the family), both `calls` positions, and five binder-scope negatives (plain lambda, fold's two binders, match_result's per-arm binders, a NESTED lambda, and a binder SHADOWING the input name); verified to FAIL pre-change on its first cell |
| `termination.bound` | under-counts rejected (`declared bound N is less than actual operation count M`) | **FIXED** (2026-08-15, `count_ops_ast` + a `bad_bound` term in `term_errors`, corpus unchanged at 100). Was: `term_list` checked the block's SHAPE and never its number, so `examples/negative/termination_bound_short.verbose` — a 13-operation body declaring `bound : 1` — was accepted at **rc 0 with a 771-byte ELF** where verbosec reports `declared bound 1 is less than actual operation count 13`. The declared bound is the only static statement a Verbose program makes about how much work a rule does; unchecked, it is decoration, which this project forbids by name. **THIS ROW MIS-PRICED ITSELF, and that is the third time in this arc** (after `hint_overflow_inverted` and attribute PRESENCE): the sentence it used to end with — *“needs an operation counter over the parsed logic — the same missing machinery as the `overflow` row”* — put a node count in the same bucket as an abstract interpreter and deferred it as an arc. Opening the reference instead of trusting the summary of it: `count_operations` (`src/verifier.rs:4225`, 33 arms) is a **plain structural node count** — every arm `1 + sum(children)`, the four literal / identifier leaves 0, and `Field(base, _)` a pass-through that adds nothing. No interval arithmetic, no dataflow, no scoping; structurally the same walk as `count_undecl_read_ast` minus the binder threading. Four rules in gen0: `count_ops_ast` over the `Ast` group plus `count_ops_args` / `count_ops_vfields` / `count_ops_arms` over its three cons-lists. **WHAT IS COUNTED is exactly `rule.logic.value`, NOT the let bindings** — verbosec stops there, and the asymmetry matters: under-counting only misses a violation, over-counting REFUSES VALID PROGRAMS. **FOUR ARMS ARE NOT `1 + children`, because verbosec's PARSER desugars before the verifier ever runs**: `sum(c, v => b)` becomes `Fold(c, Number(0), __acc, v, Binary(Add, Ident, b))` = **2 + c + b**; `count` adds an `If(p, 1, 0)` = **3 + c + p**; and fold-form `min` / `max` become `Fold(c, Number(MAX), __acc, v, If(Binary(Lt, b, Ident), b, Ident))` — the body subtree is CLONED into both the comparison and the then-branch, so it is **3 + c + 2×b**. gen0 keeps dedicated AstSum / AstCount / AstMinFold / AstMaxFold variants, so those extra nodes are added back by hand. **Every primitive is a single generic `AstCall` in gen0 and needs no special case**, which is why the walk is short: verbosec's ~20 primitive Expr variants each cost `1 + children`, and the three asymmetric ones agree anyway — `read(cfg)` is gen0's `AstCall` with one `AstVar` arg = 1 + 0 against verbosec's CHILDLESS `Expr::Read` = 1; `fetch(c, req)` is 1 + 0 + req against `Expr::Fetch(name, req)` = 1 + req; `now_unix()` is 1 + nothing. **NO GEN0 COUNTERPART**: `Expr::FoldBytes` (gen0 has no `fold_bytes` keyword, so such a program fails to parse and is refused long before this walk — `examples/byte_classifier.verbose` is refused today for exactly that reason), and `Expr::Bool` does not exist on verbosec's side at all (`true` / `false` parse to `Expr::Ident`, and are in fact rejected as an unknown binding in expression position, so gen0's `AstBool` arm is unreachable in any program verbosec accepts — it returns 0, matching either way). gen0's own `AstErr` sentinel counts 0, so a shape gen0 cannot parse UNDER-counts: the safe direction. **THE ARM-FOR-ARM MIRROR WAS MEASURED, NOT ARGUED**: a throwaway gen0 driver summing `count_ops_ast` over every rule was diffed against verbosec's own sum of `count_operations`, file by file, across all 151 examples — **147 identical**, and the 4 that differ (`app`, `byte_classifier`, `enrich`, `order_intake`) all diverge because gen0's PARSE differs (a `use` directive, `fold_bytes`, a multi-line call arg list) and are all ALREADY REFUSED by gen0, so none can reach the check. Eleven synthetic probes covering every arm family matched exactly as well, including both fold-form min/max shapes. Cost: **zero emitted bytes** — verify-side, mechanically confirmed by sweeping all 151 examples with a gen0 built from the parent and one from the patched source: **99 of the 100 accepted binaries byte-identical (size + sha256)**, the 100th being `vexprparse` itself, with the baseline-vs-baseline control coming back empty first. Corpus acceptance stays **100/151** and the refused set is unchanged; gen0's own binary grew 639902 → 642469 B (+2567) and peak self-compile RSS 719756 → 721344 KiB (+0.22%, the same order as PR #166) against the 8M-node arena; R0/R1/R2 hold. **NOT ONE CORPUS FILE AND NOT ONE `vexprparse` RULE TRIPPED THE CHECK** — which follows from the differential (verbosec already verifies every one of them, and the counts agree) but was still verified rather than assumed, because a clean sweep of a newly-strict check has exactly the shape a VACUOUS one does: `examples/invoices.verbose` with its `bound : 1` lowered to 0 is accepted by the parent gen0 at 513 B and refused at zero bytes after. **ONE test had grown into the hole** (contrast PR #162's 55, #166's 9, #167's 15): `two_generation_gen0_reads_argv_text_input_field`'s inline probe declared `bound : 1` over `byte_at(h.data, 1) * 1000 + length(h.data)`, a 4-operation body — invalid Verbose, and verbosec rejects it with the identical diagnostic. Corrected, not accommodated. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_verifies_termination_bound_against_operation_count`, which DERIVES each probe's boundary from `verifier::count_operations` rather than hard-coding it, requires ACCEPT at `bound == count` and REFUSE at `bound == count - 1` (an off-by-one is the likeliest defect and nothing else in the suite would see it — a `<=`-vs-`<` slip still refuses the negative fixture and still passes the corpus), asserts verbosec on both sides with the refusal attributed to `termination.bound` AND to the same count, and covers 21 arm families including the fold-form min/max shapes that appear in **no file under `examples/`**. Every other row in this table is a check gen0 fails to perform against a check verbosec's verifier does perform; this one is gen0 failing to mirror an EMIT-time refusal, which is a different axis and would be closed by a different kind of walk. Grouping it with `hint_overflow_bad` under "2 remaining gaps" invites the same one-estimate-for-two-things error the `overflow` / `termination.bound` pairing made twice |

| rule named after a primitive | refused for all 36 names in `PRIMITIVE_CALL_NAMES` | refused, but only for the 18 names its own `span_is_primitive` recognizes (`byte_at`, `length`, `substring`, `concat`, `le32`, `le64`, `max`, `min`, `arena_scope`, `abort_if`, `read`, `fetch`, `band`, `bor`, `bxor`, `bnot`, `shl`, `shr`). | WHICH RULE the emitted binary runs | the caller names it — `--run <rule>`, mandatory | **FIXED** (2026-08-15, `rules_select_entry`, corpus unchanged at 100 at the default index). Was: **hardwired to rule #0**, and not by any declaration — `x86_program` lays procs out in SOURCE ORDER with the head at blob offset 0, and every entry trampoline's `call rel32` targets that offset, so which rule an emitted binary RAN was decided by which rule happened to be written first. **This is the row that corrupts the scoreboard rather than one program**, which is why it belongs above the codegen rows and not among them: see the metric note below the table. Measured over the 100 files gen0 accepts, rule #0 is a HELPER another rule calls in **37** of them — 8 `sha256_*` entering at `rotr32`, 6 `aes_*` at `aes_sbox` — so gen0's `aes_gcm` binary is the AES S-box (`0`→99, `1`→124, `16`→202, `255`→22, byte-exact FIPS-197) and contains no GCM at all. Fix: `ScanState.pos` now names the entry by 0-based declaration index, and the chosen rule is ROTATED to the front of the RuleList once, at the top of `elf_program_src`. **Selection is a list transform, not a new addressing mode**, and that is the whole reason it is three rules and not an arc: `x86_program` already lays the HEAD at offset 0 and `proc_offset` already resolves every callee BY NAME against whatever order it is handed, so moving one cell redirects the entry with no rel32 arithmetic and no second entry convention — the entry_rule_* trampoline cascade, `blob_end_off`'s size chain, and `resource_errors` / `connection_errors` / `reaction_errors`' entry-shape checks all read the same rotated list because `elf_program_src` already passes one `prog` to all of them. **THE `pos` SLOT WAS ALREADY FREE, verified before it was used**: `elf_program_src` passed a literal `pos: 0` into `begin_tokenize` and never read `s.pos` anywhere else, and `gen0 0/1/2/7/4000000 < f` produced five byte-identical ELFs. It is delivered on **argv[1] even though the source arrives on stdin**, because `x86_stdin_fields` atoi's NUMBER fields out of argv regardless of channel — which is why every gen0 invocation in this repo already writes a literal `0` there. Out of range REFUSES (`bad_entry` joins the verrs gate: rc 1, zero bytes) **and** clamps to 0; neither is redundant, because verrs is consumed by `abort_if` AFTER every let is evaluated, so without the clamp a bogus index would size a binary around `rl_head_decl`'s zero-span sentinel before the refusal fired. A NEGATIVE index never reaches either: `pos : number [0, 4000000]` is a declared range and PR #35's bounds-check exits at field-load. Index 0 returns the parsed list UNROTATED — the same arena node, not a copy — so the default is byte-for-byte the pre-selection emitter: **99 of the 100 accepted binaries are byte-identical (size + sha256), the 100th being `vexprparse` itself**, refused set unchanged, baseline-vs-baseline control empty first. gen0's own binary grew 645062 → 647180 B (+2118, three rules), rules 942 → 945, concepts unchanged at 369; peak self-compile RSS 724876 → 726484 KiB (+0.22%, same order as PR #166/#169); R0/R1/R2 hold. **Only the 17 CODE sites matter, of the "53" the file greps**: 53 counts LINES containing `rl_head_*`, of which 15 are comments, 23 are `calls:` proof entries and 7 are the definitions — 17 real invocation lines, 28 invocations, and 13 of those 17 belong to standalone unit-test drivers whose documented contract IS "the FIRST rule" (see the audit note below).
