Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_selects_the_entry_rule`; verified to FAIL pre-change |
| argv `text` input fields | the field's bytes reach the rule | **FIXED** (was: read as EMPTY) — see the input-channel note below. Kept in the table because it is the reference case for the class: a gap that is not a verification gap but a CODEGEN one, invisible to every count-based check |
| runtime input bounds (`number [min, max]`, `text [..N]`) | ENFORCED at field-load — out-of-range input `sys_exit(1)`s before the rule body runs (PR #35 for numbers, PR #146 for text; the text half was an argv-controlled stack overflow) | **FIXED** — both kinds, both channels, closing the fail-closed family PR #148 opened. Was: gen0's trampolines loaded the field and ran, so `examples/aes_sbox.verbose` (`b : number [0, 255]`) given 256 / 300 / −5 returned 22 at rc 0 where verbosec exits 1. **The cheap road predicted in this row's previous version is the one that worked**: gen0 still does not RETAIN the range (`skip_field_range` discards it, `FCons` is unchanged at 6 slots) — the checks RE-SCAN it out of the raw source at `ty_start + ty_len`, the offset every FCons already carries for every field, exactly as `entry_uses_stdin` recovers a text bound via `field_text_bound`. No AST widening, no ~77 `FCons` sites. The `[min, max]` grammar posed no problem the `[..N]` one didn't: `signed_end` / `signed_at` handle an optional leading `-`, so `[-50, 50]` is read correctly. Cost **+54 B per bounded Number field, +27 B per bounded Text field**, and nothing otherwise — mechanically checked across the corpus: of the 113 accepted files, 110 have an entry concept whose declared bounds predict the measured old→new delta EXACTLY, the other 3 moved zero bytes, and **no binary changed bytes while keeping its size**. Corpus stayed 113/151; the fixed point moved by exactly +81 B (vexprparse's own `ScanState` declares `source : text [..4194304]` and `pos : number [0, 4000000]` = 27 + 54) and R0/R1/R2 all still hold. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_enforces_declared_input_field_bounds`, which fails pre-change on the aes_sbox case |
| `byte_at(<text>, <i>)` bounds | fail-closed — `i >= length(text)` (and, via the unsigned compare, `i < 0`) `sys_exit(1)`s | **FIXED** (PR #148, +9 B per call site, K 36→45). The diagnosis in this row's previous version — "no check" — was wrong in a way worth recording: the `cmp rcx, rdx ; jae` **was already there and already correct**, so the `movzx` load was never reached with an out-of-range index (probed for disclosure: a second text field was unreachable at every offset from 0 to 4 GiB, all returning 0 at rc 0, no crash). Only the ACTION on the taken branch was wrong — a defensive `xor eax,eax ; push rax` where verbosec `sys_exit(1)`s. So it was a **silent wrong answer, never a memory-safety bug**, and "gen0 emits no bounds check" would have sent a reader looking for the wrong thing |
| `substring(<t>, <a>, <b>)` bounds | fail-closed — `b > length(t)` → `sys_exit(1)`, `a > b` → `sys_exit(1)` (the latter also catching negative `a`/`b` via unsigned reinterpretation) | **FIXED** (PR #148, +9 B per call site, K 38→47). Probed for the first time in that PR and found in exactly the same state as `byte_at`: both `ja` compares present and correct, both taking a defensive `push 0` — so an out-of-range slice silently became the EMPTY text. Measured before the fix on `length(substring(s.data, s.a, s.b))` over `"hello"`: `(0,6) (0,500) (3,1) (-5,3)` all printed `0` at rc 0 where verbosec exits 1. `(5,5)` — the empty slice at the very end — is legal in both and stayed legal |
| undefined callee under `Ok` / `Err` / `match_result` / a collection node | resolved at verify time; an unresolvable callee is a verify error | **FIXED** (corpus 113 → 110). `count_badcall_ast` — the undefined-callee lint — returned a flat `0` for twelve node families (`AstOk`, `AstResErr`, `AstMatchResult`, `AstSum` / `AstCount` / `AstFold` / `AstAll` / `AstAny` / `AstMap` / `AstFilter` / `AstMinFold` / `AstMaxFold`), labelled *"Result tier slice 1 stub"*. It was not a stub, it was **a hole with teeth**, and what gave it teeth is an ASYMMETRY: gen0's `span_is_primitive` knows 18 names, verbosec's `PRIMITIVE_CALL_NAMES` has 36. `parse_int` and `now_unix` are legal primitives to verbosec and UNDEFINED CALLEES to gen0 — i.e. programs verbosec **accepts** that gen0 must refuse. Under one of the twelve unwalked nodes the lint could not see the call, gen0 accepted, and the emitter — with no proc address to call — emitted **`call <end-of-code>`, which is the first byte of the embedded SOURCE BLOB.** Measured on `examples/iso_date.verbose`: three `call 0x4005a6` (the three `parse_int(substring(i.s, …))` under `Ok(DateParts { … })`) and a **general protection fault at 0x4005ab**, executing the `o` of `@verbose` as `outsd`, a privileged I/O instruction (`dmesg`: `general protection fault ip:4005ab … in iso_g[400000+1000]`). Two more files carried the identical `call <blob>` signature: `sliding_count` (`now_unix()` under `count(...)`) and `threshold_sum` (`parse_int(...)` under `sum(...)`). Fix: both `count_badcall_ast` and its span twin `badcall_span_ast` now descend into all twelve (the two walks must agree node-for-node, or gen0 refuses and then reports a blank callee). Cost: exactly those 3 files, now rc 1 with **zero bytes** written; the other 110 binaries are byte-identical bar `vexprparse` itself. **Deliberately NOT extended** to `count_undef_ast` (undefined variable) or `count_badarity_ast` (arity), which stub the same twelve: those two only fire on programs verbosec ITSELF rejects, so no corpus file can reach them — the asymmetry above is the entire reason this one was live. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_never_emits_a_binary_that_crashes_on_iso_date`, which asserts the weaker, future-proof invariant (**refuse with zero bytes, OR emit something that runs and matches verbosec** — never the third outcome that shipped: exit 0, a 2546-byte ELF, and a SIGSEGV on the first plausible input) |
| text-producing `Result` values | `Result(text, text)` compiles natively: Ok(text) → stdout rc 0, Err(text) → stderr rc 1; concat payloads materialize through the Phase 1B/2F buffer machinery | **FIXED for span payloads / REFUSED for fresh text** (corpus 110 → 105). Was BOTH failure classes at once: the Result trampoline's only finished payload printer was the number itoa, so `tier.verbose`'s Ok arm printed the packed (start, len) span as the decimal `2864743186439` at rc 0 — a SILENT WRONG ANSWER — while every payload built from FRESH text (concat / texty-rule call) hit `x86_node`'s int3 placeholder: SIGTRAP, measured on five entries (tier, purchase, gate_result, fullname, ledger_line). Now: `Result(text, ...)` entries get a text-Ok trampoline (span write, 189 B vs 210 B, gated by `entry_result_ok_texty` in BOTH the size and emit walks), and fresh text in a value-lowered ENTRY position refuses at verify time (exit 1, zero bytes) via `entry_freshtext`. Fresh-text payloads stay refused until a materialization arc lands (runtime buffer + itoa-to-memory + span rebasing — packed spans cannot point at fresh bytes). Of the two sibling families this slice left open, the RECORD one is closed by the row below; the STREAM one (text FIELDS itoa'd in `greeting_line` / `compose`) is still open. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_emits_text_result_values_or_refuses`. **The ENTRY-ONLY scope of the fresh-text refusal was widened to the entry's TRANSITIVE callees on 2026-08-16 (PR #171)** — see the row's own residual note below the table; `purchase::discounted_purchase` was the corpus file that made it reachable, and it moved from rc 0 / 2563 B with a SIGTRAPping Err arm to rc 1 / zero bytes |
| record-output entries (`output: <user concept>`) | the record is serialized to `{"field":value,...}` on stdout, one JSON object per record (Phase 2C) | **FIXED for bare records / REFUSED for sum types** (corpus 105 → 100). The RECORD sibling of the row above, and the same class — a pointer-ish value rendered as a number at rc 0. A rule whose declared output is a user concept returns an ARENA NODE INDEX in rax (`x86_node`'s AstVariant arm stores the record at `r15 + r14*esize` and hands back `r14`), and no `entry_rule_*` branch claimed it, so the 101-byte number trampoline **itoa'd the index**: `examples/greeting.verbose::make_report` on argv `Alice 50000` printed `1` at rc 0 where verbosec prints `{"name":"Alice","bonus":5000}` — rc 0, plausible output, no diagnostic. Same on `classify`. IMPLEMENTED for a bare RECORD (zero variants, >0 fields, every field number or text): gen0 already OWNED the serializer — `x86_json_record` / `x86_json_fields`, written for the map/filter record loops — so the entry trampoline becomes `call entry ; push rax ; <that serializer> ; exit(0)` = **67 + `json_fields_size` bytes**, the first trampoline variant whose size is not a constant. Its NUMBER field blocks call the shared itoa proc at blob-origin −86, which a record body (an `AstVariant`, hence NOT texty) would never order — so `prog_needs_itoa` ORs the record entry into `prog_any_texty`, and BOTH walks (`blob_end_off`'s constant chain, `elf_program_src`'s `anytx`) call that one rule. REFUSED (`entry_badrecord` joins the `verrs` gate, exit 1, zero bytes) for a SUM-TYPE entry or a record with a non-scalar field: tag dispatch / nested-node recursion is a real emit arc, not this slice. The five dropped files were all measured broken — `sum_chain` / `print_chain` / `label_tree` printed a DIFFERENT index from verbosec's own binary (10 vs 6 at seed 3), and `token_classify` / `token_label` printed one where verbosec **refuses to emit at all**. Byte cost is exactly predicted: greeting +1390 (tramp 101→401, itoa +86, source blob +1004 now that `uses_text` is on), classify +386 (tramp +300, itoa +86; its literals already embedded the source). 97 of the 100 surviving binaries are byte-identical; the other two are the fixed ones, plus `vexprparse` itself. **The `rd_out_ty_len == 0` guard that leads `entry_out_named` is load-bearing**, not decoration: a rule declared with no `output:` block at all has an EMPTY out_ty span, and `concepts_append_result` appends a synthetic concept whose `name_len` is ALSO 0 — so `find_concept_index` byte-matched the empty query against it and a `match_result` rule printed `{"":0}`. Caught by `result_tier_slice2_compiles_match_result_to_machine_code`, which is the reason that unit test exists. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_emits_record_output_entries_or_refuses` |
| text fields in STREAMED positions (`output: text` bodies) | a text field in a `concat(...)` is written as its bytes | **FIXED** (corpus unchanged at 100). Called "the THIRD and LAST member" of the "pointer-ish value rendered as a number at rc 0" family below — **it was neither last nor third-of-three**: `read()` in three output positions followed on 2026-08-10, and a text VARIANT BINDER in this same walk followed on 2026-08-16 (PR #171, the row below this one). The sentence is left standing as written because the repetition is the lesson. The THIRD member, then, after the Result payload and the record entry above. `x86_stream_node` is the WRITER-mode walk, and its `AstField` arm routed EVERY field through the shared itoa proc with no test for the field's declared type — so a TEXT field printed its packed (start, len) pair as a decimal. Measured on `examples/greeting_line.verbose` (argv `Ada 36`): gen0 `Hello 2287825359413968899, age 36` at rc 0 vs verbosec `Hello Ada, age 36`; `0x1fbffd0b_00000003` decodes to start `0x1fbffd0b`, len 3 == `len("Ada")`. `examples/compose.verbose::display_name` printed TWO spans. The fix is WIRING, not machinery — the third slice running to that same conclusion: `x86_fold_arg`, the fold-loop sibling emitter, ALREADY had the exact lowering for an element text field, so the arm becomes `if ast_field_is_text(...) then <that 36-byte write> else <the 6-byte itoa call>`, with `code_size_stream_node`'s `AstField` arm moved identically (the two-walk drift edge — a split there desyncs every downstream offset). **ONE BASE SERVES BOTH TEXT ORIGINS, and this is the part that looks false**: the Result slice's spans pointed into the embedded SOURCE BLOB, while these point into the argv `MAP_FIXED` region at `0x20000000`, nowhere near the blob — but `x86_argv_marshal` packs `(0x20000000 - src_base) + bump`, pre-biasing by `-src_base` at emit time, so `src_base + start` resolves BOTH and an origin test would be a bug. Verified three ways on the emitted binary: the blob begins at file offset `0x2f5` with `@verbose 0.1.0` (so `src_base` = `0x400000 + 757` = `0x4002f5`); the emitted `movabs rsi, 0x400574` for the `"Hello "` literal is `src_base + 639` and `src.find(b"Hello ") == 639`; and `0x4002f5 + 0x1fbffd0b == 0x20000000` exactly. **No refusal half** — unlike the Result and record slices there is no representation this arm cannot serve, since a text field is always a packed span whatever its origin, which is why the corpus does not move. Cost is exactly `(36 - 6)` B per streamed text field, mechanically confirmed: of the 100 accepted binaries EXACTLY TWO changed — `greeting_line` 1537 → 1567 (+30, one field) and `compose` 2674 → 2734 (+60, two fields) — the other 98 byte-identical by sha256, and no binary changed bytes while keeping its size. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_emits_streamed_text_fields_correctly` |
| text VARIANT BINDERS in streamed positions (`match c(s): Ident(label) => …`) | a text payload bound by a match arm is written as its bytes | **FIXED** (2026-08-16, PR #171, corpus unchanged at 100 at the default entry). The FIFTH member of the itoa-a-pointer family, in the same `x86_stream_node` walk as the FIELD row above, one binder-kind further in: `examples/token_label.verbose::label_of` on argv `0 hello` printed `id:2287823529757900805` against verbosec's `id:hello` (`0x1fbffb61_00000005`, low 32 bits == `len("hello")`). **It had been emittable since Phase A slice 4.2 and was never run** — the entry hardwire (PR #170's row above) meant `token_label` entered at `classify`, a sum-type output the record trampoline refuses, so no gate in the repo ever executed `label_of`. Root cause: the arm binders `lets_append_binders` conses onto the `lets` list carry a DUMMY `AstNum(0)` value, so `let_index` resolved the NAME and nothing downstream could recover the TYPE — the `AstVar` branch took the 6-byte itoa call for text and number alike. Fix: `lets_append_typed_binders` (+ `typed_binder_values`) looks the payload types up through the already-existing `arm_payload_by_name` and marks a text payload's dummy `AstStr`; `let_marks_textspan` is the ONE predicate both walks call (`code_size_stream_node` counting, `x86_stream_node` emitting — the two-pass drift edge), dispatching to the same 36-byte span write the `AstField` text branch owned. **The eleventh consecutive slice in this arc whose fix was WIRING an emitter that already existed.** Cost exactly `36 - 6` = **+30 B per streamed text-binder site**, and the three walks that had to move together are `stream_size_arms` / `x86_stream_dispatch` / `x86_stream_arms` (a split there desyncs every downstream offset). **Confined to the STREAMING walk** — the value-lowered `Result` Ok / `Result` Err / record-field positions were measured byte-identical across the change and already correct, which is what scopes the fix; see footnote ⁷ of the text-position matrix, where those three turn out to be cells verbosec's own native emitter refuses. Zero corpus movement at rule #0 (`token_label` is refused there), so non-vacuity is carried by the index-1 measurements: 3027 → 3057 B and byte-exact on all three arms afterwards. Pinned by probe 1b of `two_generation_gen0_selects_the_entry_rule`, whose as-is assertion of the defect flipped to full stdout + exit-code agreement; verified to FAIL pre-change |
| fresh text in a REACHABLE CALLEE (`match_result(callee(i), …)` where the callee's arm builds `concat(...)`) | verbosec inlines the callee and materializes the concat through the Phase 1B/2F buffer machinery — it ACCEPTS, and the binary is correct | **REFUSED, deliberately** (2026-08-16, PR #171, corpus unchanged at 100 at the default entry). Not a verifier gap and not a defect in the reference — **the one `INVERSE_CAPABILITY` entry in the negative corpus**: the program is VALID, verbosec is right to accept it, and gen0 cannot express it, because a gen0 text value is a packed `(start, len)` span into the embedded source blob and fresh `concat` output sits at no addressable offset. PR #151 refused exactly this in the ENTRY rule and NAMED the callee position as its residual ("no current corpus file has that shape"); the qualifier that expired is *at rule #0*, and PR #170's entry selection produced one immediately — `purchase::discounted_purchase` at index 1 compiled to **rc 0 / 2563 B whose Err arm exits 133 (SIGTRAP) with no output**, where verbosec writes `customer age 12 is under 18` and exits 1. Now **rc 1, zero bytes**. `entry_freshtext` grows a transitive walk (`reach_visit` / `reach_calls` / `nonentry_offenders` / `reached_offenders`) with three load-bearing properties: the callee set is the DECLARED `calls:` list (sound only because purity is checked in both directions and descends into every node family — the `layer_list` dependency restated, so weakening either purity direction silently weakens this); the visited list is what makes a call CYCLE terminate; and the DFS is offender-GATED, so a program with no offender anywhere pays one linear sweep and never builds a visited list — which is why the self-compile is unaffected. **Scope stays REACHABLE, never PRESENT**, preserving PR #151's `enrich` / `order_intake` exemption; no corpus file exercises that distinction, so probe 4 of the pin exists to. Lifting this means the fresh-text materialization arc (runtime buffer + itoa-to-memory + span rebasing), not a slice. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_refuses_reachable_match_result_fresh_text` (refusal + zero bytes, a corrected literal-Err twin that must still ACCEPT and run correctly, verbosec must still ACCEPT, and reachable-not-present + cycle termination); verified to FAIL pre-change |
| `byte_at` / `length` over a DECLARED CONSTANT BYTE TABLE (`b"..."`) | the literal's decoded bytes are embedded inline via jmp-over-data (the `E9` rel32 form for a table over 127 bytes) and indexed with a compile-time-exact length; `examples/aes_sbox.verbose` is 833 B and byte-exact FIPS-197 | **REFUSED, deliberately** (2026-08-17, `bytelit_operands`, corpus 100 → 99). The second `INVERSE_CAPABILITY` entry, and the first one a LANGUAGE FEATURE created rather than an invocation change exposing. Not a verifier gap and not a defect in the reference: the program is VALID, verbosec accepts it and its binary is correct. gen0 cannot express it, for the same representational reason as the fresh-text row — **a gen0 text value is a packed `(start, len)` span into the embedded SOURCE BLOB, and the blob holds the raw four characters `\x63`, not the decoded byte `0x63`.** A real inline data block would sit BELOW `src_base`, i.e. at a negative offset the packing cannot carry. gen0 DOES own a `\xNN` decoder (`bytelit_unit_byte`, which has handled `\x` → 4-source-chars-to-one-byte since the byte-literal slice) but it feeds only the EFFECT paths — reaction content, service body, `fetch` request — which emit a real data block instead of a span. **The measured cost of NOT refusing is the reason this is a slice and not a note**: gen0 ACCEPTED the rewritten `aes_sbox.verbose` at rc 0 and emitted 2956 bytes that SIGTRAPped (int3, rc −5) on EVERY valid input 0..255, against verbosec's byte-exact 833 B; and gen0 had emitted a WORKING 19418-byte binary for the same file's previous 254-branch `if/else` spelling, so the file regressed from correct to trapping the moment the feature landed. That is the accept-what-you-cannot-emit class, and a compiler must never emit a binary that traps on a reachable arm. **The corpus figure moves 100 → 99 and the movement is honest in the same direction PR #171's 106 → 105 was**: the 100 would have been counting an ELF that existed, not a program that worked. Lifting the refusal means a post-blob data region wired through `blob_end_off`'s size chain so a decoded table lives at a POSITIVE offset from `src_base` — an arc, not a slice. Pinned by the `byte_table_indexed` fixture in `examples/negative/` (a valid program verbosec compiles and runs correctly, which is what makes it a CAPABILITY entry rather than a REFERENCE_GAP). **THE SAME REFUSAL COST SIX MORE FILES ON 2026-08-17 (corpus 99 → 93), AND THE SECOND DROP READS DIFFERENTLY FROM THE FIRST — which is the reason it is written here rather than folded into the sentence above.** The other six AES-family files each carried their OWN copy of the same 254-branch S-box chain, and all six adopted the declared table, so all six now hit this same gate. But the argument that made the first drop free does NOT transfer: `aes_sbox`'s pre-refusal binary TRAPPED on every input, whereas these six pre-change binaries all **worked** — measured with the same gen0 against the pre-change sources, `aes_gcm` emitted 672023 bytes answering 99 / 124 / 202 / 22 for 0 / 1 / 16 / 255, and the other five answered identically at 53884 / 30295 / 72215 / 73207 / 136045 B. So something real was given up, and the honest accounting is that **it was one program counted six times**: rule #0 in every one of these files IS `aes_sbox`, so each of those six "acceptances" compiled the shared leaf helper and none compiled the file's subject — the exact over-claim the metric note below documents. No NEW class of program became uncompilable (the capability gap is the one already recorded in this row), and no file lost a binary that did anything the `aes_sbox` binary did not already do. Both halves of the figure moved by exactly six: **99 → 93 at rule #0, 104 → 98 at the declared entry** |
| `bool` output formatting AND exit code | a `bool`-declared output prints `true` / `false` + `\n` and exits 1 if any `false` was printed (all seven emitters share `emit_bool_arms`) | **FIXED — format 2026-08-08, exit code 2026-08-09** (corpus unchanged at 100). The mildest member of the divergence set and the only one that was never a safety or a silent-wrong-answer bug — `1` is a *truthful* rendering of the 0/1 in rax, just not verbosec's. A bool-declared entry was claimed by no `entry_rule_*` branch, fell through to the 101-byte NUMBER trampoline and got itoa'd: `examples/invoices.verbose::important_invoice` printed `1` / `0` where verbosec prints `true` / `false`. Fixed by `entry_rule_bool` — the DECLARED type, not the runtime value: `parse_fields` already ran `type_code_of_span` over the output field's type-name token and that rule maps `bool`, and only `bool`, to code 1, so the whole predicate is `rd_out_ty(entry) == 1` with no re-scan of the raw source (unlike `entry_result_ok_texty`, whose payload type was never captured). Cost is a **79-byte** trampoline (call 5 + a 74-byte two-arm write: `test rax,rax` / `jz` / `movabs rax, "true\n"` / `push` / `mov rdx,5` / `jmp` / the `"false\n"` twin / shared `write(1, rsp, rdx)` + `exit(0)`) against the 101-byte itoa variant, so every bool file SHRANK by exactly 22 B — mechanically confirmed: of the 100 accepted binaries EXACTLY TEN moved, all by −22 (`access_check` `alert` `allowlist` `bonus` `clients` `collections` `invoices` `layers` `logs` `priv_failure`), the other 89 byte-identical by sha256, and no binary changed bytes while keeping its size. The literals ride as imm64s and land little-endian on the stack, so `[rsp]` is `t`/`f` and the single write emits exactly 5 or 6 bytes; mirroring the OUTPUT rather than verbosec's INSTRUCTION ENCODING (jmp-over-data + rip-relative `lea`) is the contract, since gen0's bytes are only ever compared to gen1's. **THE EXIT CODE followed on 2026-08-09**, once verbosec had a single contract to match (see the note below): the trampoline grew a `rbx` flag — `xor ebx,ebx` before the test, `mov bl,1` on the false arm, `mov rdi,rbx` at exit — taking it **79 → 83 bytes**, so the same ten files each moved **+4** and the other 90 stayed byte-identical. `rbx` is safe *here* only because it is set after `call entry` has returned and nothing but `write` runs before the exit; it is NOT free in general (the reaction trampoline uses it as the `open()` fd, the service accept loop as the parsed-request pointer). Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_formats_bool_outputs_as_true_false`, which asserts the bytes, an explicit anti-assertion that the output is not a bare integer, full rc agreement on both arms, and `factorial::fact` as the guard that a NUMBER entry still itoa's |
| `and` / `or` evaluation order | SHORT-CIRCUIT — the RHS is not evaluated when the LHS decides the result | **FIXED** (PR #148, K 20→26 for `and`, 20→25 for `or`). Listed here because it is the reason the two rows above are one PR and not three. gen0's stack-machine `AstBin` emitted both operands then combined, i.e. EAGER — and that was observationally invisible for as long as the bounds checks were defensive, since eager-plus-defensive computes the same boolean as short-circuit in every case (`false and (0 == 45)` is `false` either way). Making the bounds fail-closed converts the same eagerness into a spurious abort: the idiom CLAUDE.md documents as safe, `length(s) > N and byte_at(s, N) == c`, aborts on a short string. Measured before the fix: `if s.n == 1 or byte_at(s.data, 99) == 65 then 7 else 9` with `n=1` over `"short"` → verbosec 7 at rc 0, gen0 nothing at rc 1. **Shipping either bounds fix without this one would have shipped an unexploded landmine**, and shipping `byte_at` without it broke gen0's own front end (`count_rules` on `"1 + 2"`) |
| text `==` / `!=` | byte comparison — `"ab" == "ab"` is true whatever the two operands' origins | **FIXED** (2026-08-08, K 12→65 for a span-vs-span compare, corpus unchanged at 100). Was: **`==` with TEXT operands returned `false` unconditionally.** gen0's `AstBin` had one comparison lowering, `pop rcx ; pop rax ; cmp rax, rcx ; sete al`, and a gen0 text value is a packed `(start, len)` span — so it compared two *addresses*, not two strings. Two spans are integer-equal only when they name the same bytes at the same offset, and a field span (argv, marshalled into the fixed region and pre-biased by `0x20000000 - src_base`) can never equal a literal span (an offset into the embedded source blob). Objdumped on a minimal `r.tag == "ab"`: `mov rax,[rax+8]` (the field's span) then `movabs rax, 0x13700000002` (the literal's span, start 0x137 len 2) then `cmp rax, rcx` — never equal, `false` at rc 0, no diagnostic. Every operand shape failed identically: literal RHS, `read(<resource>)` RHS, and either operand order. Fix: two new rules in the "text predicates" cluster — `ast_span_text` (is this node a resolvable span: literal / text field / text param / `read` / `substring`) and `bin_text_mode`, the ONE rule both walks call (`code_size_node` for the count, `x86_node` for the bytes, the two-pass drift edge) returning 0 = integer compare, 1 = the 65-byte packed-span byte compare (length first, then a `sil` byte loop over `src_base + start`, clobbering only rax/rcx/rdx/rsi/rdi — never the arena registers), 2 = a text compare with NO span lowering (a `concat` / texty-call operand, or one text operand against a non-span one), which emits `int3` rather than an answer that cannot be justified. Cost is exactly **+53 B per span-vs-span `==` site** and nothing otherwise: 9 corpus binaries moved, each by 53 × its site count (access_check/allowlist/clients/layers/showcase/sum_by_tag +53, alert +106, config +212, priv_failure +265), the other 90 are byte-identical. Ordering comparisons on text (`<`, `>`) are NOT part of this: verbosec's native refuses them and so does gen0 (rc 1, zero bytes), so there is no silent path left. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_evaluates_text_equality` |
| text values in OUTPUT positions (`read()` direct, `read()`/`substring` in a fold body, a bare text FIELD as the whole output) | the bytes are written | **FIXED** (2026-08-10, corpus unchanged at 100). Three positions, three causes, one symptom — a packed `(start, len)` span printed as a decimal at rc 0. `examples/read_config.verbose::load_config` (`out = read(config)`) printed `2305840045686259719` = `0x1ffffd4e_00000007`, len 7 == `len("CFGDATA")`, where verbosec prints `CFGDATA`; `examples/sep_roster.verbose::sep_line` printed the separator's span mid-line; and a bare `out = i.s` printed `2287825939234553858` (len 2 == `len("ab")`). **The distinguishing observation is the whole diagnosis**: `concat("[", read(r), "]")` WORKED, and had all along — a concat is texty, so the rule routes to `x86_stream_node`, whose `span_is_read` arm already did the correct dereference-and-write. It is the DIRECT form, where the text value IS the whole output, that never reached that walk. Causes and fixes: (1) `ast_is_texty`'s AstCall arm listed `span_is_fetch` but **not** `span_is_read` — a one-line asymmetry between two primitives that produce the identical runtime-populated span, so a direct read was classified non-texty and value-lowered into the 101-byte NUMBER trampoline (objdumped: the proc is `movabs rax, <slot> ; mov rax,[rax] ; push ; pop ; ret`, then `div rcx` by 10 in the trampoline). Adding the branch also fixes a helper rule whose body is a direct read being CALLED from a concat, since `callee_is_texty` reads the same predicate. (2) `x86_fold_arg` / `fold_size_arg` gated their packed-span write on `ast_field_is_text`, so a `read` in a fold body's concat fell to the number-itoa branch — widened both to `ast_span_text`, the predicate written for `bin_text_mode` that already covered literal / text field / text param / `read` / `substring`. (3) a bare text FIELD as the entire output could not be fixed in the classifier at all: deciding "is this field text-typed" needs the params + concept table and `TextyState` carries only `(node, src, prog)`; widening it would ripple through `callee_is_texty` / `rule_is_texty` / `prog_any_texty` into `blob_end_off`'s size chain, and `callee_is_texty` resolves a DIFFERENT rule than the caller so "which params" stops having one answer. Fixed at the trampoline instead via `entry_rule_textspan`, gating on the DECLARED output type (`rd_out_ty == 2`) exactly as `entry_rule_bool` gates on `== 1`, placed LAST in both the `blob_end_off` and `elf_program_src` cascades immediately before the 101 fallback. Its 80 B = call 5 + the Result trampoline's fd-1 Ok block copied VERBATIM (75 B) — the fourth slice in this family whose fix was WIRING an emitter that already existed. **No blob forcing**, unlike the record entry which had to OR into `prog_needs_itoa`: the bytes a span points at here live in the argv `MAP_FIXED` region or a resource buffer, never in the source blob, and `x86_argv_marshal` pre-biases by `(0x20000000 - src_base)` so `src_base + start` resolves without it; a LITERAL body — the one shape that would need the blob — is `AstStr`, hence texty, hence claimed by `entry_rule_texty` several branches earlier. Cost: exactly 2 corpus binaries moved, `read_config` 1974 → 2042 (+68) and `sep_roster` 3147 → 3173 (+26, exactly `36 - 10`, one fold arg crossing from the itoa branch to the span-write branch), the other 97 byte-identical by sha256, no binary changed bytes while keeping its size, and `entry_rule_textspan` moved **zero** corpus files (no `examples/` file has that shape — it was found by enumerating positions, not by the differential). Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_emits_a_direct_read_text_output`, which pins the DIRECT form specifically because the concat form was already green |
| a record-returning callee (slice agg-1) | the aggregate crosses the call boundary through a caller-allocated destination in `rsi`; `examples/aggregate_pair.verbose::total` compiles to 793 B and prints `9007` for argv `7 9` | **OPEN, measured, and it is the accept-what-you-cannot-emit class — NOT an `INVERSE_CAPABILITY`** (2026-08-19). At the DECLARED entry (index 1, `total`) gen0 emits **rc 0, an 825-byte ELF that exits 133 (SIGTRAP) with no output**. It does not refuse; it writes a binary that traps on the one input shape the program has. At rule #0 (`swap2`) gen0 agrees with verbosec exactly — 2063 B printing `{"x":9,"y":7}` — which is why `EXPECTED_ACCEPTED` moves 93 → 94 while the file's SUBJECT traps: the metric trap this file documents at length, restated in the constant's own comment. **This slice SURFACED the defect rather than causing it**: before agg-1, verbosec refused `total` too, so gen0's trapping binary had no reference to be wrong against — the same "a limitation is only unreachable RELATIVE TO HOW YOU INVOKE THE COMPILER" lesson PR #171 recorded, seen from the other side (here it is the REFERENCE growing a capability, not the invocation changing). Closing it is a gen0 slice: `vexprparse` needs a refusal for a rule whose `output:` is a plain record concept, in the shape of `entry_badrecord`'s sum-type arm. No negative-corpus fixture is warranted — that directory holds programs at least one compiler REFUSES, and gen0 refuses nothing here. **CONFIRMED ON A SECOND FILE by slice agg-2a (2026-08-21)**, which is worth stating because one file is an anecdote: `examples/aggregate_recurse.verbose` at its declared entry (index 1, `fib`) gives the identical signature — **rc 0, a 1200-byte ELF that exits 133 (SIGTRAP) with no output**, where verbosec emits 1029 bytes printing `55089`. **The rule-#0 half went the OTHER way this time, and that half is new**: at index 0 (`fib_pair`, the recursive record-returning rule) gen0 ACCEPTS at 3750 B and prints `{"prev":55,"curr":89}` — the CORRECT answer — while **verbosec REFUSES that entry** (agg-2a's record-output-ENTRY refusal, `slice agg-2c`). So in that one cell gen0 is genuinely more capable: its record trampoline serializes the arena node index its own recursive emit already produces, whereas verbosec's `emit_self_recursive_program` has no record arm in its result dispatch and refuses rather than itoa a pointer. That is the already-tracked `input_type_unsupported` class (gen0 mirrors none of verbosec's EMIT-time refusal surface) rather than a new row, and it is a GAP not an INVERSE — the discriminator being "which compiler would you change to make the two agree?", and the answer here is verbosec (add the result arm, `slice agg-2c`). **THE RULE-#0 HALF CLOSED 2026-08-28 (slice agg-2c): verbosec grew the result arm, and the two compilers now AGREE on that cell** — `fib_pair` as entry compiles to 1157 B printing byte-for-byte what gen0's 3750 B binary prints. The DECLARED-entry half stays open and gained a THIRD file: `examples/aggregate_emit.verbose` at index 1 (`driver`) is gen0 rc 0 / a 3859-byte ELF that SIGTRAPs with no output, where verbosec emits 1270 B printing `{"prev":55,"curr":89}`. Closing that half is still the same gen0 slice this row already names |
| service `log:` block — WHICH requests earn a line | the block runs between the handler and the response write, so ONLY a request that parsed and was answered produces a line | **FIXED for the three parsing handler shapes / RESIDUAL on the constant-response one** (2026-08-10, corpus unchanged at 100, and the only corpus binary whose bytes move is `vexprparse` itself). Not an itoa-a-pointer bug — the logged BYTES were always right. The defect was WHERE the block sat. gen0 keyed placement on the log CONTENT's shape: a FIELD log went post-field-select (its spans do not exist before the parse), a STATIC-literal log stayed PRE-READ at tramp+158 where slice 5a put it, on a byte-identity argument, and slice 5b's own banner recorded the consequence as intended behaviour — *"keeps logging per ACCEPTED CONNECTION"*. Measured against verbosec over a good / malformed / good conversation on the same source: gen0 wrote **`hit\nhit\nhit\n`** where verbosec wrote **`hit\nhit\n`**, and the extra line belongs to a connection **gen0's own accept loop had already dropped with an empty response** (`wire[1]` empty in both compilers). An audit file containing a request the server refused to serve is a false record in the one artifact an Article 12 chain rests on — the same *shape* of harm as the silent-wrong-answer family (a plausible output, rc 0, no diagnostic), by a different mechanism: control-flow placement, not value rendering. Fix is three lines and, once again, WIRING an emitter that already existed: `x86_service_tramp`'s two slots collapse to one — the pre-read site is deleted from the three parsing branches, the post-parse site emits `x86_svc_log_block` unconditionally, and `jlog` becomes `logsz` instead of `if hasf == 1 then logsz else 0` (the term already existed for exactly this; `hasf` and its `svc_log_has_field` call drop out of the rule). Both parse-fail `je`s then jump over the block. Cost has the signature of a pure placement change and is worth reading as the proof: the three static-log ELFs on parsing shapes keep their EXACT sizes (1006 / 1503 / 1448) with new bytes — only the two `je` rel32s moved — while every no-log ELF, both field-log ELFs, and the S1 static-log ELF are byte-identical; corpus-wide, 99 of 100 binaries unchanged by sha256. **RESIDUAL, named**: the S1 constant-response branch emits no parse at all (it reads and answers), so it has no post-parse slot and still logs per accepted connection. verbosec diverges there for a reason of its own — a `log:` block routes S1 to its PARSING emitter, so verbosec DROPS the malformed request that its own no-log S1 build would answer. Measured and asserted as-is in the pin. Closing it means giving gen0's S1 a conditional parse: its own slice. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_service_and_reaction_output_positions`, which fails pre-change on exactly the 3-vs-2 line count |

**THE NEGATIVE CORPUS: the surface every other measurement in this repo is blind to (2026-08-10).** Every gate above feeds **valid** programs. `two_generation_corpus_acceptance_sweep` counts how many gen0 accepts; the differential harness compares OUTPUT for programs both compilers accept; the fixed point proves gen0 reproduces itself. **None of them can see a verifier that is too permissive** — a check gen0 simply does not perform is invisible to a suite made entirely of programs that pass it, and acceptance going *up* even reads as progress. The gaps in the table above were therefore each found by hand, one at a time, and nothing measured the set.

`examples/negative/` now holds 45 small programs that at least one of the two compilers refuses — 43 deliberately INVALID ones isolating a thing verbosec refuses, plus TWO VALID ones isolating things gen0 cannot build (see the INVERSE split below) — swept by `two_generation_negative_corpus_sweep` (`src/native.rs`; `two_generation` prefix, so the existing self-hosting CI job picks it up). It asserts **exact SETS, not counts** — one fix and one regression cannot cancel out — and verbosec's verdict is taken over its FULL pipeline (lex → parse → verify → native emit), because that is the pipeline gen0 implements: gen0 has no verify-only mode.

First measurement, on ba2b6ff: **5 PASS, 15 GAP, 1 INVERSE**. Two gaps were closed in the same commit, leaving **7 / 13 / 1**; the two `*_missing_partial` fixtures added 2026-08-11 take it to **9 / 13 / 1** over 23 fixtures. The twelve fixtures added 2026-08-12 complete the attribute-PRESENCE matrix and `attr_pres_errors` closes all fifteen of its cells at once: 24 / 10 / 1 over 35 fixtures. The INVERSE row closed on 2026-08-13 when verbosec grew the rule-call arity check it had never had, taking it to 25 / 10 / 0; `hint_errors` closed three of the four `hints:` cells on 2026-08-14 and `extra_reads` / `extra_calls` closed the two `purity_*_extra` cells the same day, and the three `*_in_fold` fixtures added the same day — measuring a POSITION rather than a declaration — all land as PASS in the commit that adds them, because the purity walks now descend into the twelve node families they stubbed. The three `proofs_missing*` fixtures added the same day close the mandatory-`proofs:` row and likewise land as PASS in the commit that adds them (`proofs_errors`, a second TOKEN walk). `termination_bound_short` closed on 2026-08-15 (`count_ops_ast`, a plain structural node walk — see the row above for why that row's own description of itself is the interesting part), and the two `layer_*` fixtures closed the same day (`layer_list`, a source-line walk keyed off `rd_name_start` joined to the declared `calls:` list). Standing measurement: **41 PASS / 2 GAP / 0 INVERSE_REFERENCE_GAP / 2 INVERSE_CAPABILITY over 45 fixtures** — the 42nd, `match_freshtext_in_callee`, added 2026-08-16, and the reason the single `INVERSE` constant became two (below); the 44th, `text_in_arithmetic`, added 2026-08-20 alongside the verifier's operand check, and a PASS in the commit that adds it — gen0 refuses it too (measured: rc 1, zero bytes, against a `hint_overflow_bad` control accepted at 580 B), which is the sweep's exit-status-only agreement and not a claim that gen0 refuses it for the same reason; the 43rd, `byte_table_indexed`, added 2026-08-17, and the first `INVERSE_CAPABILITY` a LANGUAGE FEATURE created rather than an invocation change exposing — which is worth its own line, because it means the capability bucket is not a fixed backlog that only shrinks. Adding a primitive can enlarge it, and the only way to find out is to compile the corpus with gen0 before shipping: `byte_at(b"...", i)` turned `examples/aes_sbox.verbose` from a file gen0 emitted CORRECTLY into one it emitted as a binary that SIGTRAPped on every input, and no verbosec-side gate could have seen that. **And the cost of a capability gap is paid per ADOPTER, not once at the fixture** — a second thing that only became visible by re-sweeping. The six other AES-family files adopted the same declared table one day later and all six left the accepted set for the same reason (99 → 93), without a single new fixture being warranted, because they are all the one shape `byte_table_indexed` already isolates. So the fixture count is the right measure of how many DISTINCT things gen0 cannot do, and a useless measure of how much of the corpus that costs; those are different questions and the sweep answers only the second. Those `proofs_missing*` three are worth reading as a FIFTH lesson about the instrument, and it is about the fixture's BODY rather than its declaration: of the three shapes verbosec refuses, only ONE was visibly broken, and which one depended entirely on what the rule body happened to do. With `ok = i.amount` the no-`proofs:` and no-`purity:` shapes came back rc 1 — the right answer from the WRONG check, because an absent `purity:` block means an empty declared read set and the under-declaration walk fires. Written the obvious way, two of the three fixtures would have scored PASS against a compiler with no presence check at all. All three therefore use a constant body (`ok = 42`), which leaves the purity walk nothing to disagree with. **Ask what ELSE could refuse your fixture** — a negative fixture measures something only when the defect under test is the only reason the file is rejected. The `*_in_fold` three are worth reading as a fourth lesson, alongside the vacuous-PASS one and the sampled-matrix one: **every fixture before them isolated a DECLARATION gen0 fails to check, and this defect was not that.** Both purity directions were implemented and both were correct; they simply never looked inside `sum` / `fold` / `map` / `Ok(...)`. A corpus organised by declaration kind has no row for "the right check, in the wrong half of the tree", so no amount of completing the declaration matrix would have surfaced it. The headline is not the count but that the remaining 2 are **two structural causes**, which no per-defect hunt would have shown: (1) attribute lines are lexed away as blank — **this group is now EMPTY**, and its last two occupants are the fifth mis-priced row in this corpus: stratification sat here as the check "a source-line walk structurally cannot serve, since it needs the call graph AND the layer value attached to each rule", and neither half held (`check_layer_discipline` is direct-call only; `rd_name_start` is the anchor a source walk needs); (2) the `hints:` block, now down to the 1 fixture that needs interval arithmetic (`hint_overflow_bad`) — and the three that closed did so *because* a hints block, unlike an attribute line, is fully present in the token stream, which is the opposite of cause (1) and is why the fix was a token walk rather than a source walk; (3) purity / termination analyses one-directional or absent — this group is now **EMPTY**: it was 3 until the two `purity_*_extra` fixtures closed on 2026-08-14 (the EXTRA direction being its own lesson — half of a set comparison reads exactly like a set comparison), and `termination_bound_short` closed on 2026-08-15; plus the emit-refusal-surface row. **`hint_overflow_bad` is now the ONLY fixture in the corpus that genuinely needs abstract interpretation**, and it is stated alone deliberately: it spent two extra slices as the head of a family it did not have, first dragging `hint_overflow_inverted` and then `termination_bound_short` along with it. Note what the gap set could NOT tell you: the purity defect fixed on 2026-08-11 lived inside a row scored as PASS, so it moved the gap set by exactly zero. **The sweep measures which checks gen0 performs, not how well it performs them.**

**THE INVERSE BUCKET SPLIT IN TWO, BECAUSE "gen0 REFUSES MORE THAN VERBOSEC" IS TWO OPPOSITE FACTS (2026-08-16).** `INVERSE` — verbosec accepts, gen0 refuses — was one constant, deliberately kept EMPTY as a tripwire after `bad_arity` graduated. It fired on 2026-08-16, and the entry that fired it does not mean what the tripwire was built to catch.

`bad_arity` was **verbosec being WRONG**: a missing check in the reference, an INVALID program the reference blessed, and the fix belonged in `src/verifier.rs`. `match_freshtext_in_callee` is the opposite: the program is **VALID**, verbosec is RIGHT to accept it and compiles it into a working binary, and gen0 refuses only because a gen0 text value is a packed `(start, len)` span into the embedded source and fresh `concat` output sits at no addressable offset. Nothing is wrong with either compiler's verdict — gen0 is less capable, in the safe direction, and the refusal is what stops it emitting the binary it shipped until PR #171: rc 0, 2563 bytes, SIGTRAP on a reachable Err arm.

Filing both under one name would have cost twice. The set becomes permanently non-empty, so **the tripwire retires and the next genuine reference defect lands quietly beside a capability entry** — and a reader seeing a non-empty `INVERSE` goes hunting for a verbosec bug that does not exist. So it is now `INVERSE_REFERENCE_GAP` (asserted **empty**; fix belongs in the reference) and `INVERSE_CAPABILITY` (non-empty by design; fix belongs in gen0, as a future arc). **The discriminator is one question, stated at both constants and repeated in the sweep's failure message: which compiler would you change to make the two agree?** Equivalently and checkably: a REFERENCE_GAP fixture is an invalid program, a CAPABILITY fixture is a valid one that verbosec compiles and runs correctly — which the pin asserts, so "verbosec is more capable" is measured rather than claimed.

One correction falls out of the split, and it had been wrong for six days without anyone noticing: `examples/negative/README.md` opened by describing the directory as holding programs "verbosec refuses", and an INVERSE fixture never was one. `bad_arity` sat there from 2026-08-10 to 2026-08-13 as a program verbosec ACCEPTED, and the sentence survived because the fixture moved to `mirrored` before anyone re-read it. The directory holds programs **at least one of the two compilers refuses**; the bucket records which, and why. **A taxonomy that has to describe a new member is the moment its existing labels get audited** — that is the one reliable way these stale charter sentences surface.

**A THIRD WAY A FIXTURE SET CAN MISLEAD, found 2026-08-12: it can SAMPLE a matrix and read as if it had enumerated one.** The two lessons above are both about a fixture passing for the wrong reason. This one is about fixtures that were never written. `@intention` / `@source` presence had three fixtures — rule × 2, concept × 1 — out of a possible fourteen, so "gen0 does not check presence" was *measured* on two of the seven declaration kinds and *assumed* for the other five, and "verbosec requires these" was never checked per-kind at all. Nothing in the sweep's output hints at the missing cells: three fixtures in `KNOWN_GAPS` look exactly like a closed question. The remedy is the one the text-position matrix in this file already applies to output positions — **when a check ranges over a finite set of contexts, enumerate the set and fixture every cell**, and derive the reference behaviour per cell from the source rather than generalising from the cell you happened to write first. Doing that here turned up no surprise (all seven kinds require both attributes, uniformly) — but "no surprise" is a result, and it was not known before it was measured.

Two findings are worth reading even if you never run the sweep. **`purity` was HALF-checked, and the half that was missing was not the half you would guess** (closed 2026-08-14, kept here because the shape recurs): gen0 caught a `reads:` MISSING an entry the logic performs, and did NOT catch an EXTRA declared read the logic never performs. "gen0 checks purity" was true right up until you over-declared — and over-declaration is not harmless, because the declared set IS the audit surface. The generalisable half: **when you add the reverse of a one-directional check, re-audit every place its walk is incomplete**, because incompleteness that is fail-OPEN in one direction is a false positive in the other. **And the corpus caught the REFERENCE being the permissive one — see the next paragraph, which is the more important of the two findings.**

**THE VERIFIER BLESSED A PROGRAM ITS OWN EMITTER REFUSED, and that is a counterexample to the project's central claim (closed 2026-08-13).** From 2026-08-10 to 2026-08-13 verbosec had **no rule-call arity check at all**. `helper(i, i)` on a one-input rule produced `verified: 1 concept(s), 2 rule(s); all proofs check out` at rc 0 — while `--native` refused it (`native call requires exactly 1 argument`) and `--run` refused it (`rule call expects 1 argument, got 2`). It therefore **failed closed in practice**: no bad binary, no wrong answer, both execution paths rejecting. That is why it is worth writing down anyway. The thesis this repo rests on is that **the verifier is the durable artifact** — the thing that makes AI-authored code safe to trust — and a verifier that certifies a program neither of its own backends will accept is not wrong about safety, it is wrong about *itself*. Same class as the bool exit-code inconsistency fixed in PR #156: the reference disagreeing with itself. A "verified" stamp is worth exactly what it excludes, and this one excluded less than its two consumers did.

The fix is `check_call_arity` (`src/verifier.rs`), and the rule was DERIVED rather than assumed: `Parser::parse_rule` makes `input:` mandatory and `parse_binding_block` yields exactly one `(name, type)` pair, so a rule structurally has exactly one input — there is no zero-input or multi-input rule to call. **The `context:` block is the one thing that looks like a counterexample and is not**: it is a genuine second concept, but it is bound at the top-level invocation (read once from argv/stdin by the emitter), never at a call site — `eval_rule_with_value` inserts only `rule.input_name` into the callee's environment, and no call syntax exists for supplying a context. So the arity is 1, unconditionally, and both executors already agreed on that number. **Primitives are exempt structurally, not by a special case**: the parser resolves all 36 `PRIMITIVE_CALL_NAMES` in call position before the generic `Expr::Call` fallback, and record/variant construction have their own AST nodes, so nothing but a rule call can reach the check — which is what lets `now_unix()` / `length(t)` / `min(a, b)` / `substring(t, a, b)` keep their 0/1/2/3-arg forms. Pinned by `arity_check_does_not_fire_on_primitives`, so the day a primitive stops being intercepted, that test fails instead of the whole corpus.

Cost: **zero emitted bytes** — verify-side, mechanically confirmed over the 1290 native rule-binaries `examples/` produces (768 emit, 522 refused natively): **1290/1290 byte-identical by size + sha256**, with the baseline-vs-baseline control coming back empty first, per PR #157's discipline. All 151 examples still verify, `vexprparse`'s own 911 rules still verify, corpus acceptance stays 100/151, and R0/R1/R2 hold. **No corpus file and no vexprparse rule tripped the check** — the hole was never load-bearing, which is also why, unusually for this arc, **not one of the 509 existing tests had grown into it** (contrast PR #159's three, PR #161's one, and PR #162's fifty-five). Pinned by `wrong_arity_rule_call_rejected_at_verify_time`, which carries a correct-arity twin so the refusal is attributable, plus `wrong_arity_rule_call_rejected_in_nested_positions` (lambda body / `let` RHS / `concat` arg — the three positions a scoping-aware walk would most likely drop); both were verified to FAIL pre-change, reporting `got []`.

**THE FIRST OF THOSE TWO FINDINGS WAS ITSELF TOO GENEROUS, AND CATCHING THAT IS THE MOST USEFUL THING THE CORPUS HAS DONE (2026-08-11).** "gen0 catches a `reads:` that is MISSING an entry" was measured from `purity_reads_missing` scoring PASS — but that fixture declares `reads: []`, and **the empty declaration was the only shape gen0 caught**. `reads: [t.a]` against a body reading `t.a + t.b` was accepted at rc 0. So the sentence above was reporting a check that fired on the degenerate case and nothing else, in the one direction that actually conceals something. See the purity row in the gaps table for the root cause and the fix.

**The procedural lesson is the durable part, and it is the second instance of the same pattern in this arc.** Both times a check read as green because the probe landed on the working half of the input space: text `==` was stuck-`false` and therefore right on every non-matching pair, and this one was right on every empty declaration. In both cases the fixture existed, ran, and passed. So: **a fixture that passes for the wrong reason is worse than a missing one** — a missing fixture leaves a visible hole, a vacuously-passing one retires the question — and **when a check has more than one failure shape, fixture EACH shape** rather than letting the cheapest one stand in for the family. Both purity shapes now have their own fixture (`*_missing` and `*_missing_partial`), for calls as well as reads, even though calls was measured correct all along: "calls was fine" is a measurement, not a property, and nothing else in the suite would notice if the two checks drifted apart.

A related instrument came out of the same slice, and it answers the "exit status only" weakness in the bullet list below. **gen0 emits no diagnostic whatsoever** — a refusal is a bare `exit 1` with zero bytes on stdout and nothing on stderr — so there is no message to compare and no cheap way to tighten the sweep. What does work is a **corrected twin**: alongside the invalid program, compile the MINIMALLY corrected one and require ACCEPT. The refusal is then attributable, because the only thing that differs between the two verdicts is the violation under test. `two_generation_gen0_detects_partial_purity_underdeclaration` does that for every shape it pins; it is per-fixture work, which is why the sweep does not do it for all 23.

What the corpus does NOT cover is stated in `examples/negative/README.md` and belongs here too, because "the negative corpus is green" must never be read as "gen0's verifier is complete": 45 fixtures is not an enumeration of verbosec's refusal paths (resources, connections, services, reactions, `concept_group` bounds, `Result` arm typing all have none); each fixture isolates one defect, so co-occurring violations are untested; and the sweep compares **exit status only** — gen0 refusing for an unrelated reason scores as a PASS.

**The two cheap gaps closed with it, and why they had to be closed on the RAW SOURCE.** `gen0's tokenizer discards attribute lines entirely`: `tokenize_indent`'s `isattr` (`c0b == 64` — a line whose first non-space byte is `@`) folds the line into `isblank`, so it emits **no tokens at all**. That single fact is the common cause of every attribute-shaped row in the table above, and it means no parser-side check is possible — you cannot check what the token stream does not contain. Rather than teach the tokenizer to emit attribute tokens (which would ripple through every parse position that currently steps over a blank line), `attr_errors` walks the RAW SOURCE **line by line**, with the same `line_width` / `next_line_start` pair the tokenizer itself uses. A line walk rather than a byte walk is both cheaper (33k frames, the same order as `tokenize_indent`'s own recursion, not 1.6M) and MORE precise: an attribute must begin a line, so a `@` anywhere else cannot be mistaken for one.

It closes exactly the two CLOSED-SET checks and no more: the attribute NAME against `{verbose, intention, source, layer}`, and — when the name is `layer` — the VALUE against `{domain, application, interface}`. The name set is the **union over every declaration kind**, so it is deliberately weaker than verbosec's context-sensitive rule (verbosec allows `@layer` on a rule and refuses it on a concept; this walk accepts both). **Weaker in that direction is the safe direction: it can miss a violation, never invent one** — which is the whole reason a source-level lexical check is admissible here at all. Measured over all 151 files in `examples/`: the only line-leading attribute names that occur are exactly those four and the only `@layer` values are `domain` and `application`, so no accepted program moves; comment lines start with `--` and Verbose has no multi-line string, so no line inside a comment or a literal can begin with `@`.

Cost: **zero emitted bytes**. The addition is verify-side, so it changes gen0's own binary and nothing gen0 emits — mechanically confirmed by compiling all 151 examples with a gen0 built from HEAD and a gen0 built from the patched source: **151/151 byte-identical (size + sha256), acceptance unchanged at 100/151**, and the baseline-vs-baseline control came back **empty**. The two negative fixtures `attr_unknown_on_rule` and `layer_unknown_name` flipped from GAP to PASS.

**PRESENCE followed on 2026-08-12, on the same walk, and the sentence that used to sit here is the reason it took a separate slice.** This paragraph previously said presence "needs to associate an attribute with the declaration it belongs to and decide where a declaration ENDS (`concept_group` nesting makes that ambiguous)" — scoping it as a stateful-walk project. **That was the expensive artifact, not the code**, exactly as the stale INDENT/DEDENT comment was for the multi-line `if` (see the note further down). The ambiguity dissolves once you notice the question never has to be answered: a declaration owns every line from its header up to the NEXT header, so the inner scan STOPS at the next header and never decides where anything ends. `concept_group` nesting then falls out for free — the group's attributes sit between its header and the first nested `concept` header, so the group's scan stops exactly there, and each nested concept gets its own. `attr_pres_errors` is five rules; all seven declaration-keyword recognizers already existed for gen0's own parser. What remains genuinely open: `@source file:line` RESOLUTION (needs to open the referenced file — a second input channel gen0 does not have) and `@layer` STRATIFICATION (needs the call graph and the layer value ATTACHED to each rule, i.e. retaining attributes through the parse rather than re-scanning the source — the one attribute check a source-line walk structurally cannot do).

**How the text-`==` defect evaded every sweep, and why that lesson outlives the bug.** It was **correct on half the input space.** A stuck-`false` equality agrees with the right answer for every non-matching pair, and a differential harness that feeds arbitrary or placeholder inputs (the sweeps in this session fed `"ab"` to every text field) practically never generates a *matching* pair — so `clients`, `layers`, `alert`, `priv_failure`, `showcase`, `config`, `allowlist`, `access_check` and `sum_by_tag` were all scored as AGREEING, run after run. Measured pre-fix on the nine, with the rule under test hoisted to entry and a matching input constructed by hand: **9/9 wrong on the matching input, 9/9 right on the non-matching one.** Two harness lessons follow, both cheap and both now applied. First, **a differential over a boolean needs inputs from both sides of the predicate** — reading the literal out of the source and feeding it back is a three-line change that would have caught this the first day. Second, **fixtures are part of the differential**: `allowlist` and `access_check` compare against `read(<resource>)`, their resource files did not exist, both compilers therefore failed closed, and the harness recorded a match — an agreement that proves nothing about the code path it was supposed to exercise. The regression pin asserts BOTH arms for exactly this reason (a false-only assertion passes against a stuck-false emitter) and writes the resource fixture itself. Note also what did NOT catch it: gen0 compiles ITSELF correctly and always has, because vexprparse contains **not one text `==`** — it compares text through `byte_at` loops (`name_eq`, the whole `span_is_*` family), which are NUMBER equalities on individual bytes. A self-hosting fixed point is a strong signal about the paths the compiler's own source exercises, and silent about the ones it does not.

The `hints:` rows have the longest history in this table and it is the reason the table exists at all. Before any of it landed, gen0 did not skip the block — it **halted** on it, and (pre-PR #141) that halt was indistinguishable from EOF, so every rule after a `hints:`-carrying rule was **silently dropped from the emitted binary at exit 0**: `showcase` emitted 1 rule of 6, `business` 1 of 4, `generated` 1 of 3, `pricing` 1 of 2. Those files counted as corpus passes the whole time. The trade then made was **a silent truncation replaced by a documented verification gap** — strictly better, but still a gap, and writing it down here is what kept it findable. Three of the four cells closed on 2026-08-14; the fourth is named in its own row above. See the `span_is_hints` banner and the "HINTS-BLOCK CHECKS" banner in `examples/vexprparse.verbose` for the per-check detail, `self_hosted_parses_all_rules_past_a_trailing_hints_block` for the skip's pin and `two_generation_gen0_verifies_hint_names_justifications_and_overflow_shape` for the checks' pin (both `src/native.rs`). **The durable lesson is about the ROW, not the code**: the previous version of this table lumped both `overflow` fixtures under "needs interval arithmetic", and that single sentence is what scoped the whole `hints:` family as an expensive arc and deferred it. It is the third time in this arc a stale framing cost more than the feature (the INDENT/DEDENT comment, the attribute-PRESENCE "genuinely ambiguous" note, and now this) — when a row says a gap is expensive, check the claim against the two compilers before believing it.

**verbosec's own bool EXIT CODE was inconsistent, and an OPTIMIZATION HINT decided which behaviour you got — CLOSED 2026-08-09.** Measured on `emit_write_string`'s seven call sites: only two — `emit_full_program` and `emit_self_recursive_program`, the standard scalar paths — followed the `false` write with `mov qword [rbp + exit_flag_slot], 1`, so the binary exited 1. The other five (`emit_vectorized_program`, `emit_fold_program`, `emit_multi_fold_program`, `emit_parallel_program`, `emit_collection_program`) exited 0 on both arms. Which emitter claims a rule is therefore what decided the exit code, and for the scalar-threshold shape that choice is made by the **`vectorizable` hint**. On the same false result: `examples/invoices.verbose::important_invoice` (hinted → SIMD) printed `false` at **rc 0**, while `examples/alert.verbose::is_critical` (unhinted → `emit_full_program`) printed `false` at **rc 1**. Two files, same language construct, same answer, different exit codes — **a hint changing observable behaviour, which the project's own rules forbid**.

The fix is the one this note previously called "the real fix": make verbosec's seven bool sites agree, rather than teach gen0 an optimizer decision. All seven now route through **one shared `emit_bool_arms`** (`src/native.rs`), so "all bool sites agree" is mechanically true instead of a coincidence of seven hand-copied blocks — which is precisely what had drifted. The contract is stated in the Language Features section above. Cost, measured across the 748 native rule-binaries the corpus produces: **4 binaries moved, 744 unchanged** (`access_check::all_authorized` +26 B, `invoices::important_invoice` +12 B, `parallel_threshold::above` +53 B, `retirement::retirement_status` +26 B), each matching its predicted per-site cost exactly. `emit_multi_fold_program` has no bool rule anywhere in `examples/`, so it is covered by a synthetic pin rather than by the corpus — an uncovered emitter being exactly how the original drift survived.

Two mechanism notes worth keeping. **`emit_vectorized_program` has no rbp frame at all**, so its flag lives in `r10` (the one register that emitter never touches; Linux syscalls clobber only rax/rcx/r11) rather than in a slot — the single deviation from the `Slot(i32)` shape every other emitter uses. **`emit_parallel_program` forks**, and the child prints records `[0, mid)` and exits with *its own* flag; the parent's `wait4` therefore had to grow a real status pointer (it passed `NULL` before) so the child's half can be folded in. Without that, a `false` printed in the child would have been silently dropped from the program's exit code — the same class of defect this change exists to remove.

gen0 follows, now that there is one contract to follow: `entry_rule_bool`'s trampoline carries the flag in `rbx` (set after `call entry` returns, and only `write` runs before the exit, so the reaction/service `rbx` conventions are not in play), **79 → 83 bytes**. Exactly the 10 bool-entry corpus files moved, each by +4; the other 90 are byte-identical; acceptance stays 100/151. `two_generation_gen0_formats_bool_outputs_as_true_false`'s `assert_eq!((got_rc, ref_rc), (Some(0), Some(1)))` — which pinned the divergence — flipped to `(Some(1), Some(1))`.

**The declared-bound checks are gated by ONE rule per kind, and that is the whole safety argument.** `num_bound_check_size` and `text_bound_check_size` decide "this field gets a runtime check"; the emit rules (`x86_num_bound_check`, `x86_text_bound_argv`, `x86_text_bound_stdin`) each gate on the corresponding SIZE rule rather than re-deriving the predicate. That is deliberate and it is the same discipline `entry_uses_stdin` enforces for the channel choice, for the same reason: a size walk and an emit walk that disagree about whether N bytes are present desync `p_filesz` and `src_base`, and then every `byte_at` in the emitted binary reads garbage — a failure that looks nothing like an off-by-N. The `ty` gate mirrors verbosec exactly (`emit_bounds_check` fires for `Type::Number` only, `emit_text_bound_check` for `Type::Text` only), which is why a `bool` field carrying a range is deliberately NOT checked. Two things the re-scan road has to get right and does: the discriminator is the first byte INSIDE the bracket (`.` ⇒ the text form, digit-or-`-` ⇒ the numeric form), which is load-bearing because `type_code_of_span` maps `bytes` to 0, the **same code as `number`** — without it a `bytes [..N]` field would be checked against a misparsed numeric range; and an unrecognised shape emits NO check rather than a wrong one. Known divergences, both benign: verbosec BOUNDS its text scan at `max + 1` bytes on the principle that the declaration says it never has to read further, while gen0 checks after the strlen its `rep movsb` needs anyway (same accept/reject verdict, different work on the rejecting path, and the kernel's `MAX_ARG_STRLEN` caps argv at 131072 regardless); and gen0 inlines its 12-byte house `exit(1)` at each site instead of routing to a shared end-of-binary label, which is free to do because gen0's emitted bytes are never byte-compared to verbosec's — only gen1 to gen0. Note also that for `ScanState.source` specifically the new check is *dead*: the stdin read loop already fail-closes at `r13 >= 4 MiB`, the same constant as the declared bound. It is the fields declared SMALLER than the region (`[..200000]`) that were unchecked, and are now checked.

**gen0's input channel is DERIVED from the field's declared bound, not assumed.** gen0 has two input trampolines — `x86_argv_marshal` (52 B per text field: strlen, `rep movsb` into the fixed region, push the packed span) and `x86_stdin_marshal` (a 55-byte read-to-EOF loop plus 13 B per text field). Which one it emits used to be decided by `field_list_has_text`: *any* text field in the entry concept routed the whole program to stdin. `docs/self-hosting-stdin-channel-design.md` chose that as "the simplest deterministic rule" to unblock the self-compile, and it was right for exactly one program — the self-compile — and a **silent wrong answer** for every other. verbosec's default channel is argv, so a program whose text field was meant to arrive on argv got a binary that compiled, ran, exited 0, and read an empty stdin instead: `length(h.data)` over an argv string returned 0, and `sha512_fold` returned digest byte 0 = 106 where verbosec and Python `hashlib` both return 207. The 52-byte argv text arm was unreachable dead code the whole time, which is why nothing caught it — and no corpus count can catch this class, because acceptance only means "exit 0 with an ELF written".

The rule is now: **a text field whose DECLARED bound exceeds Linux's `MAX_ARG_STRLEN` (131072 bytes) cannot be delivered through argv, so it — and only it — routes to stdin.** Everything else (bounded at or below 131072, or with no declared bound) keeps verbosec's default argv channel. That is a mechanical consequence of a declaration plus a kernel constant, not a convention: the channel is visible in the source, at the field. `entry_uses_stdin` is the single rule both walks call — `blob_end_off`'s `msize` (size) and `elf_program_src`'s marshal dispatch (emit) — so the two can never disagree about which trampoline is there; a split would desync `p_filesz` and `src_base` and every `byte_at` in the emitted binary would read garbage. The cost of the change is exactly `39k - 55` bytes for an entry concept with `k` text fields (+23 at k=2, -16 at k=1), and it moved 35 of the 80 accepted corpus binaries — precisely the 35 whose entry concept has a text field; the other 45 are byte-identical.

The load-bearing consequence for the self-compile: `ScanState.source` declared `text [..256]` while carrying 1.3 MB, a false declaration nothing ever checked. It now declares `[..4194304]` — the same constant as the `cmp r13, 0x400000` fail-closed guard the stdin trampoline emits — and that declaration is what keeps gen1 reading its program from stdin. Shrink it below 131072 and the self-compile silently switches to argv and stops working. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_reads_argv_text_input_field` (`src/native.rs`), which asserts the argv pointer AND length from a gen0-emitted binary, asserts a `[..4194304]` field still reads stdin, and closes on the external oracle: gen0-compiled `sha512_fold` must return 207, the value `hashlib.sha512(b"").digest()[0]` gives.

**THE CORPUS FIGURE HAS BEEN MEASURING THE WRONG PROGRAM FOR 37 OF ITS 100 FILES, AND THAT IS A THIRD KIND OF OVER-CLAIM (2026-08-15).** The two already recorded in this file are both about a *defect* declared closed on incomplete evidence. This one is about the *instrument*: the number was correct, mechanically produced, CI-enforced, and quoted in ~30 PR bodies — and it did not mean what its name says.

**"gen0 accepts `examples/aes_gcm.verbose`" meant "gen0 compiled `aes_sbox`."** Because the entry rule was hardwired to rule #0 (see the row above), the sweep measured *whether the first rule in each file happens to be emittable*, and rule #0 is a HELPER another rule calls in **37 of the 100** accepted files. Every `aes_*` file enters at `aes_sbox`, every `sha256_*` at `rotr32`, `vexprparse` itself at `skip_spaces`. The gen0 `aes_gcm` binary returns 99 / 124 / 202 / 22 for inputs 0 / 1 / 16 / 255 — the FIPS-197 S-box, byte-exact, and no GCM anywhere in it. So for over a third of the corpus, "accepted" certified a leaf helper and said nothing about the file's subject.

**The corrected figure, with the entry declared rather than inferred from source order: 106/151.** Measured by re-sweeping every file at its call-graph ROOT (the last rule no other rule calls, index 0 for a pure cycle). Two independent conventions — last-root and last-declared-rule — agree on **all 151 files**, which is worth knowing because it means the repo's "write the top-level composition last" habit is uniform enough that the choice of convention is not doing the work. Exactly **6 files gain** and **0 are lost**: `sum_chain` (1047 B, `sum_seed` → 0/6/15/55), `print_chain` (4271 B, `show` → `3+2+1+0`), `label_tree` (2757 B, `measure` → 1/3/7/11), `token_classify` (931 B, `score` → 0/5/7/−1), `token_label` (3027 B, `label_of`) and `purchase` (2563 B, `discounted_purchase`) — all six previously counted as REFUSALS attributed to capability gaps, and none of them was one.

**PR #171 revises that to 105/151, and the revision is the honest direction.** Two of the six gained files were measured broken the moment they became reachable (the paragraph below), and one of the two cannot be fixed without an arc: `purchase::discounted_purchase` now REFUSES at its declared entry (rc 1, zero bytes) instead of emitting a 2563-byte binary whose Err arm SIGTRAPs. **The count going DOWN is the count becoming true** — the 106 was counting an ELF that existed, not a program that worked, which is the same error the 100 was making for 37 files before entry selection exposed it. Re-measured at the declared entry with a gen0 built from the parent and one from the patched source: base **106**, patched **105**, and exactly **2 of 151 files move** — `purchase` 2563 B → refused, `token_label` 3027 → 3057 B (+30, the variant-binder fix) — with the other **149 byte-identical by size + sha256**. That second number is the one worth having: it says the transitive fresh-text walk newly refuses *nothing except the file it was written for*, which no argument about the walk's scope could establish on its own.

**The sweep's asserted number stayed 100 through the entry-selection slice deliberately, and moved to 99 on 2026-08-17 for an unrelated reason.** It invokes at the default index, so it still measures rule-#0 breadth, and moving it in the same slice that introduced selection would have made one number mean two things across a commit boundary. It moved later because a LANGUAGE FEATURE — declared constant byte tables — made `examples/aes_sbox.verbose` a shape gen0 cannot emit, so gen0 now refuses it; see that row in the gaps table. What must not happen is the number continuing to be read as breadth-of-subject: **quote it as "97/158 at rule #0" or not at all.** *(The first move was the same one file on both halves: 100 → 99 and 105 → 104. `aes_sbox` declares exactly one rule, so rule #0 and its declared entry are the same rule and the two figures cannot disagree there. The second move, 99 → 93 and 104 → 98 on 2026-08-17, is the other six AES-family files adopting the same declared table — and it is the sharpest illustration this file has of why the rule-#0 figure needs the qualifier, because those six were SIX ACCEPTANCES OF ONE PROGRAM: rule #0 in all six is `aes_sbox`, and their pre-change gen0 binaries — 53884 to 672023 bytes of them — every one answered 99 / 124 / 202 / 22, the S-box, not the file's subject.)* *(Third move, 93 → 94 and 98 → 99 on 2026-08-19: slice agg-1 adds `examples/aggregate_pair.verbose`. Both halves move by one, and the two halves mean OPPOSITE things about the same file — at rule #0 gen0 compiles `swap2` correctly and byte-for-byte agrees with verbosec, while at the declared entry it emits an 825-byte ELF that SIGTRAPs. So the 94 is honest about `swap2` and says nothing about `total`, and the 99 counts a file whose subject gen0 cannot emit. Recorded as its own gaps-table row above; this is the first time the two figures have disagreed about what a single new file is worth.)* *(Fourth move, 96 → 97 and 155 → 156 on 2026-09-02: `examples/gcm_frame.verbose`, the TLS 1.3 AEAD framing rules. Rule #0 is `gcm_nonce` and gen0 gets it byte-for-byte right, as it does four of the file's other five rules; the one it gets wrong is `gcm_j0` at index 1 — the file's single aggregate hop, `let n = gcm_nonce(i)` — with the same record-let SIGTRAP signature as `aggregate_pair::total`, a fourth file in that row. Detail in the sweep constant's comment.)*

**The generalisable lesson is about metrics rather than about gen0.** The two "family is closed" notes above teach *never declare a category empty; enumerate the positions you tested*. This one teaches the harder sibling: **a mechanical, CI-enforced, reproducible number can still measure the wrong thing, and mechanising it is what stops anyone asking what it measures.** Nothing in the harness was broken — it did exactly what it said. The defect was in the sentence "gen0 accepts N files", where "accepts" quietly meant "compiled whichever rule was written first". When a metric is derived from an artifact, state the derivation next to the number, not in the doc comment: this one *was* documented ("entry `elf_program_src`" appears in the sweep's own comment) and still went 30 PRs without anyone noticing that the corresponding phrase for every OTHER file is "entry: whatever came first".

**Two live defects surfaced immediately, and their invisibility is the same fact restated: nothing in this repo had ever RUN those rules.** (1) `token_label::label_of`'s text-typed VARIANT BINDER is itoa'd — argv `0 hello` gives gen0 `id:2287823529757900805` against verbosec's `id:hello`, and `0 ada` gives `...803`; both decode as `0x1fbffb61_0000000N` with identical start and N == the exact byte length. A new member of the itoa-a-pointer family, in the arena variant-binder position, alongside the field position the 2026-08-08 slice fixed. Asserted AS-IS in `two_generation_gen0_selects_the_entry_rule`, so the fix flips a named test. (2) `purchase::discounted_purchase` at index 1 compiles and its Ok arm is byte-exact (`500 30` → 450), but its Err arm SIGTRAPs (rc 133) where verbosec writes the reason and exits 1 — that is precisely the residual PR #151 named and dismissed as *"an entry that CALLS a non-texty helper whose body builds fresh text still traps in the callee's proc — no current corpus file has that shape"*.
