Entry selection is what gives a corpus file that shape: `entry_freshtext` walks the ENTRY rule, and here the fresh `concat` lives in the CALLEE `validate_purchase`. Closing it means walking the entry's transitive callees, which is its own slice; until then, `purchase` is the one of the six gained files whose selected binary is not safe to run on both arms. Neither defect is caused by this slice and neither moves the sweep, which still refuses `purchase` at index 0.

**BOTH CLOSED 2026-08-16 (PR #171), and the reason they existed at all is the most transferable thing here: a limitation is only unreachable RELATIVE TO HOW YOU INVOKE THE COMPILER.** Neither defect was introduced by entry selection and neither was new — the variant-binder itoa had been in the emitter since slice 4.2, and the fresh-text-in-callee trap was *named in writing* by PR #151 and dismissed with "no current corpus file has that shape". Both statements were true, and both silently depended on an invocation convention nobody had written down as a premise: the entry rule is whatever was declared first. PR #170 changed the invocation surface, and two dormant defects became reachable the same afternoon, in files that had been in `examples/` for months. **When you scope a residual as unreachable, record WHAT makes it unreachable** — here "no corpus file has that shape" was really "no corpus file has that shape *at rule #0*", and the qualifier is the whole claim. See also the corpus-figure note above, which is the same stale premise seen from the metric side rather than the defect side.

(1) **The variant binder is FIXED**, by the same wiring the streamed-text-field slice used, one walk further in. The arm binders that `lets_append_binders` conses onto the `lets` list carried a DUMMY `AstNum(0)` value, so `let_index` resolved the name and nothing downstream could tell a text payload from a number — the stream walk's `AstVar` branch took the 6-byte itoa call for both. New `lets_append_typed_binders` looks the arm's payload types up via the already-existing `arm_payload_by_name` and marks a TEXT payload's dummy as `AstStr`; new `let_marks_textspan` is the ONE predicate both walks call (`code_size_stream_node` for the count, `x86_stream_node` for the bytes — the two-pass drift edge), dispatching to the same 36-byte span write the `AstField` text branch already owned. **The eleventh consecutive slice in this arc whose fix was WIRING an emitter that already existed**, and the fifth member of the itoa-a-pointer family. Cost is exactly `36 - 6` = **+30 B per streamed text binder site**, measured on both cells of the new matrix column: `token_label::label_of` 3027 → 3057 (one site, `concat("id:", label)`) and a bare-binder twin 2494 → 2524 (one site, `label` as the whole arm body). Both are byte-exact against verbosec afterwards on every arm.

(2) **The fresh text in a reachable callee is REFUSED**, not fixed, and the split is at the representation boundary PR #151 established: gen0's text values are packed `(start, len)` spans into the embedded source blob and fresh `concat` output sits at no addressable offset, so materializing it is a real arc (runtime buffer + itoa-to-memory + span rebasing), not a slice. What changes is the SCOPE of `entry_freshtext`: it walked the entry rule only, and now walks the entry's TRANSITIVE callees via `reach_visit` / `reach_calls`, a visited-list DFS. Three properties are load-bearing. **The callee set is the declared `calls:` list**, sound for exactly the reason `layer_list` states — purity is checked in both directions and descends into every node family, so for any program gen0 accepts the declared set IS the performed set; weaken either purity direction and this walk silently weakens with it. **The visited list is what makes a call CYCLE terminate** (a revisited name returns the list unchanged), so a recursive callee cannot diverge the walk. **The DFS is offender-GATED** — `nonentry_offenders` sweeps per-rule counts first, each `arena_scope`'d, and no visited list is ever built when the program has no offender anywhere, which is why the self-compile pays one linear sweep. Scope stays REACHABLE rather than PRESENT, so `enrich`/`order_intake`-shaped concat in a never-called rule is still accepted; probe 4 of the pin exists because no corpus file exercises that distinction.

Measured, `purchase::discounted_purchase` at index 1 moves from **rc 0 / 2563 B whose Err arm exits 133 with no output** to **rc 1 / zero bytes**; verbosec still accepts and still emits (`500 12` → `customer age 12 is under 18`, rc 1), which is what makes this a CAPABILITY refusal rather than a verifier gap — see the two INVERSE buckets in `examples/negative/README.md`. **Cost at the default entry is zero, and proving that non-vacuous took the index-1 measurements above**, because at rule #0 `token_label` enters at `classify` (a sum-type output, refused by `entry_badrecord`) and `purchase` at `validate_purchase` (refused by `entry_freshtext`'s original entry-only walk) — so both defect files were already refused where the sweep looks. Sweeping all 151 examples through a gen0 built from the parent and one from the patched source, with the SAME inputs fed to both: **151 of 151 byte-identical by size + sha256**, no binary changing bytes while keeping its size, acceptance unchanged at **100/151** with an unchanged refused set, and the baseline-vs-baseline control empty first. gen0's own binary grew 647180 → 651769 B (+4589), rules 945 → 954, concepts 369 → 375; peak self-compile RSS 724800 → 727396 KiB (+0.36%) against the 8M-node arena. R0/R1/R2 hold. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_refuses_reachable_match_result_fresh_text` (refusal, corrected twin, verbosec-still-accepts, reachable-not-present + cycle termination) and by probe 1b of `two_generation_gen0_selects_the_entry_rule`, whose as-is assertion flipped to full stdout + exit-code agreement on all three arms; both verified to FAIL pre-change.

**The corpus figure is now mechanical.** gen0's acceptance breadth over `examples/` — currently **97/158 at rule #0** — is produced by `two_generation_corpus_acceptance_sweep` (`src/native.rs`), which builds gen0, feeds it every `examples/*.verbose`, asserts the count and prints the refused list on failure. Run it with `cargo test --release -- --ignored --test-threads=1 two_generation_corpus` (~25 s; `ulimit -s unlimited` is raised internally); the `two_generation` prefix puts it in the existing `self-hosting-bootstrap` CI job. Before this test the number lived only in commit messages, which is how PR #142's predecessor state — files counting as corpus passes while emitting truncated binaries — went unchallenged for as long as it did. **"Accepted" means gen0 exits 0 having written an ELF; it does NOT mean the binary is correct, that gen0 verified what verbosec would, or — until entry selection landed — that the rule compiled was the file's subject** (see the table above and the metric note). Byte-identity and run-correctness are the fixed-point test's job (R0/R1/R2); this one measures breadth.

**THE `rl_head_*` AUDIT, and why "53 call sites" was 17 (2026-08-15).** Entry selection had to answer one question before touching anything: which of the places that read the HEAD of the RuleList mean *the entry rule* and which merely mean *the head of the list I am currently walking*. `grep -c rl_head_ examples/vexprparse.verbose` says **53**, and that number is LINES: 15 comments, 23 `calls:` proof entries, 7 rule definitions, and **17 real invocation lines carrying 28 invocations**. Classifying those 17:

- **ENTRY SEMANTICS (6 lines, 5 rules)** — `elf_program_src` (the emit walk), `blob_end_off` (the size walk, which must agree with it or `p_filesz` and `src_base` desync), `resource_errors` and `connection_errors` (both derive `has_input` from the ENTRY's params to refuse a resource-reading program with no marshal), and `reaction_errors` (`trig_is_head` — does the reaction's trigger name the entry, which is load-bearing because `x86_reaction_tramp` is not even passed `prog` and simply calls offset 0). **All five needed to follow the selection and all five got it for free**, because `elf_program_src` builds ONE `prog` and hands the same list to every one of them. That is the property that made a list rotation the right shape: had any of the five re-parsed the source independently, they would have silently disagreed about which rule is the entry — the same class of two-walk drift as the `entry_uses_stdin` channel decision.
- **LIST TRAVERSAL / STANDALONE PROBE DRIVERS (11 lines, 12 rules)** — `count_reads`, `count_calls`, `rule_term_bound`, `rule_term_kind`, `rule_param_count`, `rule_return_type`, `nth_param_type`, `eval_nth_rule`, `eval_main`, `body_type`. Every one is an `#[ignore]`-free unit-test driver invoked directly from `src/native.rs`, each documented as answering a question about "the FIRST rule" (or, for `eval_nth_rule`, about the n-th, via its own `drop_rules`). **NONE was changed.** "The first rule's declared bound" is their contract, not an accident of it; rewriting them to follow an entry index would change what dozens of unit tests assert while fixing nothing — and `eval_main`, whose banner already says *"Convention: the FIRST rule in the source is the entry point"*, is gen0's INTERPRETER driver, a different entry notion from the emitter's that happens to share a phrase.

The useful generalisation: **an accessor named for a POSITION (`_head_`) is used for two unrelated reasons, and grep cannot tell them apart.** The discriminator here was not the accessor but the CALLER — is this rule part of the emit pipeline `elf_program_src` drives, or is it a standalone driver with its own input? Answering that per call site is the substance of a slice like this; the count of grep hits is not.

**R0's source-text reorder is now replaceable, and was deliberately NOT replaced.** `two_generation_bootstrap_fixed_point` builds gen1 by rewriting `examples/vexprparse.verbose` in the harness — moving the `rule elf_program_src` block to the top of the file — precisely because gen1's entry proc has to land at blob offset 0. Entry selection makes that a parameter: measured, `gen1' = gen0(the ORIGINAL unmodified source, 929)` emits a valid 3005651-byte ELF, `gen2' = gen1'(same source, 929)` is **byte-identical to gen1'**, and gen1' reproduces gen0's exact bytes for `invoices` / `clients` / `factorial` / `gcd` / `payroll` / `config` / `logs`. So the reorder can go. It is left in place anyway: R0 is the single highest-value assertion in the repo, and swapping its construction in the same commit that introduces the mechanism it would depend on removes the independent check exactly when it is most needed. The follow-up slice is one line in the harness plus deriving the index; the evidence above is the go-ahead, not the change.

**`int3` in a gen0 binary: PRESENCE is not the defect, REACHABILITY is (measured 2026-08-07).** gen0 emits a one-byte `int3` (0xCC) as the placeholder for a value slot it cannot lower. **50 of the 110 accepted binaries contain at least one**, and *most of those binaries are correct*, because the traps sit on arms that valid input never enters. The reference case: `examples/aes_sbox.verbose` carries two `int3` and is byte-exact against FIPS-197 — measured `sbox[0]=99 (0x63)`, `sbox[1]=124 (0x7c)`, `sbox[16]=202 (0xca)`, `sbox[255]=22 (0x16)`. **Do not read the count as a crisis, and never build a blanket "refuse any program whose emit contains an `int3`" gate** — it would drop ~45% of the corpus, most of it working, and it would have missed the actual iso_date bug entirely (after the multi-line-record-field fix, iso_date's `int3` count was **0** and it still SIGSEGV'd, because that crash was a `call` into the data section, not a trap). The only way to tell the two apart is to RUN the binary.

So it was run. All 110 accepted binaries were fed plausible in-range argv derived from the entry (first) rule's input concept — **96 ran** (79 rc 0, 12 rc 1, 5 killed by a signal), 14 skipped for a collection-typed or absent input concept (`access_check`, `banner_roster`, `callable_let_variant`, `expense_audit`, `expr_stmt`, `hello_http`, `payroll`, `recursive_text_eval`, `report`, `retirement`, `roster`, `sep_roster`, `sum_by_tag`, `tagged_bonuses` — an untested remainder, not a clean bill). **Five reach a trap or produce a wrong answer, and they are one defect: gen0's TEXT-producing result path is unimplemented.** Differentially against verbosec on the same argv:

| example (entry rule) | gen0 | verbosec |
|---|---|---|
| `tier::classify_tier` (`Result(text, text)`) | Ok arm rc 0 printing `2864743186439` — **a raw packed (start,len) span, a silent wrong answer**; Err arm SIGTRAP | `premium` / `balance 1 is below…` |
| `purchase::validate_purchase` (`Result(number, text)`) | Ok arm **correct** (`500`, rc 0); Err arm SIGTRAP | `500` / `customer age 12 is under 18` |
| `gate_result::gate` (`Result(text, text)` + text lets) | SIGTRAP on both arms | `welcome ada` / `sorry ada, …` |
| `fullname::compose_greeting` (record with a `concat` text field) | SIGTRAP on all inputs | `{"fullname":"Ada Lovelace","age":36}` |
| `ledger_line::format_line` (`output: text` via chained lets) | SIGTRAP on all inputs | `[ada#7] amount=500 posted` |

Reachability is confirmed at the byte level, not inferred: for each of the five the kernel-reported trap `ip` is exactly `int3_site + 1` (x86 reports RIP after the 1-byte trap), so the `int3` was *executed*. The common denominator is that gen0's number trampoline is the only finished one — `purchase`'s Ok arm is a Number and works; every arm that must yield TEXT either traps at the placeholder or, where the packed span survives to the printer, emits the span as a decimal integer. **The silent-wrong-answer row is the dangerous one**, and it is the same class as the argv-text and defensive-bounds defects above: a compiler that returns a number that is simply not the answer.

**CLOSED for the Result half (2026-08-07, corpus 110 → 105).** The slice split the defect at the representation boundary, because gen0's text values are packed spans into the embedded source and FRESH text (concat output) has no such representation. The SPAN half is IMPLEMENTED: the Result trampoline now dispatches on the entry's DECLARED Ok type via `entry_result_ok_texty` (the out_ty span stores only the `Result` token, so the payload type is RE-SCANNED at `ty_start + ty_len` — the `field_text_bound` road again), and a `Result(text, ...)` entry gets a 189-byte trampoline variant whose Ok block is the byte-mirror of the already-span-aware Err block (write(1, src_base+off, len) ; `\n` ; exit 0) instead of the 210-byte itoa variant. Measured: a literal-payload tier twin and an `Ok(v.name)` field-payload probe both produce verbosec's EXACT stdout/stderr/exit codes under gen0. The FRESH-TEXT half is REFUSED: `entry_freshtext` joins the verrs gate and counts concat / texty-rule calls in positions `x86_node` value-lowers — the ENTRY rule's let RHSes plus its body under the same texty/value dispatch as `x86_proc` (`count_valtext_*` mirrors `x86_node`, `count_streamtext_*` mirrors `x86_stream_node`, the same drift edge as the size mirrors) — so all five files above are now rc 1 with ZERO bytes instead of trapping or lying. Scope is deliberately ENTRY-ONLY (presence is not the defect, reachability is: `enrich`/`order_intake`-shape concat in never-called rules must not refuse) and service programs are exempt wholesale (the service trampoline is closed — zero `e8` — and its `x86_svc_*` streamers handle handler concat). Named residual: an entry that CALLS a non-texty helper whose body builds fresh text still traps in the callee's proc — no current corpus file has that shape. **That residual came due on 2026-08-15 and is CLOSED by PR #171** — and the qualifier in the sentence above is exactly what expired: "no current corpus file has that shape" silently meant "at rule #0", so entry selection (PR #170) produced one the same afternoon (`purchase::discounted_purchase` at index 1, rc 0 / 2563 B, SIGTRAP on its Err arm). `entry_freshtext` now walks the entry's TRANSITIVE callees over the declared `calls:` graph, via an offender-gated visited-list DFS that a call cycle cannot diverge; scope stays REACHABLE rather than PRESENT, so the `enrich` / `order_intake` exemption above is preserved. Every one of the 105 surviving binaries is byte-identical to before the slice. Pinned by `two_generation_gen0_emits_text_result_values_or_refuses` (refusal half + two differential probes + the untouched number path via `config.verbose`), and `streaming_text_codegen_elf_prints_bytes`'s refusal case flipped from its v1 posture (compile to int3, SIGTRAP at runtime) to the v2 posture (verify-time refusal, zero bytes).

**The sweep was then re-run against the 105, this time differentially and INCLUDING collection-typed entries** (count-prefixed argv — 10 of the previously-untested 14 now ran; still never run: `callable_let_variant`, `expr_stmt`, `hello_http` (no argv-shaped entry), `recursive_text_eval` (unencodable input)). Signal-killed is down to **ONE**: `retirement`, whose entry is a SCALAR-body `map` over RECORD elements — `x86_stream_node`'s AstMap arm documents that shape as an int3 placeholder ("Record elements -> int3"); it is a different feature hole (collection element marshalling), not a text-path miss, first observed because collection entries had never been run. The 19 rc-1 exits (the previous 12 plus `access_check`, `banner_roster`, `config`, `ladder_recursive`, `sep_roster`, `sum_by_tag`, `tagged_bonuses`) are now **differentially confirmed on stdout + exit code** — each matches its verbosec-native reference exactly (stderr bytes not compared). The differential also SURFACED four pre-existing divergence families among rc-0 binaries, all byte-identical to main so none introduced by the slice, none yet fixed: (a) **bool outputs** — gen0 prints `0`/`1` and always exits 0 where verbosec prints `false`/`true` and exits 1 on false in several shapes (`invoices`, `clients`, `collections`, `logs`, `layers`, `bonus`, `priv_failure`, `alert`; for the reaction files `audit_log`/`audit_simple` the comparison is also apples-to-oranges — gen0 emits the reaction trampoline, silent when the trigger is false). **The FORMAT half is CLOSED 2026-08-08** — see the bool row in the gaps table; the "always exits 0" half is NOT, and the note below that table explains why it cannot be closed in gen0 alone (verbosec's own seven bool sites disagree, and which one runs is picked by a hint gen0 skips); (b) **record-output entries print the result's arena NODE INDEX at rc 0 instead of JSON** (`classify`, `greeting`) — the record sibling of the tier defect, a silent wrong answer, **now CLOSED** (2026-08-07): the diagnosis in this sentence was exactly right, `x86_json_record` was already there for collection elements and the entry trampoline simply never called it. See the record row in the gaps table; (c) **group-valued entries** print an arena index in both compilers with different allocation orders (`sum_chain`, `label_tree`, `print_chain` first rules — neither number is a value) — **now REFUSED by gen0** in the same slice as (b), because "verbosec is also wrong here" is not a licence to emit a wrong answer at rc 0; (d) **text FIELDS in streamed positions are itoa'd** — `x86_stream_node`'s AstField arm assumes Number, so `concat("Hello ", g.name, ...)` prints g.name's packed span as a decimal (`greeting_line`, `compose`) — the STREAM sibling of the tier Ok defect. Measured after (b): `greeting_line Ada 36` → gen0 `Hello 2287825359413968899, age 36` (`0x1fbffd0b_00000003`, fixed-region offset `0x1fbffd0b`, len 3) vs verbosec `Hello Ada, age 36`. **CLOSED 2026-08-08** — see the streamed-text row in the gaps table; the corpus does not move (100), because unlike (a)–(c) this one had no representation to refuse.

**THE ITOA-A-POINTER FAMILY WAS DECLARED CLOSED HERE ON 2026-08-08, AND THAT WAS THE SECOND OF TWO OVER-CLAIMS. IT IS NOT CLOSED; IT IS TRACKED BY THE MATRIX BELOW.** *(First over-claim: this paragraph was headed "THE SILENT-WRONG-ANSWER FAMILY IS CLOSED", and that was wrong within the day — text `==` was a fourth silent wrong answer, of a different mechanism, sitting in plain sight in nine corpus files. Second over-claim: the corrected heading, "THE ITOA-A-POINTER FAMILY IS CLOSED", was wrong for two more days — `read()` in a direct output position and in a fold body, plus a bare text FIELD as a whole output, were three more members of exactly the family being declared empty, fixed 2026-08-10. Both headings are corrected in place rather than deleted, because the repetition is the lesson: each time, "closed" was asserted about the members the current sweep could SEE, and each time the sweep had a structural blind spot — first a defect correct on half its input space, then a position nobody probed because its COMPOSED form worked. **Never declare a category empty. Name the mechanism you closed, and enumerate the positions you actually tested** — which is why this section now ends in a matrix rather than a claim.)* All three members — a Result payload span (PR #151), a record entry's arena index (PR #152), a streamed text field's argv span (this slice) — were the same defect wearing different clothes: a POINTER-ISH value reaching a printer that only knew how to itoa, producing rc 0 and a plausible decimal. Two things are worth carrying forward. First, **every one of the three was fixed by WIRING, not by new machinery** — the serializer or the write sequence already existed in a sibling emitter (`x86_json_record` for the record entry, the span-aware Err block for the Result payload, `x86_fold_arg` for the streamed field) and simply was not reached from the position that needed it. Check for an existing emitter before writing one. Second, **the useful discipline was splitting at the REPRESENTATION boundary**: implement what the representation genuinely supports, refuse the rest at verify time with zero bytes, and scope the refusal to what the ENTRY rule REACHES rather than what the file contains. That is what kept `enrich`/`order_intake`-shaped dead concat from being refused, and it is why the corpus moved 110 → 105 → 100 and then stopped.

The differential was re-run over all 100 accepted binaries (collections included via count-prefixed argv), **with every declared resource materialised first** — without that both sides fail closed and the harness records a meaningless agreement. Before the bool-format slice: **84 agree** on stdout + exit code, **11 diverge**, **1 signal**, 4 skipped as unencodable. After the format half: 88/7/1/4. **After the exit-code half (2026-08-09): 91 agree, 4 diverge, 1 signal, 4 skipped** — the seven bool-rc divergences (`alert`, `bonus`, `clients`, `collections`, `layers`, `logs`, `priv_failure`) all resolved, and the remaining 4 are two pre-existing things:

- **2 are streamed-read spans** (`read_config`, `sep_roster`): `read(<resource>)` reaching a streamed position is still itoa'd as its packed (start, len) pair — `read_config` prints `2305840045686259713` where verbosec prints the file's contents. This is the last surviving member of the itoa-a-pointer family, in the one position the 2026-08-08 streamed-text slice did not cover (that slice fixed text FIELDS; a `read` is a BoundText, a different arm of the same walk). **BOTH FIXED 2026-08-10** — see the output-positions row in the gaps table. Post-fix the differential is **92 agree / 3 diverge / 1 signal / 4 skipped**, and the 3 remaining divergences are the 2 reaction apples-to-oranges comparisons plus `vexprparse` itself (whose `source : text [..4194304]` routes gen0 to stdin while verbosec defaults to argv — the channel artifact noted below, which this harness surfaces as a divergence rather than hiding it in `agree`).
- **2 are the apples-to-oranges reaction pair** (`audit_log`, `audit_simple`): gen0 emits the REACTION trampoline (which writes the log file and prints nothing) where `--run <bool rule>` makes verbosec emit the bool rule directly. Different programs, not a divergence. **HARNESS FIXED 2026-08-10** — `main.rs:520` accepts a REACTION name at `--run`, so asking verbosec for `--run <reaction name>` makes the two comparable, and comparing the APPENDED FILE (a reaction prints nothing, so its stdout agreement was vacuous) shows them byte-identical: `audit_log` writes `suspicious purchase: amount=5000 age=17\n` on both sides, `audit_simple` `suspicious purchase detected\n`. Both move to `agree`.

**Differential after the reaction-harness fix (2026-08-10): 94 agree / 1 diverge / 1 signal / 4 skipped**, over the same 100 accepted binaries, with declared resources materialised first and reaction files compared on their appended bytes rather than their (empty) stdout. The delta against the 92/3/1/4 above is entirely the 2 reaction files moving diverge→agree, and it is a HARNESS change, not a compiler change — nothing about how gen0 compiles a reaction moved in this slice. **The 1 remaining divergence is `vexprparse` itself**, the stdin-vs-argv channel artifact described below (its `source : text [..4194304]` routes gen0 to stdin while verbosec defaults to argv, so feeding the same argv to both compares different input channels); it is fed `/dev/null` on stdin so the run is deterministic and the artifact is *surfaced* rather than hidden in `agree` or masked as a timeout. **The 1 signal is still `retirement`** (the `AstMap`-over-record-elements `int3` placeholder — a collection-element marshalling hole). Of the 4 skips, three are long-standing (`callable_let_variant` / `expr_stmt`, group-typed entry input neither backend can marshal from argv; `recursive_text_eval`, which verbosec refuses natively); the fourth is `hello_http`, skipped *because it is a service* — services are covered by the effect-position matrix below, over real TCP, not by a stdout differential.
- (A third thing to know about, though it happens to land in `agree` under the current harness: `vexprparse`'s own entry `skip_spaces` declares `source : text [..4194304]`, so by the documented `entry_uses_stdin` rule gen0's binary reads STDIN while verbosec's default channel is argv. Feeding the same argv to both compares different input channels; agreement there is an artifact either way, not evidence.)

**THE TEXT-POSITION MATRIX (2026-08-10).** The itoa-a-pointer family was declared closed **twice** and was wrong twice, and both times for the same procedural reason: **one position was tested and the rest were assumed to follow.** The 2026-08-08 slice fixed text FIELDS in streamed positions and the note above concluded the family was closed; a `read()` in the *same* walk was still broken, in two positions. The reason nobody looked is worth stating plainly — **`concat("[", read(r), "]")` works, and always has.** Probing the composed form and generalising from it is the natural move and it is wrong: a concat is texty, so it routes to the streaming walk, which already owned a correct `span_is_read` arm. It is the DIRECT form, where the value IS the whole output, that never reaches that walk. So the family is no longer tracked by asserting a category is empty; it is tracked by a MATRIX of position × source, re-runnable, with every cell's verdict recorded. Each cell synthesizes a probe `.verbose`, compiles it with BOTH compilers, runs both, and compares stdout + exit code:

| position | literal | text field | `read()` | `substring` | `concat` (fresh) | variant binder ⁵ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| direct output (`o = X`) | ok | ok ¹ | ok ¹ | vc-refuses | ok | ok ⁶ |
| inside `concat(...)` | ok | ok | ok | **trap** ² | vc-refuses | ok ⁶ |
| `Result` Ok payload | ok | ok | ok | vc-refuses | gen0-refuses ³ | gen0-only ⁷ |
| `Result` Err payload | ok | ok | ok | vc-refuses | gen0-refuses ³ | gen0-only ⁷ |
| record field | ok | ok | ok | vc-refuses | gen0-refuses ³ | gen0-only ⁷ |
| fold init (Phase 5b) | ok | n/a ⁴ | ok ¹ | n/a ⁴ | ok | n/a ⁸ |
| fold body arg | ok | ok | ok ¹ | vc-refuses | **trap** ² | n/a ⁸ |

¹ the three cells this slice fixed — all three were a span printed as a decimal at rc 0.
² `int3` — **fail-loud**, not a silent wrong answer. A known feature hole (no streamed-in-loop lowering for that shape), acceptable under the standing "presence is not the defect, reachability is" rule.
³ PR #151's `entry_freshtext` verify-time refusal (zero bytes): fresh text has no packed-span representation, so it is refused rather than guessed.
⁴ not expressible — the fold-init position is in caller scope, where no element binder exists, and the probe's input concept has no text field of its own. Not a gap.
⁵ the column added 2026-08-16 (PR #171): a TEXT payload bound by a `match` arm on a `variants:` concept — `match classify(s): Ident(label) => …`. Read the paragraph below before the cells; this column is a lesson about the matrix, not only about the emitter.
⁶ **the two cells PR #171 fixed** — both were the itoa-a-pointer defect again, in the arena variant-binder position. Measured on a `token_label`-shaped probe: `label` as the whole arm body gave `2287823723031429125` and `concat("id:", label)` gave `id:2287823581297508357`, both decoding as `0x…_0000000N` with N == the exact byte length. Both are byte-exact against verbosec afterwards, at exactly +30 B (`36 - 6`) per site: 2062 → 2092 and 2107 → 2137, and `examples/token_label.verbose::label_of` 3027 → 3057.
⁷ **gen0 is MORE capable here, and there is therefore no oracle.** verbosec's native emitter refuses all three outright (`Result-context expression not yet supported in native: MatchVariant(...)`, and the `record-context` twin), while gen0 accepts and produces an answer that is correct by inspection and by the language's own contracts: `Ok(label)` → stdout `hello` rc 0, `Err(label)` → **stderr** `hello` rc 1, `Rec { name: label }` → `{"name":"hello"}` rc 0. All three are byte-identical across PR #171 (2199 / 2195 / 2478, same sha256 before and after) — they are value-lowered paths through the Result trampoline and the record serializer, and the defect was confined to the STREAMING walk, which is why the fix touched only the stream walks and these cells did not move. In the negative corpus's taxonomy these are GAPs, not inverses (verbosec refuses, gen0 accepts) and they are instances of the already-tracked `input_type_unsupported` row — "gen0 mirrors none of verbosec's emit-time refusal surface". No fixture is added for them here; that belongs to that row's own slice.
⁸ not expressible — a fold site has no arm binder in scope.

**Every cell is byte-exact against verbosec, a fail-loud trap, an explicit refusal, or — new in the ⁷ cells — a gen0-only answer with no reference to diff against. No cell returns a wrong answer at rc 0.**

**THE COLUMN SET WAS NEVER AUDITED, AND THAT IS THE THIRD TIME THIS FAMILY HAS BEEN OVER-CLAIMED (2026-08-16).** The two over-claims recorded above were both about POSITIONS — a cell nobody probed, in a matrix that had no row for it. This matrix was built to fix exactly that, and it enumerated its rows carefully. **Nobody audited its COLUMNS.** A variant binder is a source of text like a field or a `read()`, it had been emittable since Phase A slice 4.2, and it was simply not one of the five columns — so the sentence "every cell is now byte-exact" was true and told you nothing about it, while `examples/token_label.verbose` sat in the corpus itoa-ing a pointer. The generalisable form: **a matrix is only as complete as BOTH of its axes, and the axis you built the matrix to fix is the one you will audit.** When you add a source of text to the language, add a column here and measure every cell — the two that were broken here were found by enumerating the column, not by any sweep.

Worth noting what the enumeration bought beyond the two fixes: it also established that the three value-lowered cells were *already* correct, which is what confines the defect to the streaming walk and justifies the fix's scope. A narrower probe ("does token_label work now?") would have fixed the same two cells and left the scope claim unevidenced.

**THE EFFECT-POSITION MATRIX (2026-08-10).** The three positions the table above listed as *"untested, not clean"* — reaction `append_file` content, service response body, service `log:` content — are no longer stdout-shaped, so they are measured by a different method and get their own matrix. **Method, per cell:** synthesize a probe `.verbose`, compile it with BOTH compilers (verbosec via `--run <reaction name>` for a reaction, `compile_service` for a service), then *exercise the effect* — run the reaction binary and diff the APPENDED FILE byte-for-byte, or spawn the server, hold a real TCP conversation and diff the WIRE bytes per request AND the log file. Every service conversation is **good / MALFORMED / good**, which is not decoration: the wire bytes agreed on all shapes before this slice and only the log file diverged, so a well-formed-only conversation cannot see the class. Cells are pinned by `two_generation_gen0_service_and_reaction_output_positions`.

| position | literal | number field | text field | req.method / req.path | `read()` | `resp.status` / `req.timestamp` / `resp.body` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| reaction `append_file` content | byte-exact | byte-exact | gen0-refuses ¹ | n/a | vc-refuses ² | n/a |
| service response body | byte-exact | n/a | n/a | byte-exact (bare and in `concat`) | gen0-refuses ³ | n/a |
| service `log:` content | **byte-exact** ⁴ | n/a | n/a | **byte-exact** ⁴ (bare ⁵, and in `concat`) | vc-refuses ² | gen0-refuses ³ |

¹ `rx_carg_ok` accepts `AstStr` and a NUMBER field only, in deliberate arm-for-arm acceptance parity with `x86_rx_carg` — verbosec emits it (Phase 1B, runtime strlen), gen0 refuses with zero bytes. The safe direction, and the reason a text field never reached an unguarded itoa here.
² verbosec's own native emitter refuses (`append_file content must be a text literal, concat(...), or text-returning rule call` / `concat argument type not yet supported`); gen0 refuses too. No divergence.
³ verbosec emits, gen0 refuses with zero bytes — a slice gap, again the safe direction.
⁴ the cell this slice FIXED, for both content shapes at once: see the `log:` row of the gaps table. Before the fix a static-content log wrote a line for a connection the server had dropped.
⁵ **one accept-what-verbosec-refuses cell**, and it is on gen0's side of the ledger: a BARE `req.path` as log content is accepted by verbosec's *verifier* and refused by its *native emitter* (`append_file content must be a text literal, concat(...), or text-returning rule call; got Field(Ident("req"), "path")`), while gen0 accepts it and emits a correct 995-byte binary. That is a live verify/emit gap on verbosec's side, not a gen0 defect — the same class the `svc_log_field_ok` banner in `examples/vexprparse.verbose` already documents for a handler param not named `req`. It has no reference to diff against, so the cell is verified only against gen0's own bytes.

**Not covered, and not claimed:** the S1 (constant-response) handler shape with a `log:` block still logs per accepted connection because it never parses — asserted as-is in the pin, and explained in the gaps-table row. Reaction content via a text-returning rule call (Phase 2H-a) was not probed. Service positions were exercised over HTTP/1.0 only; `raw_tcp` is refused by gen0's verifier and untested here.

**No silent wrong answer remains in the sweep** — *and that sentence was true only of what the sweep could reach. Twice over, now: the text-`==` defect was live in nine of these same binaries and scored as agreement (see the caveat below and the text-`==` row in the gaps table), and the service `log:` placement defect was invisible to every stdout differential ever run, because the wrong output was a FILE. The effect-position matrix above exists because "the sweep is clean" only ever means "clean on the channels the sweep reads".* The 1 signal is still `retirement` (the `AstMap`-over-record-elements int3 placeholder — a collection-element marshalling hole, not a text-path miss), and `recursive_text_eval` is still the one accept-what-verbosec-refuses gap: verbosec refuses natively with `native input field 'node' has unsupported type (only number/text today)` while gen0 emits 2789 B that at least fails closed (rc 1, zero output). Both are unchanged and both remain open.

**One caveat the sweep's own numbers hide, and it is the more interesting finding.** `access_check` and `allowlist` sit in the `agree` bucket for a REASON THAT DOES NOT EXERCISE THE RULE: both `read(<resource>)` a file the sweep never creates, so both binaries fail closed (rc 1, empty stdout) and trivially match. Create the files and they diverge — **gen0 evaluates `<text field> == read(<resource>)` as FALSE unconditionally**. Measured with `/tmp/verbose_allowed_tag.txt` containing `ab`: `allowlist ab` → verbosec `true`, gen0 `false`; `allowlist zz` → both `false`. Same on `access_check` for `1 ab`, `2 ab ab` (verbosec `true`, gen0 `false`) and `2 ab zz` (both `false`). This is **pre-existing** — the pre-slice binaries printed `0` for every input, i.e. the same wrong boolean, just rendered as an integer — so the bool slice did not introduce it and does not fix it; it only made the wrong answer legible. It is a genuine silent-wrong-answer family that survived the "family is closed" sweep because a differential run without resource files present cannot see it. **Any future differential over gen0 must materialise declared resources before drawing conclusions.**

**CLOSED 2026-08-08, and the caveat above under-stated it by an order of magnitude.** The narrow symptom ("`<text field> == read(<resource>)` is false") was not a `read()` bug at all — `read()` works, and Number `==` works. The defect was **all text equality**: literal RHS, `read()` RHS, either operand order, `!=` too. Nine corpus files carried it, not two. The reason the caveat found only the two `read()` ones is the same reason the whole thing hid for so long — the other seven compare against a *literal*, the sweep feeds placeholder text, and a stuck-`false` equality is RIGHT on every non-matching input. See the text-`==` row in the gaps table for the root cause and the fix, and the paragraph after it for the two harness changes (both-arms inputs, and fixtures) that make this class visible. Post-fix differential over the 100, with resource fixtures created: **84 agree / 11 diverge / 1 signal / 4 skipped**, up from 81/14/1/4 on the same harness before the fix — the three that moved are `access_check`, `allowlist` and `sum_by_tag`, the only text-`==` files whose divergence a placeholder-input sweep could see. The other six were verified separately with hand-built matching inputs: 9/9 wrong before, 18/18 right after (both arms each).

**Three of the four never-run files are now run (2026-08-07), and one of them was a gap.** `hello_http` — the only accepted SERVICE program — was started and spoken to over TCP: gen0's binary answers `GET / HTTP/1.0` with bytes IDENTICAL to verbosec's 429-byte service binary (`HTTP/1.0 200 OK\r\nContent-Length: 29\r\n\r\nHello from Verbose over HTTP!`). `expr_stmt::eval_expr` and `callable_let_variant::depth` agree with verbosec EXACTLY, including the failure: both take a `concept_group`-typed input that argv cannot encode, and BOTH compilers emit a binary that SIGSEGVs on `depth 0` (rc 139 each) or prints `0` (`expr_stmt`, both rc 0). That is a shared verbosec/gen0 hole — a group-typed entry input has no argv marshalling in either backend — not a gen0 divergence, and it is the reason these files were never in a sweep. `recursive_text_eval` is the one real finding: verbosec REFUSES it natively (`native input field 'node' has unsupported type (only number/text today)`) while gen0 ACCEPTS and emits a 2789-byte binary — another accept-what-verbosec-refuses gap, though the binary at least fails closed (rc 1, zero output) rather than lying.

**A stale comment cost more than the feature did (2026-08-06, corpus 80 → 113).** 33 corpus files — essentially the whole crypto arc: `aes_*`, `sha256_*`, `ghash*`, `hmac_sha256`, `hkdf_*`, `x25519`, `p256_*`, `ed_*`, `sc_*` — were blocked on a **multi-line `if / then / else`**, the layout every one of them uses. Three banners in `examples/vexprparse.verbose` and the `Status:` line of `docs/indent-dedent-design.md` all said the blocker was that *"there is no INDENT/DEDENT in this tokenizer"*. That was false, and had been since brick 8b: `line_width`, `tokenize_indent`, a real `ColStack`, and `tok_kind` emitting `Newline = 600` / `Indent = 700` / `Dedent = 800` were all already there, along with `skip_seps` and `skip_seps_dedent`. The actual blocker was that the if-chain (`parse_primary`'s `isif` branch → `if_then` → `if_else`) peeked for `then` (207) and `else` (208) at the **raw** cursor: with a separator sitting there the peek saw 600/700, both rules took their tolerate-the-absence arm, and `parse_or` — handed a separator — returned `AstErr` consuming nothing, collapsing the parse. Routing those three peek positions through the already-existing `skip_seps` fixed it in six lines. The stale sentence had the slice scoped as a column-stack project; **the comment was the expensive artifact, not the code.** Zero files were lost and 79 of the 80 previously-accepted binaries are byte-identical (the 80th is `vexprparse` itself, whose source the six edits changed).

Two constraints on that fix are load-bearing and worth not re-litigating. First, separators are transparent **only** in the three if-keyword positions — this is deliberately *not* a general line-continuation rule, because verbosec REJECTS a newline inside a binary expression (`out = n.v +` ⏎ `1`), and making separators transparent everywhere would have gen0 accepting programs verbosec refuses, i.e. a new row in the gaps table above, in the wrong direction. Second, gen0's `skip_seps` has **no `depth` counter** where verbosec's `skip_if_separators` does, so it eats an Indent without ever pairing the matching Dedent. Measured benign across all 151 files (`parse_stmts` re-skips at every statement head; `skip_seps_dedent` absorbs strays at item level), but it costs one real layout: an Indent *between* `if` and its condition puts a Dedent immediately before `then`, which gen0 cannot cross — verbosec parses that, gen0 refuses it. Refusing what verbosec accepts is the safe direction, so the slice stops there rather than reach for `skip_seps_dedent` (which would swallow block-closing Dedents and re-open PR #141's silent-truncation hole). The remedy, when a real program needs it, is a `(cell, depth)` return from `skip_seps` threaded through `if_then → if_else → if_build`. All of this is pinned by `self_hosted_parses_multi_line_if_then_else` (`src/native.rs`), including the refusal — so the day the depth counter lands, that assertion flips and tells you to move the layout into the accepted set.

## Transpilation Strategy (rejected direction)

Rust/Go/other source → Verbose transpilation is **rejected** for the same reason as LLVM: the source does not contain Verbose's declarations (reads, overflow bounds, termination bound, intention). Any transpiler must either emit trivial proofs (losing all verification value and all hint-driven optimizations) or infer them (violating the zero-trust rule that proofs are declared, never guessed).

The healthier answers to "don't isolate from existing ecosystems" are:
1. **Binary interop** — Verbose emits ELF; other languages link via FFI.
2. **Assisted generation** — tooling that suggests a Verbose equivalent from foreign source, with proof slots filled by a human or AI (not by the compiler).
3. **Manual module bindings** — external functions imported through an explicit Verbose declaration stating the proofs on our side.

Rule: **if the proof is not declared, it does not exist**. No pipeline fabricates proofs.

Full rationale: README.md → "Why Not Transpile Rust/Go → Verbose?".

## Development Rules

- **Always explain what you're doing and why.** The creators are learning alongside the AI. Every change must be explained clearly.
- **No silent changes.** Explain what changed, why, and what impact it has.
- **Explain concepts when they arise.** Don't assume knowledge of compiler theory or Rust internals.
- **Zero external dependencies** — everything is hand-written.
- **Zero-trust verification** — the compiler verifies AI proofs, never trusts them.
- **All tests must pass** before any commit — and the suite MUST be run serially: `cargo test -- --test-threads=1`. Bare `cargo test` is **flaky by construction**, not occasionally: many tests write a fresh binary to a temp path and then `spawn` it, and in a multithreaded harness another thread's fork inherits the write descriptor, so the exec races into `ETXTBSY` ("Text file busy"). Measured on unmodified `628a820`: **12 failures in parallel, 520/520 serial**, and the failing SET differs run to run — which is exactly what makes it dangerous, because it reads like a regression in whatever you just changed. It is not a path-collision problem and must not be "fixed" by renaming temp files (419 of the 508 `temp_dir().join(...)` sites are already distinct). CI is already correct — `--test-threads=1` at `.github/workflows/ci.yml:37`, `:99` and `release.yml:29` — so no PR has ever been judged on a parallel run; the hazard is purely local.
- **Closed attributes** — unknown `@attributes` are rejected, not silently ignored.
- **No false explicitation** — every declaration must be mechanically verified or exploited. If it's just decoration, remove it.
- **A hint must never change observable behaviour.** `vectorizable`, `parallel`, `cache_result` may change speed, code size, syscall count, instruction selection — never stdout, never the exit code, never which inputs are accepted. A hint selects an *implementation*; a declaration selects a *meaning*. This rule was unwritten until 2026-08-09, and that is exactly how it got broken: `vectorizable` routed a rule to `emit_vectorized_program`, which exited 0 on a false result while the unhinted path exited 1, so adding a *performance* annotation silently changed what `if ./check "$x"` did. The concrete instance is documented under the gen0 gaps table; the general rule belongs here. When adding or extending a hint, the acceptance test is a differential of the hinted and unhinted builds over the same inputs — `optimization_hints_do_not_change_observable_behaviour` in `src/native.rs` is the worked example, and it deliberately asserts that the two builds ARE different code (SIMD bytes present in one, absent in the other) so it cannot pass vacuously.
- **The emitter must be reproducible.** The same `.verbose` input compiles to byte-identical output, every run, in every backend. An auditor verifying a deployed binary against a rebuild has to get a match, or the whole "read the binary, it's 700 bytes" pitch collapses. The practical rule: **never let the iteration order of a `HashMap`/`HashSet` reach emitted bytes or layout** — Rust's default hasher is randomly seeded, so `for x in &set`, `.iter()`, `.keys()`, `.values()`, `.intersection()` and friends are all per-process-random. Collect into a `Vec` and `sort()` first, or index a declaration-order `Vec` and use the map only for `.get()` / `.contains()`. Watch for the subtle form too: a *stable* `sort_by_key` leaves tied entries in hash order, so the key must be unique (this is exactly how the WASM data section stayed nondeterministic behind a sort that looked correct). Diagnostics-only ordering is tolerated today. Pinned by `scc_callable_order_is_deterministic_across_compiles` in `src/native.rs`; the standing check is a baseline-vs-baseline corpus sweep, which must come back empty.
- **Read the reference implementation before believing a summary of it.** A "known gap" note is a claim about cost, and it decays. Four times in the gen0 verification arc a gap sat deferred as an arc because a table row said so, and each time the reference turned out to be a plain walk: `hint_overflow_inverted` was described as needing interval arithmetic and needed two integer literals and a `>`; attribute PRESENCE was called "structurally out of reach" and was a line walk; `termination.bound` was filed under "the same missing machinery as the `overflow` row" while `count_operations` is a 33-arm structural node count with no arithmetic in it at all; and `@layer` STRATIFICATION was said to "need the call graph, not the source text" while `check_layer_discipline` is a direct-call name lookup with no traversal in it. The cost of opening the function is seconds; the cost of trusting the row was a slice each time. When you write such a note yourself, name the mechanism the check would need — not the neighbour it superficially resembles, because a row that groups a cheap check with an expensive one prices both at the expensive one. **The layer row adds a second failure mode to watch for: naming the wrong DATA STRUCTURE.** It asserted that the check needed attributes retained through the parse, which was true of the *shape* it imagined and false of the one that works — a parsed declaration already carries its own name's byte offset, which is all a source-line scan needs to find the attribute block that belongs to it.
- **The native backend is the destination.** It was once phrased as "the Rust transpiler is a fallback"; that transpiler is gone (2026-08-19), so the rule is simply that architectural decisions keep the direct-emission path primary. The portable answers are WASM (hosted) and, if a non-x86-64 native target is ever needed, a new emitter argued on its own merits — not a source-to-source escape hatch.
- **Every feature must serve security, performance, or unique machine code.** No ergonomic sugar without optimization value.
- **All documentation in English.** The repo is international. **One scoped exception: `docs/learn/`.** Those are pedagogical articles (cross-posted to [arcker.org](https://arcker.org), the canonical home) written in French, pinned to a stated language version per article. They are blog content that happens to live in the repo, not compiler reference docs — they do not track the current codebase and are exempt from the English rule and the "keep up to date" expectation. Everything else under `docs/` (design notes, effect model, native designs, etc.) stays English and current.
- **Pop sub-agents for exploration-heavy work.** When mapping a refactor across many files, scanning for every touch point of a construct, or investigating a broad "where is X used" question, delegate to a sub-agent (`Agent` tool, `Explore` type for searches, `general-purpose` for mixed read+reasoning). The agent's summary lands in the main context; the raw file reads do not. Reserve the main context for actual edits and conversation with the human. Sub-agents are not a substitute for judgment — always read the code you're about to modify yourself, and verify the agent's claims against the file before acting on them.

## Design Lessons

R&D journal of hard-won insights — documented scars from building a language that doesn't exist yet. Read before proposing large changes: [docs/design-lessons.md](docs/design-lessons.md).


## Running

```bash
cargo run -- examples/collections.verbose                                           # verify
cargo run -- examples/collections.verbose --run client_blocked --input examples/collections.json  # interpret
cargo run -- examples/report.verbose --run total_revenue --input examples/report.json --json  # JSON output
cargo run -- examples/business.verbose --native /tmp/biz --run critical_invoice     # native x86-64
cargo run -- examples/invoices.verbose --native /tmp/inv --run important_invoice --stdin  # native, reads stdin
echo "15000" | /tmp/inv                                                            # → true
cargo run -- examples/alert.verbose --native /tmp/alert --run should_alert --stream  # streaming
printf "3 auth\n1 web\n" | /tmp/alert                                              # → true\nfalse
cargo run -- examples/invoices.verbose --wasm /tmp/rule.wasm --run important_invoice # WASM
cargo run -- examples/invoices.verbose --benchmark --run important_invoice          # compare all backends
cargo run -- --demo-http /tmp/server                                                 # HTTP server — tier-3 emitter probe, NOT in .verbose (see docs/known-gaps.md)
cargo test -- --test-threads=1                                                      # 582 tests — SERIAL IS REQUIRED
make demo                                                                           # full demo
```
